奉佩里翁之命，德米特里奥斯得了一柄寻常的剑，而佩里翁自己卸去了全身护身的甲胄。两人相交，如两场风暴迎面相撞；到头来，德米特里奥斯负伤倒地，人事不省。

德米特里奥斯沦为俘虏，被押着解往泰奥多雷特国王的疆土。

===Original===

“Only you are my private capture,” said Perion; “conquered by my own hand and in fair fight. Now I am unwilling to insult the most valiant warrior whom I have known by valuing him too cheaply, and I accordingly fix your ransom as the person of Dame Melicent.”

Demetrios bit his nails.

“Needs must,” he said at last. “It is unnecessary to inform you that when my property is taken from me I shall endeavour to regain it. I shall, before the year is out, lay waste whatever kingdom it is that harbours you. Meanwhile I warn you it is necessary to be speedy in this ransoming. My other wives abhor the Frankish woman who has supplanted them in my esteem. My son Orestes, who succeeds me, will be guided by his mother. Callistion has thrice endeavoured to kill Melicent. If any harm befalls me, Callistion to all intent will reign in Nacumera, and she will not be satisfied with mere assassination. I cannot guess what torment Callistion will devise, but it will be no child’s play﻿—”

“Hah, infamy!” cried Perion. He had learned long ago how cunning the heathen were in such cruelties, and so he shuddered.

===Chinese===

「唯独你，是我私人俘获的，」佩里翁说道，「由我亲手战胜，于一场公平的搏战中赢来。我不愿把平生所知最骁勇的战士估得太贱，以致辱没了他，故而我把你的赎金定为梅莉森特夫人本人。」

德米特里奥斯咬起了指甲。

「事出无奈，也只得如此。」他终于开口。「不必我提醒你也知道：我的财物一旦被人夺去，我必设法夺回。不出今年，无论哪个王国收容了你，我都要将它夷为废墟。同时我警告你：这桩赎身之事，务必从速。我其余的那些妻子，个个憎恶那个法兰克女子，因为她把她们从我恩宠中的位置篡夺了去。我身后由我儿子俄瑞斯忒斯（Orestes）继位，而他必受母亲摆布。卡利斯提翁（Callistion）已三番下手要害梅莉森特的性命。倘若我遭了不测，卡利斯提翁实际上就将在纳库梅拉（Nacumera）称尊，况且她绝不会满足于单单的暗杀。我猜不透卡利斯提翁会设计出怎样的折磨，但那必不会是儿戏——」

「哈，可耻！」佩里翁喊道。他早就领教过，异教徒在这类酷行上是何等巧诈，因此不由得打了个寒噤。

===Original===

Demetrios was silent. He, too, was frightened, because this despot knew﻿—and none knew better﻿—that in his lordly house far oversea Callistion would find equipment for a hundred curious tortures.

“It has been difficult for me to tell you this,” Demetrios then said, “because it savours of an appeal to spare me. I think you will have gleaned, however, from our former encounters, that I am not unreasonably afraid of death. Also I think that you love Melicent. For the rest, there is no person in Nacumera so untutored as to cross my least desire until my death is triply proven. Accordingly, I who am Demetrios am willing to entreat an oath that you will not permit Theodoret to kill me.”

“I swear by God and all the laws of Rome﻿—” cried Perion.

“Ey, but I am not very popular in Rome,” Demetrios interrupted. “I would prefer that you swore by your love for Melicent. I would prefer an oath which both of us may understand, and I know of none other.”

So Perion swore as Demetrios requested, and set about the conveyance of Demetrios into King Theodoret’s realm.

===Chinese===

德米特里奥斯默然不语。他心里也是害怕的，因为这位专制君王深知——无人比他知之更深——在他海外那座华贵的府邸里，卡利斯提翁将找得到施行上百种精奇酷刑的诸般家什。

「这些话我难以对你出口，」德米特里奥斯接着说，「因为它带着乞求饶命的意味。不过我想，从我们先前的几次交手，你早已看出我对死亡的畏惧从非无端。我也想，你是爱梅莉森特的。至于其余：在我的死讯经三重验证之前，纳库梅拉没有一个人不识时务到敢拂逆我最细微的心愿。因此，我——德米特里奥斯其人——甘愿向你乞取一个誓言：你不得容许泰奥多雷特杀我。」

「我凭上帝和罗马的一切法律起誓——」佩里翁喊道。

「欸，可我在罗马并不怎么得人心，」德米特里奥斯打断了他。「我更愿你凭你对梅莉森特的爱起誓。我宁愿要一个你我二人都领会的誓言，除此之外，我想不出还有别的。」

于是佩里翁依德米特里奥斯所求立了誓，随即着手把德米特里奥斯解往泰奥多雷特国王的国度。

## X How Demetrios Wooed / 十 德米特里奥斯如何求欢

===Original===

Ahasuerus came and went at will. Nothing was known concerning this soft-treading furtive man except by the proconsul, who had no confidants. By his decree Ahasuerus was an honoured guest at Nacumera. And always the Jew’s eyes when Melicent was near him were as expressionless as the eyes of a snake, which do not ever change.

Once she told Demetrios that she feared Ahasuerus.

“But I do not fear him, Melicent, though I have larger reason. For I alone of all men living know the truth concerning this same Jew. Therefore, it amuses me to think that he, who served my wizard father in a very different fashion, is today my factor and ciphers over my accounts.”

Demetrios laughed, and had the Jew summoned.

This was in the Women’s Garden, where the proconsul sat with Melicent in a little domed pavilion of stonework which was gilded with red gold and crowned with a cupola of alabaster. Its pavement was of transparent glass, under which were clear running waters wherein swam red and yellow fish.

===Chinese===

亚哈随鲁来去随心。关于这个足音轻软、行迹诡秘的人，除了总督无人知晓底细，而总督又素来没有可以交心的人。凭着他的一道教令，亚哈随鲁成了纳库梅拉备受礼敬的宾客。而每当梅莉森特走到近旁，这犹太人的一双眼睛总如蛇眼一般毫无表情——蛇眼是从来不变的。

有一回，她对德米特里奥斯说，她怕亚哈随鲁。

「可我并不怕他，梅莉森特，尽管我该怕的理由比你更充足。因为普天之下活着的人里，唯有我知晓这个犹太人的真相。所以想到此人——他曾以一种截然不同的方式事奉我那术士父亲——如今却做了我的代理人，伏案替我核算账目，我便觉得有趣。」

德米特里奥斯大笑，命人把犹太人召来。

这是在女眷园中。总督与梅莉森特同坐在一座小小的石砌穹顶凉亭里，亭身镀着红金，顶上冠着一顶雪花石膏的圆盖。亭中地面铺着透明的玻璃，玻璃之下清水流转，红的黄的鱼儿游弋其间。

===Original===

Demetrios said:

“It appears that you are a formidable person, Ahasuerus. My wife here fears you.”

“Splendour of the Age,” returned Ahasuerus, quietly, “it is notorious that women have long hair and short wits. There is no need to fear a Jew. The Jew, I take it, was created in order that children might evince their playfulness by stoning him, the honest show their commonsense by robbing him, and the religious display their piety by burning him. Who forbids it?”

“Ey, but my wife is a Christian and in consequence worships a Jew.” Demetrios reflected. His dark eyes twinkled. “What is your opinion concerning this other Jew, Ahasuerus?”

“I know that He was the Messiah, Lord.”

===Chinese===

德米特里奥斯说：

「看来你是个了不得的人物，亚哈随鲁。我这妻子便怕你。」

「时代的光耀（Splendour of the Age），」亚哈随鲁平平静静地答道，「女人头发长、见识短，这是尽人皆知的。用不着怕一个犹太人。依我看来，犹太人被造出来，就是为了让孩子拿石头砸他、以显其顽皮，让老实人劫夺他、以显其常识，让虔诚人烧死他、以显其虔敬。有谁禁止么？」

「唉，可我的妻子是个基督徒，因此她敬拜的正是一个犹太人。」德米特里奥斯沉吟道。他黑色的眼睛闪闪发亮。「那么，对于这另一位犹太人，你有何高见，亚哈随鲁？」

「我知道祂就是弥赛亚（Messiah），主上。」

===Original===

“And yet you do not worship Him.”

The Jew said:

“It was not altogether worship He desired. He asked that men should love Him. He does not ask love of me.”

“I find that an obscure saying,” Demetrios considered.

“It is a true saying, King of Kings. In time it will be made plain. That time is not yet come. I used to pray it would come soon. Now I do not pray any longer. I only wait.”

===Chinese===

「可你并不敬拜祂。」

犹太人说：

「祂要的并不全是敬拜。祂求的是世人爱祂。祂没有向我求爱。」

「我觉得这话玄奥难解。」德米特里奥斯思忖道。

「这是一句真话，万王之王。时候一到，它自会显明。那时刻还没有到来。我从前常祈祷它快些降临。如今我不再祈祷了。我只是等待。」

===Original===

Demetrios tugged at his chin, his eyes narrowed, meditating. He laughed.

Demetrios said:

“It is no affair of mine. What am I that I am called upon to have prejudices concerning the universe? It is highly probable there are gods of some sort or another, but I do not so far flatter myself as to consider that any possible god would be at all interested in my opinion of him. In any event, I am Demetrios. Let the worst come, and in whatever baleful underworld I find myself imprisoned I shall maintain myself there in a manner not unworthy of Demetrios.” The proconsul shrugged at this point. “I do not find you amusing, Ahasuerus. You may go.”

“I hear, and I obey,” the Jew replied. He went away patiently.

Then Demetrios turned toward Melicent, rejoicing that his chattel had golden hair and was comely beyond comparison with all other women he had ever seen.

===Chinese===

德米特里奥斯捻着下巴，眯起眼睛，沉吟不语。随后他笑了。

德米特里奥斯说：

「这与我毫不相干。我算什么人，竟该对宇宙怀抱成见？多半是存在着这一种或那一种神灵的，但我还不至于自抬身价到这般地步，以为哪一位可能存在的神，会有一丝一毫的兴趣听取我对他的看法。无论如何，我是德米特里奥斯。就让最坏的降临吧；无论我被囚禁在哪一处凶冥地府，我都要在那里撑持出一个不辱德米特里奥斯之名的局面。」说到此处，总督耸了耸肩。「我觉得你不有趣，亚哈随鲁。你可以走了。」

「我听见了，我遵命。」犹太人答道。他耐着性子走了出去。

于是德米特里奥斯转向梅莉森特，心中欢喜，只因他的这件动产生着一头金发，容颜之美，远非他平生所见的任何别的女子所能比拟。

===Original===

Said Demetrios:

“I love you, Melicent, and you do not love me. Do not be offended because my speech is harsh, for even though I know my candour is distasteful I must speak the truth. You have been obdurate too long, denying Kypris what is due to her. I think that your brain is giddy because of too much exulting in the magnificence of your body and in the number of men who have desired it to their own hurt. I concede your beauty, yet what will it matter a hundred years from now?

“I admit that my refrain is old. But it will presently take on a more poignant meaning, because a hundred years from now you﻿—even you, dear Melicent!﻿—and all the loveliness which now causes me to estimate life as a light matter in comparison with your love, will be only a bone or two. Your lustrous eyes, which are now more beautiful than it is possible to express, will be unsavoury holes and a worm will crawl through them; and what will it matter a hundred years from now?

“A hundred years from now should anyone break open our gilded tomb, he will find Melicent to be no more admirable than Demetrios. One skull is like another, and is as lightly split with a mattock. You will be as ugly as I, and nobody will be thinking of your eyes and hair. Hail, rain and dew will drench us both impartially when I lie at your side, as I intend to do, for a hundred years and yet another hundred years. You need not frown, for what will it matter a hundred years from now?

“Melicent, I offer love and a life that derides the folly of all other manners of living; and even if you deny me, what will it matter a hundred years from now?”

===Chinese===

德米特里奥斯说道：

「我爱你，梅莉森特，而你不爱我。不要因我言语粗厉而见怪，因为尽管我知道自己的坦率不堪入耳，我还是必须说实话。你冥顽得太久了，迟迟不肯偿付爱神库普里斯（Kypris）应得之债。我想，你的头脑之所以发昏，是因为你太沉迷于自己肉身的美妙，也太得意于那么多男子为你神魂颠倒、自招祸殃。你的美貌我承认；可是一百年之后，这又有什么相干？

「我承认，我这个老调子早已唱旧。但它立刻就要生出更痛切的意味，因为一百年之后，你——连你，亲爱的梅莉森特！——连同如今这一切可爱之处（正是它使我觉得，与你的爱情一比，生命不过是桩轻事），都只会剩下一两根骨头。你那双明亮的眼眸，如今美得言语无法形容，届时将成为两个令人作呕的窟窿，有一条蛆虫从中爬过；而一百年之后，这又有什么相干？

「一百年之后，倘若有谁砸开我们这座包金的坟墓，他会发现梅莉森特并不比德米特里奥斯更可赞叹。一颗颅骨与另一颗颅骨毫无二致，鹤嘴锄劈起来同样轻巧。你将和我一样丑陋，再没有人会惦念你的眼睛和头发。等我依着自己的打算躺到你身旁，一百年，又一百年，冰雹、雨水和露水将不分彼此地把你我一同浇透。你不必皱眉，因为一百年之后，这又有什么相干？

「梅莉森特，我献上的是爱情，是一种足以嘲笑其余一切活法之愚蠢的人生；即便你拒绝我，一百年之后，这又有什么相干？」

===Original===

His face was contorted, his speech had fervent bitterness, for even while he wooed this woman the man internally was raging over his own infatuation.

And Melicent answered:

“There can be no question of love between us, seignior. You purchased my body. My body is at your disposal under God’s will.”

Demetrios sneered, his ardours cooled. He said, “I have already told you, my girl, I do not care for that which can be purchased.”

===Chinese===

他的脸扭曲了，言语间带着炽烈的苦涩，因为即使在这向女子求欢的时刻，这男人心底里仍在为自己的痴恋狂怒不已。

梅莉森特答道：

「你我之间谈不上爱情，大人。你买下的是我的肉身。凭上帝的旨意，我的肉身任凭你处置。」

德米特里奥斯冷笑一声，热望冷却下来。他说：「我早已告诉过你，我的姑娘，能花钱买到的东西，我不稀罕。」

---

===Original===

In such fashion Melicent abode among these odious persons as a lily which is rooted in mire. She was a prisoner always, and when Demetrios came to Nacumera﻿—which fell about irregularly, for now arose much fighting between the Christians and the pagans﻿—a gem which he uncased, admired, curtly exulted in, and then, jeering at those hot wishes in his heart, locked up untouched when he went back to warfare.

To her the man was uniformly kind, if with a sort of sneer she could not understand; and he pillaged an infinity of Genoese and Venetian ships﻿—which were notoriously the richest laden﻿—of jewels, veils, silks, furs, embroideries and figured stuffs, wherewith to enhance the comeliness of Melicent. It seemed an all-engulfing madness with this despot daily to aggravate his fierce desire of her, to nurture his obsession, so that he might glory in the consciousness of treading down no puny adversary.

Pride spurred him on as witches ride their dupes to a foreknown destruction. “Let us have patience,” he would say.

Meanwhile his other wives peered from screened alcoves at these two and duly hated Melicent. “Let us have patience!” they said, also, but with a meaning that was more sinister.

===Chinese===

就这样，梅莉森特栖身于这些可憎的人中间，如同一株扎根在污泥里的百合。她始终是个囚徒；而每当德米特里奥斯来到纳库梅拉——这样的时节来去无定，因为眼下基督徒与异教徒之间正多刀兵——她便是一件宝石：他开匣取出，赞叹一番，草草自鸣得意，然后讥笑着自己心头那些炽热的欲念，待他重返沙场时，又原封不动地锁回匣中。

这男人待她始终和善，只是带着一种她无法理解的冷笑；他劫掠了数不清的热那亚（Genoese）船和威尼斯（Venetian）船——那是公认装载最富的——夺来珠宝、面纱、绸缎、皮裘、刺绣和织花的缎帛，用来增益梅莉森特的美貌。这位暴君仿佛陷在一种吞噬一切的疯狂里，日日加深自己对她的烈欲，豢养自己的痴念，好使他得以自豪地意识到：自己所践踏的，绝非什么孱弱的对手。

骄傲驱策着他前行，正如女巫驱策着她们的傀儡，奔向早已注定的毁灭。「我们且忍耐。」他常这样说。

与此同时，他的其余妻妾从遮着屏格的内室里窥望这两个人，恰如其分地恨着梅莉森特。「我们且忍耐！」她们也这样说，只是话里的意思更为阴毒。

## XIX How Flamberge Was Lost / 十九 弗朗贝尔热如何失去

===Original===

These hunted men spent the following night upon the Needle, since there it was not possible for an adversary to surprise them. Perion’s was the earlier watch, until midnight, and during this time Demetrios slept. Then the proconsul took his equitable turn. When Perion awakened the hour was after dawn.

What Perion noted first, and within thirty feet of him, was a tall galley with blue and yellow sails. He perceived that the promontory was thronged with heathen sailors, who were unlading the ship of various bales and chests. Demetrios, now in the costume of his native country, stood among them giving orders. And it seemed, too, to Perion, in the moment of waking, that Dame Mélusine, whom Perion had loved so long ago, also stood among them; yet, now that Perion rose and faced Demetrios, she was not visible anywhere, and Perion wondered dimly over his wild dream that she had been there at all. But more importunate matters were in hand.

The proconsul grinned malevolently.

“This is a ship that once was mine,” he said. “Do you not find it droll that Euthyclos here should have loved me sufficiently to hazard his life in order to come in search of me? Personally, I consider it preposterous. For the rest, you slept so soundly, Messire de la Forêt, that I was unwilling to waken you. Then, too, such was the advice of a person who has some influence with the waterfolk, people say, and who was perhaps the means of bringing this ship hither so opportunely. I do not know. She is gone now, you see, intent as always on her own ends. Well, well! her ways are not our ways, and it is wiser not to meddle with them.”

===Chinese===

这两个被追猎的人在针岬上度过了接下来的一夜，因为在那个地方，敌人无从对他们发动突袭。头一班守更轮到佩里翁，守到午夜，这段时辰里德米特里奥斯沉睡着。随后总督接了他那公平合宜的一班。待佩里翁醒来，时辰已过了破晓。

佩里翁最先注意到的，是离他不过三十英尺处，一艘张着蓝黄两色船帆的高大桨帆船。他看出海岬上挤满了异教水手，正把大大小小的货包和箱笼从船上卸下。德米特里奥斯已换上他故国的衣装，立在他们中间发号施令。而在初醒的那一瞬，佩里翁还恍惚觉得，他许久以前爱过的梅吕西娜夫人（Dame Mélusine）也立在他们中间；可是待佩里翁起身、面对德米特里奥斯时，她已无处可寻，佩里翁便隐约纳罕，自己竟做了这样一场她当真在场的怪梦。不过，眼下还有更要紧的事等着。

总督恶狠狠地咧嘴一笑。

「这艘船从前是我的，」他说。「这儿的欧提克洛斯对我的情分，竟厚到肯拿性命去冒险、来寻我这样一个人，你不觉得滑稽么？依我看来，这实在是荒唐透顶。至于旁的——德·拉福雷爵爷，你睡得那样酣沉，我实在不忍唤醒你。再说，这也是一位人物的意思；据说她对水族颇有几分影响力，这艘船来得这般凑巧，或许正是她的手段。我不知道。你瞧，她如今已经走了，同往常一样，一心只顾她自己的目的。罢了，罢了！她的道路不是我们的道路，不去搅扰才是明智。」

===Original===

But Perion, unarmed and thus surrounded, understood only that he was lost.

“Messire Demetrios,” said Perion, “I never thought to ask a favour of you. I ask it now. For the ring’s sake, give me at least a knife, Messire Demetrios. Let me die fighting.”

“Why, but who spoke of fighting? For the ring’s sake, I have caused the ship to be rifled of what valuables they had aboard. It is not much, but it is all I have. And you are to accept my apologies for the somewhat miscellaneous nature of the cargo, Messire de la Forêt﻿—consisting, as it does, of armours and gems, camphor and ambergris, carpets of raw silk, teakwood and precious metals, rugs of Yemen leather, enamels, and I hardly know what else besides. For Euthyclos, as you will readily understand, was compelled to masquerade as a merchant-trader.”

Perion shook his head, and declared: “You offer enough to make me a wealthy man. But I would prefer a sword.”

At that Demetrios grimaced, saying, “I had hoped to get off more cheaply.” He unbuckled the crosshandled sword which he now wore and handed it to Perion. “This is Flamberge,” Demetrios continued﻿—“that magic blade which Galas made, in the old time’s heyday, for Charlemagne. It was with this sword that I slew my father, and this sword is as dear to me as your ring was to you. The man who wields it is reputed to be unconquerable. I do not know about that, but in any event I yield Flamberge to you as a free gift. I might have known it was the only gift you would accept.” His swart face lighted. “Come presently and fight with me for Melicent. Perhaps it will amuse me to ride out to battle and know I shall not live to see the sunset. Already it seems laughable that you will probably kill me with this very sword which I am touching now.”

===Chinese===

而佩里翁手无寸铁，又被这般团团围住，心里只明白一件事：他是完了。

「德米特里奥斯爵爷，」佩里翁说，「我从没想到会向您求一份情。如今我求了。看在那枚戒指的份上，至少给我一把刀吧，德米特里奥斯爵爷。让我战斗着死去。」

「嘿，可是谁说起打仗了？看在戒指的份上，我已命人把船上载着的贵重财物尽数取出。不算多，却已是我所有的全部。至于这批货色的杂七杂八，还请德·拉福雷爵爷海涵——须知其中装的是甲胄与宝石、樟脑与龙涎香、生丝织的地毯、柚木与贵金属、也门（Yemen）皮的毯子、珐琅器，此外还有什么，我几乎说不上来。因为欧提克洛斯，您不难明白，是不得已才扮作行商的。」

佩里翁摇了摇头，声明道：「您所奉上的，足以使我成为一个富翁。可我更愿意要一柄剑。」

闻言德米特里奥斯做了个苦脸，说道：「我本指望能更便宜地了结。」他解开此刻佩在身上的那柄十字护手的剑，递给佩里翁。「这便是弗朗贝尔热，」德米特里奥斯续道——「便是加拉斯（Galas）于远古鼎盛之世为查理曼（Charlemagne）锻成的那柄魔剑。我弑父用的便是这柄剑；这剑之于我，正如那枚戒指之于你。执此剑者，相传不可战胜。此事真假，我不得而知；但无论如何，我把弗朗贝尔热作为一件无偿的赠礼交到你手上。我早该料到，这是你唯一肯领受的礼物。」他黝黑的面庞亮了起来。「不日就请来与我为梅莉森特一战吧。纵马赴战，且自知决计活不到日落，这在我或许是一桩乐事。你多半就要用我这会儿正抚着的这柄剑杀死我了——如今想来，这已显得可笑。」

===Original===

The champions faced each other, Demetrios in a half-wistful mirth, and Perion in half-grudging pity. Long and long they looked.

Demetrios shrugged. Demetrios said:

“For such as I am, to love is dangerous. For such as I am, nor fire nor meteor hurls a mightier bolt than Aphrodite’s shaft, or marks its passage by more direful ruin. But you do not know Euripides?﻿—a fidgety-footed liar, Messire the Comte, who occasionally blunders into the clumsiest truths. Yes, he is perfectly right; all things this goddess laughingly demolishes while she essays haphazard flights about the world as unforeseeably as travels a bee. And, like the bee, she wilfully dispenses honey, and at other times a wound.”

Said Perion, who was no scholar:

“I glory in our difference. For such as I am, love is sufficient proof that man was fashioned in God’s image.”

“Ey, there is no accounting for a taste in aphorisms,” Demetrios replied. He said, “Now I embark.” Yet he delayed, and spoke with unaccustomed awkwardness. “Come, you who have been generous till this! will you compel me to desert you here﻿—quite penniless?”

===Chinese===

两位豪杰相对而立：德米特里奥斯面上是半含怅惘的欢容，佩里翁是半含不甘的怜悯。他们久久地、久久地相望。

德米特里奥斯耸了耸肩。德米特里奥斯说：

「对于我这样的人，爱是危险的。对于我这样的人，无论烈火还是流星，都不曾掷出比阿芙罗狄忒（Aphrodite）之箭更有力的飞矢，它们过境之处，也不曾留下比它更凄惨的废墟。不过，你没读过欧里庇得斯（Euripides）吧？——一个两脚闲不住的撒谎家，伯爵爵爷，只是偶尔跌跌撞撞，撞进几句最笨拙的真理。是的，他说得全然不错：这位女神一面在世间作漫无章法的飞游，其行踪之不可逆料，一如蜜蜂，一面嬉笑着摧毁万物。而她又同蜜蜂一样，任性地点发蜂蜜，时或点发一处伤创。」

并非学者的佩里翁说道：

「我为我们的相异而欢欣。对于我这样的人，爱便足以证明：人是照着上帝的形象造出来的。」

「唉，人各有所好的格言，原是没有道理可讲的，」德米特里奥斯答道。他说：「如今我要登船了。」然而他耽搁着，带着一种不惯有的笨拙开口：「来吧，你这直到此刻都慷慨大方的人！难道你要逼我把你抛弃在这里——身无分文？」

===Original===

Said Perion:

“I may accept a sword from you. I do accept it gladly. But I may not accept anything else.”

“That would have been my answer. I am a lucky man,” Demetrios said, “to have provoked an enemy so worthy of my opposition. We two have fought an honest and notable duel, wherein our weapons were not made of steel. I pray you harry me as quickly as you may; and then we will fight with swords till I am rid of you or you of me.”

“Assuredly, I shall not fail you,” answered Perion.

===Chinese===

佩里翁说道：

「我可以接受你的一柄剑。我也甘心接受它。但其余任何东西，我都不能接受。」

「这本来也会是我的回答。我真是个幸运的人，」德米特里奥斯说，「竟招惹下这样一位配得上与我作对的仇敌。你我二人已经打过一场光明磊落、声名赫赫的决斗，只是我们的兵器并非钢制。我求你尽快来追击我；然后我们便以剑相搏，直到我摆脱了你，或你摆脱了我。」

「放心，我断不会误你的事，」佩里翁答道。

===Original===

These two embraced and kissed each other. Afterward Demetrios went into his own country, and Perion remained, girt with the magic sword Flamberge. It was not all at once Perion recollected that the wearer of Flamberge is unconquerable, if ancient histories are to be believed, for in deduction Perion was leisurely.

Now on a sudden he perceived that Demetrios had flung control of the future to Perion, as one gives money to a sot, entirely prescient of how it will be used. Perion had his moment of bleak rage.

“I will not cog the dice to my advantage any more than you!” said Perion. He drew the sword of Charlemagne and brandished it and cast it as far as even strong Perion could cast, and the sea swallowed it. “Now God alone is arbiter!” cried Perion, “and I am not afraid.”

He stood a pauper and a friendless man. Beside his thigh hung a sorcerer’s scabbard of blue leather, curiously ornamented, but it was emptied of power. Yet Perion laughed exultingly, because he was elate with dreams of the future. And for the rest, he was aware it is less grateful to remember plaudits than to recall the exercise of that in us which is not merely human.

===Chinese===

这两人相互拥抱，相互亲吻。随后，德米特里奥斯回他自己的国度去了；佩里翁留了下来，腰间围着魔剑弗朗贝尔热。若古老的史传可信，则佩带弗朗贝尔热者不可战胜——这个道理，佩里翁并没有一下子想起来，因为论起推理，佩里翁向来是从容不迫的。

蓦然间，他省悟了：德米特里奥斯是把对未来的掌控掷给了佩里翁，就像人把钱塞给酒鬼，早已把那钱会怎么花销料得分毫不差。佩里翁心中掠过一阵阴冷的狂怒。

「我同你一样，绝不在骰子上做手脚来便宜自己！」佩里翁说。他拔出查理曼之剑，挥舞一番，然后用尽强健如佩里翁者所能使出的全力，将它远远掷出，大海把它吞没了。「如今唯上帝是裁决者！」佩里翁喊道，「而我无所畏惧。」

他站在那里，一个赤贫的人，一个无友的人。腰侧悬着一只术士的青皮剑鞘，饰纹奇巧，神力却已被掏空。然而佩里翁扬声畅笑，因为他对未来的种种梦想正使他意气飞扬。再者，他也明白：回想世人的喝彩，滋味总不如回想我们身上那非仅属凡人之物的施展。

## XXX How Melicent Conquered / 三十 梅莉森特如何克敌制胜

===Original===

Into Melicent’s bedroom, about two o’clock in the morning, came Ahasuerus the Jew. She sat erect in bed and saw him cowering over a lamp which his long glistening fingers shielded, so that the lean face of the man floated upon a little golden pool in the darkness. She marvelled that this detestable countenance had not aged at all since her first sight of it.

He smoothly said:

“Now let us talk. I have loved you for some while, fair Melicent.”

“You have desired me,” she replied.

===Chinese===

凌晨两点钟光景，犹太人亚哈随鲁走进了梅莉森特的卧房。她在床上坐直了身子，只见他佝偻着凑近一盏灯，用修长而莹亮的手指护住灯火；那人瘦削的脸庞，便这样浮在黑暗中一小泓金色的光池之上。她暗自称奇：这张可憎的面容，自她初见以来，竟没有一丝一毫的苍老。

他从容自若地说道：

「现在让我们谈谈吧。我爱慕你，已经有一段时日了，美丽的梅莉森特。」

「你不过是欲得我而已，」她答道。

===Original===

“Faith, I am but as all men, whatever their age. Why, what the devil! man may have Javeh’s breath in him, but even Scripture proves that man was made of clay.” The Jew now puffed out his jaws as if in recollection. “*You are a handsome piece of flesh*, I thought when I came to you at Bellegarde, telling of Perion’s captivity. I thought no more than this, because in my time I have seen a greater number of handsome women than you would suppose. Thereafter, on account of an odd reason which I had, I served Demetrios willingly enough. This son of Miramon Lluagor was able to pay me well, in a curious coinage. So I arranged the bungling snare Demetrios proposed﻿—too gross, I thought it, to trap any woman living. *Ohé*, and why should I not lay an open and frank springe for you? Who else was a king’s bride-to-be, young, beautiful, and blessed with wealth and honour and every other comfort which the world affords?” Now the Jew made as if to fling away a robe from his gaunt person. “And you cast this, all this, aside as nothing. I saw it done.”

“Ah, but I did it to save Perion,” she wisely said.

“Unfathomable liar,” he returned, “you boldly and unscrupulously bought of life the thing which you most earnestly desired. Nor Solomon nor Periander has won more. And thus I saw that which no other man has seen. I saw the shrewd and dauntless soul of Melicent. And so I loved you, and I laid my plan﻿—”

===Chinese===

「说真的，我不过和天下男人一个样，不论年岁几何。嗐，见鬼！人身上纵有雅威（Javeh）的气息，可连《圣经》也作证：人本是泥土捏成的。」这时犹太人鼓起两腮，仿佛在追忆什么。「『好一件漂亮的血肉』——当年我到贝勒加德去见你、告诉你佩里翁被囚的消息时，心里想的不过这一句。此外再无别的念头，因为在我这一生里，见过的漂亮女子比你所能料想的多得多。其后，由于我心头一个古怪的理由，我相当甘愿地侍奉起德米特里奥斯来。米拉蒙·卢阿戈尔（Miramon Lluagor）的这个儿子，付得起优厚的酬报，用的是一种古怪的钱币。于是，德米特里奥斯设想的那个笨拙圈套，便由我一手布置——我当时便觉得它粗陋得很，套不住世上任何一个活女人。哦嗬，我何不干脆给你布下一个光明磊落、坦然无忌的罗网？普天之下，除了你，还有谁是国王的准新娘——年轻，貌美，享尽财富尊荣，享尽尘世所能赐予的一切安乐？」这时犹太人做了个动作，仿佛要从他瘦削的身躯上甩脱一件长袍。「而你把这，把这所有的一切，弃若敝屣。这一切，我都亲眼看着。」

「啊，可我那样做是为了搭救佩里翁呀，」她振振有词地说道。

「深不可测的说谎人，」他回道，「你大着胆子、不择手段地向生命本身，买到了你最热切渴求的东西。所罗门（Solomon）不曾赢得更多，佩里安德（Periander）也不曾。于是我见到了任何一个男人都不曾见过的东西。我见到了梅莉森特那精明无畏的灵魂。于是我爱上了你，于是我布下了我的计划——」

===Original===

She said, “You do not know of love﻿—”

“Yet I have builded him a temple,” the Jew considered. He continued, with that old abhorrent acquiescence, “Now, a temple is admirable, but it is not builded until many labourers have dug and toiled waist-deep in dirt. Here, too, such spatterment seemed necessary. So I played, in fine, I played a cunning music. The pride of Demetrios, the jealousy of Callistion, and the greed of Orestes﻿—these were as so many stops of that flute on which I played a cunning deadly music. Who forbids it?”

She motioned him, “Go on.” Now she was not afraid.

“Come then to the last note of my music! You offer to bargain, saying, *Save Perion and have my body as your chattel*. I answer *Click*! The turning of a key solves all. Accordingly I have betrayed the castle of Nacumera, I have this night admitted Perion and his broad-shouldered men. They are killing Orestes yonder in the Court of Stars even while I talk with you.” Ahasuerus laughed noiselessly. “Such vanity does not become a Jew, but I needs must do the thing with some magnificence. Therefore I do not give Sire Perion only his life. I give him also victory and much throat-cutting and an impregnable rich castle. Have I not paid the price, fair Melicent? Have I not won God’s masterpiece through a small wire, a purse, and a big key?”

===Chinese===

她说：「你根本不懂得爱——」

「话虽如此，我到底为爱造起过一座殿堂，」犹太人沉吟道。他又接着说下去，带着那副一贯的、令人憎厌的依顺神气：「要知道，殿堂固然可敬，可若没有许多劳役在没腰的污泥里掘挖苦干，它便起不来。在这件事上，这般溅泥带垢，似乎也在所难免。于是我终于吹奏起来——我吹奏了一支狡狯的乐曲。德米特里奥斯的骄矜，卡利斯提翁的妒恨，俄瑞斯忒斯的贪欲——这些便是我那管笛子上一个个按孔，我在上面吹出了狡狯而致命的曲调。谁又禁止得了呢？」

她示意他：「说下去。」此刻，她已经不害怕了。

「那就来听听我乐曲的最后一音吧！你提议交易，说：『救下佩里翁，把我的身体当作你的私产。』我回答一声：咔哒！钥匙一转，万事皆休。为此，我出卖了纳库梅拉城堡，今夜已把佩里翁和他那些宽肩膀的汉子放了进来。就在我与你说话的当口，他们正在那边的星辰宫里结果俄瑞斯忒斯呢。」亚哈随鲁无声地笑了。「这样的虚荣，本不合犹太人的身份，可我做事，总得办出几分气派。所以我给佩里翁爵爷的，不止是一条性命。我还给他胜利，给他一场痛快的杀人见血，给他一座固若金汤的富庶城堡。我难道没有付足价钱么，美丽的梅莉森特？我岂不是凭一根细铁丝、一只钱袋、一把大钥匙，就赢得了上帝的杰作？」

===Original===

She answered, “You have paid.”

He said:

“You will hold to your bargain? Ah, you have but to cry aloud, and you are rid of me. For this is Perion’s castle.”

She said, “Christ help me! You have paid my price.”

Now the Jew raised his two hands in very horrible mirth. Said he:

“Oh, I am almost tempted to praise Javeh, who created the invincible soul of Melicent. For you have conquered: you have gained, as always, and at whatever price, exactly that which you most desired, and you do not greatly care about anything else. So, because of a word said you would arise and follow me on my dark ways if I commanded it. You will not weight the dice, not even at this pinch, when it would be so easy! For Perion is safe; and nothing matters in comparison with that, and you will not break faith, not even with me. You are inexplicable, you are stupid, and you are resistless. Again I see my Melicent, who is not just a pair of purple eyes and so much lovely flesh.”

===Chinese===

她答道：「你付足了。」

他说：

「你肯信守这桩买卖么？啊，你只消高声一喊，便可以摆脱我。因为这里是佩里翁的城堡了。」

她说：「基督佑助我！你付清了我的价钱。」

这时犹太人举起双手，那副欢愉煞是骇人。他说道：

「哦，我几乎忍不住要赞颂雅威了，是他造出了梅莉森特这副不可战胜的灵魂。因为你已经得胜了：一如既往，不问代价几何，你得到的恰恰是你最渴望的东西，而对其余的一切，你都殊不在意。所以，只因一句许下的话，我若下令，你便会起身随我走上我那些黑暗的路途。你不肯给骰子灌铅，哪怕身处眼下的窘境——尽管这事易如反掌！因为佩里翁平安了；与之相比，万事皆不足轻重；而你也不肯背信，哪怕是对我。你匪夷所思，你愚不可及，而你无可抗拒。我又见到了我的梅莉森特——她不只是一双紫眸，加上那么一副可爱的皮肉。」

===Original===

His face was as she had not ever known it now, and very tender. Ahasuerus said:

“My way to victory is plain enough. And yet there is an obstacle. For my fancy is taken by the soul of Melicent, and not by that handsome piece of flesh which all men﻿—even Perion, madame!﻿—have loved so long with remarkable infatuation. Accordingly I had not ever designed that the edifice on which I laboured should be the stable of my lusts. Accordingly I played my cunning music﻿—and accordingly I give you Perion. I that am Ahasuerus win for you all which righteousness and honour could not win. At the last it is I who give you Perion, and it is I who bring you to his embrace. He must still be about his magnanimous butchery, I think, in the Court of Stars.”

Ahasuerus knelt, kissing her hand.

“Fair Melicent, such abominable persons as Demetrios and I are fatally alike. We may deny, deride, deplore, or even hate, the sanctity of any noble lady accordingly as we elect; but there is for us no possible escape from worshipping it. Your wind-fed Ferions, who will not ever acknowledge what sort of world we live in, are less quick to recognise the soul of Melicent. Such is our sorry consolation. Oh, you do not believe me yet. You will believe in the oncoming years. Meanwhile, O all-enduring and all-conquering! go now to your last labour; and﻿—if my Brother dare concede as much﻿—do you now conquer Perion.”

===Chinese===

此刻他的面容，是她从未见识过的模样，而且还十分温柔。亚哈随鲁说道：

「我通往胜利的路，已经够平坦了。然而还横着一重障碍。因为夺去我心神的，是梅莉森特的灵魂，而不是那件漂亮的血肉——世人，连佩里翁也在内，夫人！——都曾怀着那般痴迷的爱恋，爱了它这么长久。所以，我从不曾打算让自己惨淡经营的这座殿堂，沦为豢养我情欲的厩栏。于是我吹奏了我的狡狯乐曲——于是我把他交还给你。我，亚哈随鲁，为你赢得了正道与荣誉所不能赢得的一切。归根结底，是我把佩里翁给了你，是我把你送进他的怀抱。此刻，他想必还在星辰宫里，忙着他那番宽宏大量的屠戮吧。」

亚哈随鲁跪了下来，吻她的手。

「美丽的梅莉森特，德米特里奥斯和我这样的可憎之徒，命里注定彼此相像。任凭我们否认、讥嘲、哀叹，乃至憎恨某位贵妇的圣洁，全随我们高兴；可要逃避对这圣洁的崇奉，我们却万无可能。你们那些餐风饮露的费里翁（Ferion）们，永远不肯承认我们活在怎样一个世界上，要认出梅莉森特的灵魂，也要迟钝得多。这便是我们可怜的慰藉。哦，你如今还不肯信我。来日方长，你自会相信的。去吧，啊，坚忍一切、克胜一切的人！去赴你最后的劳作；还有——倘若我那位兄长敢许让这么多——去，把佩里翁也征服了吧。」

===Original===

Then he vanished. She never saw him any more.

She lifted the Jew’s lamp. She bore it through the Women’s Garden, wherein were many discomfortable shadows and no living being. She came to its outer entrance. Men were fighting there. She skirted a hideous conflict, and descended the Queen’s Stairway, which led (as you have heard) toward the balcony about the Court of Stars. She found this balcony vacant.

Below her men were fighting. To the farther end of the court Orestes sprawled upon the red and yellow slabs﻿—which now for the most part were red﻿—and above him towered Perion of the Forest. The conqueror had paused to cleanse his sword upon the same divan Demetrios had occupied when Melicent first saw the proconsul; and as Perion turned, in the act of sheathing his sword, he perceived the dear familiar denizen of all his dreams. A tiny lamp glowed in her hand quite steadily.

===Chinese===

随后他消逝了。她从此再也没有见过他。

她提起犹太人的灯。她提着灯穿过女眷园——园中只有许多令人不安的暗影，不见一个活人。她走到园子的外侧入口。那里正有人在厮杀。她绕开一场可憎的混战，走下王后阶梯——如你所闻，这阶梯通向环绕星辰宫的露台。她发觉这露台空无一人。

露台之下，人们在厮杀。庭院远端，俄瑞斯忒斯摊仰在红黄相间的石板上——此刻大半已是红色——他身上高高耸立着林中佩里翁。得胜者方才驻了足，正俯在那张长榻上拭净佩剑——当初梅莉森特初见总督时，德米特里奥斯坐的正是这一张；佩里翁转身纳剑入鞘的当口，望见了自己全部梦境中那位亲爱而熟稔的居客。一盏小小的灯，在她手里燃得十分平稳。

---

===Original===

“O Melicent,” said Perion, with a great voice, “my task is done. Come now to me.”

She instantly obeyed whose only joy was to please Perion. Descending the enclosed stairway, she thought how like its gloom was to the temporal unhappiness she had passed through in serving Perion.

He stood a dripping statue, for he had fought horribly. She came to him, picking her way among the slain. He trembled who was fresh from slaying. A flood of torchlight surged and swirled about them, and within a stone’s cast Perion’s men were despatching the wounded.

These two stood face to face and did not speak at all.

===Chinese===

「哦，梅莉森特，」佩里翁声如洪钟地说道，「我的功业已成。现在，到我这里来吧。」

她当即从命——取悦佩里翁，本是她唯一的欢欣。走下那道封闭的阶梯时，她心里想：这一路幽暗，多么像她为侍奉佩里翁而熬过的那些尘世愁苦。

他伫立着，像一尊淋淋漓漓滴着水的雕像，因为他方才打了一场惨烈的恶仗。她向他走去，在尸骸之间择路而行。他——这个刚从杀戮里出来的人——正在发颤。一片火炬的光潮在他们周身翻涌回旋，一石之遥的地方，佩里翁的部下正在结果那些伤者。

这两个人面对面站着，全然一言不发。

===Original===

I think that he knew disappointment first. He looked to find the girl whom he had left on Fomor Beach.

He found a woman, the possessor still of a compelling beauty. Oh, yes, past doubt: but this woman was a stranger to him, as he now knew with an odd sense of sickness. Thus, then, had ended the quest of Melicent. Their love had flouted Time and Fate. These had revenged this insolence, it seemed to Perion, by an ironical conversion of each rebel into another person. For this was not the girl whom Perion had loved in far red-roofed Poictesme; this was not the girl for whom Perion had fought ten minutes since: and he﻿—as Perion for the first time perceived﻿—was not and never could be any more the Perion that girl had bidden return to her. It were as easy to evoke the Perion who had loved Mélusine.﻿ ﻿…

Then Perion perceived that love may be a power so august as to bedwarf consideration of the man and woman whom it sways. He saw that this is reasonable. I cannot justify this knowledge. I cannot even tell you just what great secret it was of which Perion became aware. Many men have seen the sunrise, but the serenity and awe and sweetness of this daily miracle, the huge assurance which it emanates that the beholder is both impotent and greatly beloved, is not entirely an affair of the sky’s tincture. And thus it was with Perion. He knew what he could not explain. He knew such joy and terror as none has ever worded. A curtain had lifted briefly; and the familiar world which Perion knew, for the brief instant, had appeared to be a painting upon that curtain.

===Chinese===

我想，他先尝到的是失望。他原指望见到的，是他留在福莫尔海滩上的那个少女。

他找到的却是一个女人，依然怀有摄人心魄的美貌。哦，是的，毋庸置疑：可这个女人于他是一个陌生人——此刻他怀着一种奇异的病楚之感，才认清了这一点。于是，寻访梅莉森特的旅程，便这样到了尽头。他们的爱情曾嘲弄时间与命运。而在佩里翁看来，时间与命运报复这份狂妄的办法，是把这两个叛儿讥诮地各自变作了另一个人。因为这已不是他在遥远的红瓦波伊捷斯所爱的那个少女；这也不是十分钟前他还为之苦战的那个少女：而他——佩里翁生平第一次省悟到——也已经不是、且永远不再可能是那个少女曾嘱他回到她身边去的佩里翁了。正如要唤回那个爱上梅吕西娜的佩里翁，也是一般容易。……

于是佩里翁省悟到：爱可以是一种如此威严的力量，以至于受它拨弄的男人和女人，都显得渺不足道。他看出这是合理的。我无法为这一知见申辩。我甚至说不出，佩里翁当时觉察到的，究竟是何等重大的一个秘密。许多人都见过日出，可这每日奇迹中的宁谧、敬畏与甘美，它所焕发的那股浩然的确信——确信观者既无能为力，又深蒙眷爱——却又不尽然关乎天光的色调。佩里翁的际遇也是如此。他知道了他无法解释的东西。他尝到了从未有人用言语道出的欢欣与惶恐。一道帷幕曾短暂掀起；佩里翁所熟识的这个世界，在那短促的一瞬里，竟显出原是绘在那帷幕上的一幅画。

---

===Original===

Now, dazzled, he saw Melicent for the first time.﻿ ﻿…

I think he saw the lines already forming in her face, and knew that, but for him, this woman, naked now of gear and friends, had been tonight a queen among her own acclaiming people. I think he worshipped where he did not dare to love, as every man cannot but do when starkly fronted by the divine and stupendous unreason of a woman’s choice, among so many other men, of him. And yet, I think that Perion recalled what Ayrart de Montors had said of women and their love, so long ago:﻿—“They are more wise than we; and always they make us better by indomitably believing we are better than in reality a man can ever be.”

I think that Perion knew, now, de Montors had been in the right. The pity and mystery and beauty of that world wherein High God had﻿—scornfully?﻿—placed a smug Perion, seemed to the Comte de la Forêt, I think, unbearable. I think a new and finer love smote Perion as a sword strikes.

I think he did not speak because there was no scope for words. I know that he knelt (incurious for once of victory) before this stranger who was not the Melicent whom he had sought so long, and that all consideration of a lost young Melicent departed from him, as mists leave our world when the sun rises.

I think that this was her high hour of triumph.

Caetera Desunt

===Chinese===

此刻，他目眩神驰，第一次看见了梅莉森特。……

我想，他看见她脸上已然初绽的细纹，并且知道，若不是为了他，这个如今衣装与亲朋俱已剥尽的女人，今夜本该君临在自己子民的欢呼声中，做她的王后。我想，他在不敢爱之处奉上了崇拜；正如每个男人，当一个女子从那么多男子之中偏偏选中他、那神圣而惊人的非理性当面逼来时，都不能不如此。然而我想，佩里翁还记得艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔在很久以前说过的、关于女人和她们的爱的那句话：——「她们比我们有智慧；她们总是不屈不挠地相信我们比一个男人实际所能企及的更好，从而把我们变得更好。」

我想，佩里翁此刻明白了：德·蒙托尔当年说得不错。那个至高的上帝——带着轻蔑么？——将一个志得意满的佩里翁安置于其中的世界，它的怜悯、神秘与美，我想，在拉福雷伯爵看来是难以忍受的。我想，一种崭新而更精纯的爱，像剑锋劈落一般击中了佩里翁。

我想，他没有开口，是因为言语在这里全无余地。我知道，他跪了下来（平生头一遭不复关心胜负），跪在这个陌生女人面前——她已不是他苦苦寻觅了这许久的那个梅莉森特——而那个失去了的年轻梅莉森特的一切牵念，也从他心头散尽了，如同日出之时，雾霭散出于我们的世界。

我想，这正是她凯旋的至高时辰。

*Caetera Desunt*（余文阙如）

## VI How Melicent Sought Oversea / 六 梅莉森特如何渡海寻访

===Original===

It is a tale which they narrate in Poictesme, telling how love began between Perion of the Forest, who was a captain of mercenaries, and young Melicent, who was daughter to the great Dom Manuel, and sister to Count Emmerik of Poictesme. They tell also how Melicent and Perion were parted, because there was no remedy, and policy demanded she should wed King Theodoret.

And the tale tells how Perion sailed with his retainers to seek desperate service under the harried Kaiser of the Greeks.

This venture was ill-fated, since, as the Free Companions were passing not far from Masillia, their vessel being at the time becalmed, they were attacked by three pagan galleys under the admiralty of the proconsul Demetrios. Perion’s men, who fought so hardily on land, were novices at sea. They were powerless against an adversary who, from a great distance, showered liquid fire upon their vessel.

Then Demetrios sent little boats and took some thirty prisoners from the blazing ship, and made slaves of all save Ahasuerus the Jew, whom he released on being informed of the lean man’s religion. It was a customary boast of this Demetrios that he made war on Christians only.

And presently, as Perion had commanded, Ahasuerus came to Melicent.

===Chinese===

这是他们在波伊捷斯讲述的故事：爱如何开始在佣兵队长林中佩里翁与年轻的梅莉森特之间——她是伟大的多姆·曼努埃尔（Dom Manuel）之女、波伊捷斯伯爵埃默里克之妹。他们还讲到，梅莉森特与佩里翁如何被生生拆散，因为无可挽回，而权略又要求她嫁给泰奥多雷特王。

故事又讲到，佩里翁如何携麾下部众扬帆出海，去投那备受侵扰的希腊人的皇帝（Kaiser of the Greeks），寻一场亡命的效力。

此番冒险命数不济：自由佣兵（Free Companions）行经马西利亚（Masillia）不远处的海面时，船只恰逢无风而停滞，便遭到总督德米特里奥斯统率的三艘异教桨帆战船的攻击。佩里翁的部众在陆上何等剽悍，到了海上却是生手。面对那从远处便向他们的船只倾泻液火的敌手，他们全然无能为力。

随后，德米特里奥斯派出小艇，从那艘烈焰熊熊的船上掳走约莫三十名俘虏，把他们尽数没为奴隶，唯独放过了犹太人亚哈随鲁——一经得知这瘦削男子的教门，便将他开释了。这位德米特里奥斯惯常夸口，说他只对基督徒开战。

不多时，亚哈随鲁便奉佩里翁之嘱，来见梅莉森特。

===Original===

The princess sat in a high chair, the back of which was capped with a big lion’s head in brass. It gleamed above her head, but was less glorious than her bright hair.

Ahasuerus made dispassionate report. “Thus painfully I have delivered, as my task was, these fine messages concerning Faith and Love and Death and so on. Touching their rationality I may reserve my own opinion. I am merely Perion’s echo. Do I echo madness? This madman was my loved and honoured master once, a lord without any peer in the fields where men contend in battle. Today those sinews which preserved a throne are dedicated to the transportation of luggage. Grant it is laughable. I do not laugh.”

“And I lack time to weep,” said Melicent.

So, when the Jew had told his tale and gone, young Melicent arose and went into a chamber painted with the histories of Jason and Medea, where her brother Count Emmerick hid such jewels as had not many equals in Christendom.

She did not hesitate. She took no thought for her brother, she did not remember her loved sisters: Ettarre and Dorothy were their names, and they also suffered for their beauty, and for the desire it quickened in the hearts of men. Melicent knew only that Perion was in captivity and might not look for aid from any person living save herself.

===Chinese===

公主坐在一张高椅上，椅背顶端镶着一颗硕大的黄铜狮头。狮头在她头顶闪闪发光，却仍不及她那头明亮的秀发荣耀夺目。

亚哈随鲁不动声色地复命。「我便这样忍着苦楚，如差事所责，把这番关于信仰、爱情、死亡等等的漂亮口信一一送到了。至于它们是否合乎理性，我姑且保留一己之见。我不过是佩里翁的回声。我回响的可是疯狂？这个疯子曾是我敬爱的主人，一位在男儿们兵戈相争的疆场上无可匹敌的君侯。到今天，那副曾经撑起一座王座的筋骨，被派去搬运行李了。就算这事可笑吧。我笑不出来。」

「我连哭泣的工夫都没有。」梅莉森特说。

于是，犹太人把来龙去脉述毕、抽身离去之后，年轻的梅莉森特站起身来，走进一间四壁画着伊阿宋（Jason）与美狄亚（Medea）故事的屋子——她的兄长、波伊捷斯伯爵埃默里克，便把那些在基督世界罕有其匹的珠宝藏在这屋里。

她没有半分迟疑。她全不为兄长着想，也想不起自己挚爱的姊妹：她们名叫埃塔尔（Ettarre）与多萝西（Dorothy），她们同样为自己的美貌、为美貌在男人们心中撩起的欲念而受苦。梅莉森特只知道，佩里翁沦为囚徒，除了她自己，再不能指望世上任何活人相救。

===Original===

She gathered in a blue napkin such emeralds as would ransom a pope. She cut short her marvellous hair and disguised herself in all things as a man, and under cover of the ensuing night slipped from the castle. At Manneville she found a Venetian ship bound homeward with a cargo of swords and armour.

She hired herself to the captain of this vessel as a servant, calling herself Jocelin Gaignars. She found no time﻿—wherein to be afraid or to grieve for the estate she was relinquishing, so long as Perion lay in danger.

Thus the young Jocelin, though not without hardship and odd by-ends of adventure here irrelevant, came with time’s course into a land of sunlight and much wickedness where Perion was.

There the boy found in what fashion Perion was living and won the dearly purchased misery of seeing him, from afar, in his deplorable condition, as Perion went through the outer yard of Nacumera laden with chains and carrying great logs toward the kitchen. This befell when Jocelin had come into the hill country, where the eyrie of Demetrios blocked a crag-hung valley as snugly as a stone chokes a gutter-pipe.

Young Jocelin had begged an audience of this heathen lord and had obtained it﻿—though Jocelin did not know as much﻿—with ominous facility.

===Chinese===

她用一方蓝色布巾收拢了那批足以赎出一位教皇的祖母绿。她剪短那头不可思议的秀发，浑身上下装扮成男子，趁着随后而来的夜色掩蔽，悄然溜出了城堡。在曼讷维尔（Manneville），她寻到一艘满载刀剑甲胄、即将启程归国的威尼斯船。

她受雇于这条船的船长充当仆役，自称若斯林·盖尼亚尔（Jocelin Gaignars）。只要佩里翁尚在危难之中，她便寻不出一点工夫，去害怕，去为自己正在舍弃的身家哀伤。

就这样，年轻的若斯兰虽不乏艰辛，途中又生出些与此处无干的奇遇旁枝，终究随着时序的流转，来到一片阳光灿然而罪恶盛行的土地，佩里翁便在那里。

在那里，这少年探明了佩里翁过着怎样的日子，也换来了那份以重价购得的凄楚：远远望见他境况之凄惨——佩里翁锁链加身，扛着粗大的木柴穿过纳库梅拉（Nacumera）的外院，向厨房走去。其时若斯兰已进入山地；在那儿，德米特里奥斯的巢穴嵌在一道危岩高悬的山谷口，严严实实，恰似一颗石子堵住檐槽的水管。

年轻的若斯兰求得觐见这位异教君侯，而且一求即得——其顺利，兆头凶险，只是若斯兰自己并不知情。

## V How Melicent Wedded / 五 梅莉森特如何成婚

===Original===

“That may not be, my cousin.”

It was the real Bishop of Montors who was speaking. His company, some fifteen men in all, had ridden up while Melicent and Perion looked seaward. The bishop was clothed, in his habitual fashion, as a cavalier, showing in nothing as a churchman. He sat a-horseback for a considerable while, looking down at them, smiling and stroking the pommel of his saddle with a gold-fringed glove. It was now dawn.

“I have been eavesdropping,” the bishop said. His voice was tender, for the young man loved his kinswoman with an affection second only to that which he reserved for Ayrart de Montors. “Yes, I have been eavesdropping for an instant, and through that instant I seemed to see the heart of every woman that ever lived; and they differed only as stars differ on a fair night in August. No woman ever loved a man except, at bottom, as a mother loves her child: let him elect to build a nation or to write imperishable verses or to take purses upon the highway, and she will only smile to note how breathlessly the boy goes about his playing; and when he comes back to her with grimier hands she is a little sorry, and, if she think it salutary, will pretend to be angry. Meanwhile she sets about the quickest way to cleanse him and to heal his bruises. They are more wise than we, and at the bottom of their hearts they pity us more stalwart folk whose grosser wits require, to be quite sure of anything, a mere crass proof of it; and always they make us better by indomitably believing we are better than in reality a man can ever be.”

===Chinese===

「那是万万不能的，我的表妹。」

说话的正是蒙托尔主教本人。趁梅莉森特与佩里翁眺望大海的当口，他率领的一行人马——总共约十五骑——已经驰到近前。主教一如平素的做派，通身骑士装束，丝毫不见教士气象。他在马上端坐了好一阵，居高临下望着这两人，含着笑，用一只缀金流苏的手套抚弄鞍头。此刻已是破晓时分。

「我方才一直在偷听，」主教说道。他的嗓音很温柔，因为这位年轻人深爱他的女亲眷，那份情意仅次于他留给艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔本人的那一分。「是的，我只偷听了片刻，而在那片刻之间，我仿佛看透了古往今来一切女人的心；她们彼此的差别，不过是八月晴夜里星辰与星辰之间的差别。女人爱男人，归根结底，无不似母亲爱孩子：任他立志去缔造一个国家，去写不朽的诗篇，或是去大路上剪径劫财，她也只会微笑着，看这孩子做他的游戏做得何等忘情；待他回到她身边，两手比先前更脏了些，她会微微心疼，倘若觉得有益，还会装出嗔怒的模样。与此同时，她已着手用最快捷的法子把他洗净，替他敷好伤痕。她们比我们更有智慧；在心底深处，她们怜悯我们这些更孔武的人——我们粗钝的头脑，要对一件事确信不疑，非要一件蛮实的凭据不可。而她们总能不屈不挠地相信，我们好得远超过任何男子实际所能企及的地步，就这样把我们变得更好。」

===Original===

Now Ayrart de Montors dismounted.

“So much for my sermon. For the rest, Messire de la Forêt, I perfectly recognised you on the day you came to Bellegarde. But I said nothing. For that you had not murdered King Helmas, as is popularly reported, I was certain, inasmuch as I happen to know he is now at Brunbelois, where Dame Mélusine holds his person and his treasury. A terrible, delicious woman! begotten on a water-demon, people say. I ask no questions. She is a close and useful friend to me, and through her aid I hope to go far. You see that I am frank. It is my nature.” The bishop shrugged. “In a phrase, I accepted the Vicomte de Puysange, although it was necessary, of course, to keep an eye upon your comings in and your goings out, as you now see. And until this the imposture amused me. But this”﻿—his hand waved toward the *Tranchemer*﻿—“this, my fair friends, is past a jest.”

“You talk and talk,” cried Perion, “while I reflect that I love the fairest lady who at any time has had life upon earth.”

===Chinese===

这时，艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔翻身下了马。

「我的布道就到此为止。至于其余，德·拉福雷爵爷，你初到贝勒加德的那一天，我就把你认了个透澈。可我什么也没说。至于外间盛传你谋害了赫尔马斯王（King Helmas）一事，我确信你并未下手，因为我恰好知道，他如今就在布兰布卢瓦，梅吕西娜夫人拘着他的人，也拘着他的财库。一个可怕又迷人的女人！据说是一位水妖所生。我不多问。她于我是亲近而有用的朋友，借她之力，我指望能走得极远。你瞧，我是坦诚的。天性使然。」主教耸了耸肩。「一言以蔽之，我认可了这位皮桑日子爵（Vicomte de Puysange），当然喽，自然免不了盯着你的来踪去迹，这你如今也看见了。而直到此刻，这桩冒名顶替的把戏一直逗我发笑。可是这个」——他的手朝特朗什梅尔号（*Tranchemer*）一挥——「这个，我漂亮的朋友们，可就远不止玩笑了。」

「你说个没完，说个没完，」佩里翁喊道，「我心里想的却是：我爱着自有生民以来尘寰之上最美的一位淑女。」

===Original===

“The proof of your affection,” the bishop returned, “is, if you will permit the observation, somewhat extraordinary. For you propose, I gather, to make of her a camp-follower, a soldier’s drab. Come, come, *messire*! you and I are conversant with warfare as it is. Armies do not conduct encounters by throwing sugar-candy at one another. What home have you, a landless man, to offer Melicent? What place is there for Melicent among your Free Companions?”

“Oh, do I not know that!” said Perion. He turned to Melicent, and long and long they gazed upon each other.

“Ignoble as I am,” said Perion, “I never dreamed to squire an angel down toward the mire and filth which for a while as yet must be my kennel. I go. I go alone. Do you bid me return?”

The girl was perfectly calm. She took a ring of diamonds from her hand, and placed it on his little finger, because the others were too large.

===Chinese===

「你这深情的凭据，」主教回敬道，「恕我直言，未免有些异乎寻常。因为据我看来，你打算把她弄成一个随营的婆娘、一个军汉的姘妇。得了，得了，爵爷！你我都是识得战争本相的人。两军交锋，可不是拿糖果你掷我扔地闹着玩。你这没有寸土的人，拿什么家园献给梅莉森特？在你的自由佣兵中间，又有什么地方容得下梅莉森特？」

「唉，我何尝不知道！」佩里翁说。他转向梅莉森特，两人久久地、久久地彼此凝望。

「我虽出身微贱，」佩里翁说，「却从不曾梦想过，要护卫一位天使下到那泥淖污秽里去——那地方一时半刻还得充作我的犬窝。我走了。我独自走。你可肯吩咐我回来？」

那少女镇定至极。她从自己手上褪下一枚钻石戒指，套在他的小指上，因为戴到其余的指头上都嫌太大了。

===Original===

“While life endures I pledge you faith and service, Perion. There is no need to speak of love.”

“There is no need,” he answered. “Oh, does God think that I will live without you!”

“I suppose they will give me to King Theodoret. The terrible old man has set my body as the only price that will buy him off from ravaging Poictesme, and he is stronger in the field than Emmerick. Emmerick is afraid of him, and Ayrart here has need of the King’s friendship in order to become a cardinal. So my kinsmen must make traffic of my eyes and lips and hair. But first I wed you, Perion, here in the sight of God, and I bid you return to me, who am your wife and servitor forever now, whatever lesser men may do.”

“I will return,” he said.

Then in a little while she withdrew her lips from his lips.

“Cover my face, Ayrart. It may be I shall weep presently. Men must not see the wife of Perion weep. Cover my face, for he is going now, and I cannot watch his going.”

===Chinese===

「只要此生未尽，我便向你许以忠贞与效命，佩里翁。至于爱，无须再说。」

「无须再说，」他答道。「啊，难道上帝以为，我离了你还能独活么！」

「我想，他们终究会把我许给泰奥多雷特王。那可怕的老翁已然定下：唯有我的身体，才买得动他罢手，不去蹂躏波伊捷斯；而他在战阵上又比埃默里克更强。埃默里克怕他，这里的艾亚特又需要王的友谊，好升作枢机主教。于是我的亲眷们只好拿我的眼睛、嘴唇和头发去做买卖。可是在此之前，我先嫁与你，佩里翁，就在此地，在上帝的眼前；我吩咐你回到我身边来——从今往后，无论等而下之的人们如何作为，我永远是你的妻，你的仆役。」

「我会回来的。」他说。

过了片刻，她把自己的唇从他唇上收了回来。

「替我遮住脸，艾亚特。只怕我一会儿就要落泪了。男人们不可看见佩里翁的妻子流泪。遮住我的脸吧，因为他这就走了，我不忍看他离去。」

## III How Melicent Wooed / 三 梅莉森特如何求爱

===Original===
Then Perion knew that vain regret had turned his brain, very certainly, for it seemed the door had opened and Dame Melicent herself had come, warily, into the panelled gloomy room. It seemed that Melicent paused in the convulsive brilliancy of the firelight, and stayed thus with vaguely troubled eyes like those of a child newly wakened from sleep.
And it seemed a long while before she told Perion very quietly that she had confessed all to Ayrart de Montors, and had, by reason of de Montors’ love for her, so goaded and allured the outcome of their talk﻿—“ignobly,” as she said﻿—that a clean-handed gentleman would come at three o’clock for Perion de la Forêt, and guide a thief toward unmerited impunity. All this she spoke quite levelly, as one reads aloud from a book; and then, with a signal change of voice, Melicent said: “Yes, that is true enough. Yet why, in reality, do you think I have in my own person come to tell you of it?”
“Madame, I may not guess. Hah, indeed, indeed,” Perion cried, because he knew the truth and was unspeakably afraid, “I dare not guess!”
“You sail tomorrow for the fighting oversea﻿—” she began, but her sweet voice trailed and died into silence. He heard the crepitations of the fire, and even the hurried beatings of his own heart, as against a terrible and lovely hush of all created life. “Then take me with you.”

===Chinese===
佩里翁这才明白，自己必是叫徒然的悔恨搅昏了头脑；因为门仿佛开了，梅莉森特夫人亲自走了进来，提着小心，走进这间镶着壁板、一片幽暗的屋子。她仿佛在火光跳荡的璀璨里停住了脚步，就这样伫立着，眼里含着隐约的不安，像一个初从睡梦中醒来的孩子。
仿佛过了许久，她才极平静地向佩里翁吐露：她已把一切向艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔招认了，并且——用她自己的话说，「卑劣地」——凭着德·蒙托尔对她的爱恋，连逼带诱地左右了他们那场谈话的结果，使得一位双手干净的绅士将于三点钟来寻佩里翁·德·拉福雷，引一个窃贼去领那份非分的免罪。这一切她说得平平稳稳，像是从一本书上诵读出来的；随后梅莉森特陡然换了一种声口：「是啊，这倒是千真万确。可你想过没有：我究竟为什么亲身前来，把这件事说给你听？」
「夫人，我猜不得。哈，真的，真的，」佩里翁喊道，因为他知道真相，心里怕得说不出的厉害，「我不敢猜！」
「明日你便要扬帆出海，去赴海外的战事——」她起了个头，可那甜美的嗓音渐渐低微下去，终于归于沉寂。他听得见炉火的噼啪，甚至自己心口的急跳，衬着万有生灵那可畏而又可爱的静默。「那就带我同去。」

===Original===
Perion had never any recollection of what he answered. Indeed, he uttered no communicative words, but only foolish babblements.
“Oh, I do not understand,” said Melicent. “It is as though some spell were laid upon me. Look you, I have been cleanly reared, I have never wronged any person that I know of, and throughout my quiet, sheltered life I have loved truth and honour most of all. My judgment grants you to be what you are confessedly. And there is that in me more masterful and surer than my judgment, that which seems omniscient and lightly puts aside your confessings as unimportant.”
“Lackey, impostor, and thief!” young Perion answered. “There you have the catalogue of all my rightful titles fairly earned.”
“And even if I believed you, I think I would not care! Is that not strange? For then I should despise you. And even then, I think, I would fling my honour at your feet, as I do now, and but in part with loathing, I would still entreat you to make of me your wife, your servant, anything that pleased you.﻿ ﻿… Oh, I had thought that when love came it would be sweet!”

===Chinese===
佩里翁全然记不起自己答了些什么。的确，他吐出的不是达意的话语，只是些痴傻的呓语。
「哦，我不明白，」梅莉森特说，「仿佛有什么符咒加在了我身上。你瞧，我自幼教养清白，据我所知从不曾亏负过任何人；在我一生恬静而受着庇护的岁月里，我把真实与名誉看得比什么都重。我的理智承认，你正如你自认的那种人。可我身内另有一个东西，比我的理智更强横、也更确凿，它仿佛无所不知，把你那些自白轻轻撇在一旁，只当无关紧要。」
「仆役、骗子、窃贼！」年轻的佩里翁答道，「这便是一张清单，开着我挣来得堂堂正正的全部名衔。」
「就算我相信你，我想我也毫不在意！这岂不奇怪？因为那样我本该鄙视你才是。可即便那样，我想，我仍会把我的名节掷到你脚边——一如此刻——只带着几分憎恶，仍旧恳求你把我做成你的妻子、你的奴仆，任何合你心意的东西。……哦，我原以为，爱情来临时该是甜美的！」

===Original===
Strangely quiet, in every sense, he answered:
“It is very sweet. I have known no happier moment in my life. For you stand within arm’s reach, mine to touch, mine to possess and do with as I elect. And I dare not lift a finger. I am as a man that has lain for a long while in a dungeon vainly hungering for the glad light of day﻿—who, being freed at last, must hide his eyes from the dear sunlight he dare not look upon as yet. Ho, I am past speech unworthy of your notice! and I pray you now speak harshly with me, madame, for when your pure eyes regard me kindly, and your bright and delicate lips have come thus near to mine, I am so greatly tempted and so happy that I fear lest heaven grow jealous!”
“Be not too much afraid﻿—” she murmured.
“Nay, should I then be bold? and within the moment wake Count Emmerick to say to him, very boldly, ‘Beau sire, the thief half Christendom is hunting has the honour to request your sister’s hand in marriage’?”

===Chinese===
他答了话，无论从哪种意义上说，都出奇地平静：
「甜美极了。我一生不曾有过比这更幸福的时刻。因为你就站在一臂之内，我触得着，也占有得着，尽可凭我的意思处置。而我竟不敢抬起一根指头。我就像一个人，在地牢里躺了许久，徒然渴慕着白昼的欣光——及至终于获释，却还得遮起双眼，躲开那他一时还不敢仰视的、亲爱的太阳。嚯，我不配蒙你垂顾，言语都道不尽！我求你现在对我说话狠些，夫人，因为当你纯洁的眼睛温存地看着我，你那明亮娇柔的唇这样临近我的唇，我受着莫大的诱惑，又这样幸福，直怕上天也要生妒！」
「不必这样害怕——」她低声道。
「不然，难道我就该放肆么？难道该这就去唤醒埃默里克伯爵，大着胆子对他说：『好爵爷，半个基督世界都在缉拿的窃贼，有幸向令妹求婚』？」

===Original===
“You sail tomorrow for the fighting oversea. Take me with you.”
“Indeed the feat would be worthy of me. For you are a lady tenderly nurtured and used to every luxury the age affords. There comes to woo you presently an excellent and potent monarch, not all unworthy of your love, who will presently share with you many happy and honourable years. Yonder is a lawless naked wilderness where I and my fellow desperadoes hope to cheat offended justice and to preserve thrice-forfeited lives in savagery. You bid me aid you to go into this country, never to return! Madame, if I obeyed you, Satan would protest against pollution of his ageless fires by any soul so filthy.”
“You talk of little things, whereas I think of great things. Love is not sustained by palatable food alone, and is not served only by those persons who go about the world in satin.”
“Then take the shameful truth. It is undeniable I swore I loved you, and with appropriate gestures, too. But, *dompnedex*, madame! I am past master in these specious ecstasies, for somehow I have rarely seen the woman who had not some charm or other to catch my heart with. I confess now that you alone have never quickened it. My only purpose was through hyperbole to wheedle you out of a horse, and meanwhile to have my recreation, you handsome jade!﻿—and that is all you ever meant to me. I swear to you that is all, all, all!” sobbed Perion, for it appeared that he must die. “I have amused myself with you, I have abominably tricked you﻿—”

===Chinese===
「明日你便要扬帆出海，去赴海外的战事。带我同去。」
「这桩壮举倒真配得上我。因为你是一位娇养长大的贵女，享惯了这个时代所能供给的种种奢华。眼下便有一位卓越而强大的君主要来向你求娶；他并非全然配不上你的爱，将与你共度许多幸福而有光彩的年月。而那边，是一片无法无天的赤裸荒野，我和我那些亡命的同伙，指望在那蛮荒之中欺瞒被触怒的正义，保全早已该死过三回的性命。你却吩咐我帮助你到那样的地方去，永不再回！夫人，我若依了你，只怕撒旦也要抗议，不许这样一个肮脏的灵魂，去玷污他那一炉万古不灭的火。」
「你说的是小事，我想的却是大事。爱情并不单靠甘美的食物来供养，也不只由那些遍身绸缎、周游世界的人来侍奉。」
「那就收下这可耻的真相吧。无可否认，我发过誓说我爱你，还配上一套相称的手势。可是*dompnedex*（主上帝啊），夫人！这类虚有其表的狂热，我是炉火纯青的宗师；因为不知怎么，我见过的女人，少有不是带着这一种或那一种妩媚，来俘获我心的。如今我招认：唯独你，从不曾令它悸动。我唯一的目的，是用浮夸的言辞从你那里哄一匹马，顺便也寻我的乐子，你这标致的婆娘！——你于我的意义，从来不过如此。我向你发誓，仅此而已，仅此，仅此！」佩里翁呜咽道，因为他眼看是非死不可的了。「我拿你取过乐，我可憎地欺骗过你——」

===Original===
Melicent only waited with untroubled eyes which seemed to plumb his heart and to appraise all which Perion had ever thought or longed for since the day that Perion was born; and she was as beautiful, it seemed to him, as the untroubled, gracious angels are, and more compassionate.
“Yes,” Perion said, “I am trying to lie to you. And even at lying I fail.”
She said, with a wonderful smile:
“Assuredly there were never any other persons so mad as we. For I must do the wooing, as though you were the maid, and all the while you rebuff me and suffer so that I fear to look on you. Men say you are no better than a highwayman; you confess yourself to be a thief: and I believe none of your accusers. Perion de la Forêt,” said Melicent, and ballad-makers have never shaped a phrase wherewith to tell you of her voice, “I know that you have dabbled in dishonour no more often than an archangel has pilfered drying linen from a hedgerow. I do not guess, for my hour is upon me, and inevitably I know! and there is nothing dares to come between us now.”

===Chinese===
梅莉森特只是静候着，一双澄澈无扰的眼睛仿佛直探他的心底，把佩里翁自出生那日起的一切所思所愿，一一估量；在他看来，她美得一如那些安宁慈和的天使，而且更怀悲悯。
「是啊，」佩里翁说，「我是在向你撒谎。可连撒谎，我也撒不成。」
她带着一个美妙的微笑，说道：
「千真万确，世上再没有第二对人，疯得像我们这样。因为求爱的竟须是我，仿佛你才是待字的闺秀；而你一路回绝我、受着煎熬，教我都不敢看你。人们说你不过是个剪径的强盗；你自己招认是窃贼：可你的控告者，我一个也不信。佩里翁·德·拉福雷，」梅莉森特说——歌谣的编撰人从来不曾炼出一个句子，足以向你描摹她的嗓音——「我知道，你沾染污行的次数，决不多过一位大天使从篱垣上偷窃晾晒的亚麻布。我不是在猜测，因为我的时辰已经临头，我是不可避免地知道了！如今再没有任何东西，胆敢来横在你我之间。」

===Original===
“Nay﻿—ho, and even were matters as you suppose them, without any warrant﻿—there is at least one silly stumbling knave that dares as much. Saith he: ‘What is the most precious thing in the world?﻿—Why, assuredly, Dame Melicent’s welfare. Let me get the keeping of it, then. For I have been entrusted with a host of common priceless things﻿—with youth and vigour and honour, with a clean conscience and a child’s faith, and so on﻿—and no person alive has squandered them more gallantly. So heartward ho! and trust me now, my timorous yoke-fellow, to win and squander also the chiefest jewel of the world.’ Eh, thus he chuckles and nudges me, with wicked whisperings. Indeed, madame, this rascal that shares equally in my least faculty is a most pitiful, ignoble rogue! and he has aforetime eked out our common livelihood by such practices as your unsullied imagination could scarcely depicture. Until I knew you I had endured him. But you have made of him a horror. A horror, a horror! a thing too pitiful for hell!”
Perion turned away from her, groaning. He flung himself into a chair. He screened his eyes as if before some physical abomination.
The girl kneeled close to him, touching him.
“My dear, my dear! then slay for me this other Perion of the Forest.”

===Chinese===
「不——嚯，就算事情真如你所料，全无凭据——至少还有一个蠢头蠢脑、磕磕绊绊的无赖，就敢横这个心。他说：『世上最珍贵的东西是什么？——唔，自然是梅莉森特夫人的福祉。那么，就让我把它的保管权弄到手吧。因为我素来受托保管过一大堆寻常的无价之物——青春、气力、名誉，清白的良心，孩童的信念，等等——而世上活着的人里，再没有谁把它们挥霍得比我更漂亮。所以呀，向心进发！我胆小的同轭伙计，如今信我一回，看我去把世间第一的珍宝也赢来挥霍掉。』呃，他便这样咯咯地笑，用胳膊肘捅我，附耳说着邪佞的言语。真的，夫人，这个连我最微末的官能都要平分一半的无赖，是个十足可怜、下贱的恶棍！他从前还靠种种行当贴补过我们共同的生计——那些行当，你那无瑕的想象力简直无从描画。认识你以前，我一直容忍着他。可你已把他变成了一个恐怖。一个恐怖，一个恐怖啊！一件连地狱都嫌太可怜的东西！」
佩里翁呻吟着，从她面前转过身去。他把自己摔进一把椅子里。他遮住双眼，仿佛眼前是什么有形的秽物。
那少女跪到他近旁，抚着她身侧的他。
「我亲爱的，我亲爱的！那就为我杀了这另一个林中佩里翁吧。」

===Original===
And Perion laughed, not very mirthfully.
“It is the common usage of women to ask of men this little labour, which is a harder task than ever Hercules, that mighty-muscled king of heathenry, achieved. Nay, I, for all my sinews, am an attested weakling. The craft of other men I do not fear, for I have encountered no formidable enemy save myself; but that same midnight stabber unhorsed me long ago. I had wallowed in the mire contentedly enough until you came.﻿ ﻿… Ah, child, child! why needed you to trouble me! for tonight I want to be clean as you are clean, and that I may not ever be. I am garrisoned with devils, I am the battered plaything of every vice, and I lack the strength, and it may be, even the will, to leave my mire. Always I have betrayed the stewardship of man and god alike that my body might escape a momentary discomfort! And loving you as I do, I cannot swear that in the outcome I would not betray you too, to this same end! I cannot swear﻿—Oh, now let Satan laugh, yet not unpitifully, since he and I, alone, know all the reasons why I may not swear! Hah, Madame Melicent!” cried Perion, in his great agony, “you offer me that gift an emperor might not accept save in awed gratitude; and I refuse it.” Gently he raised her to her feet. “And now, in God’s name, go, madame, and leave the prodigal among his husks.”
“You are a very brave and foolish gentleman,” she said, “who chooses to face his own achievements without any paltering. To every man, I think, that must be bitter work; to the woman who loves him it is impossible.”
Perion could not see her face, because he lay prone at the feet of Melicent, sobbing, but without any tears, and tasting very deeply of such grief and vain regret as, he had thought, they know in hell alone; and even after she had gone, in silence, he lay in this same posture for an exceedingly long while.

===Chinese===
佩里翁笑了，笑得并不怎么欢畅。
「求男人做这一点点劳役，原是女人们的通例；而这桩差事，却比异教里那位筋强骨健的君王赫拉克勒斯干成的任何一件都更难。不，我纵有一身筋骨，也是个有案可查的弱者。旁人的伎俩我不怕，因为我生平遭遇过的可畏敌人，唯有我自己；可正是那个午夜的刺客，早已把我挑落马下。在你来以前，我本可以在这泥沼里打滚，滚得相当心满意足。……啊，孩子，孩子！你又何苦来搅扰我！因为今夜我想变得像你一样洁净，而那是我永远做不到的。我躯壳里屯驻着成队的魔鬼，我是被每一种恶习轮番糟蹋的玩物；我没有那份力气，说不定，连离开这泥沼的心都没有。为了叫这肉身躲过一时的不适，我对人所托、对神所托，向来一概背叛！而我既然这样爱着你，便不能发誓说：到头来，为了同一个缘故，我不会连你也背叛！我不能发誓——哦，如今就让撒旦笑吧，只是别笑得全无怜悯，因为唯有他和我两个，知道我不能发誓的全部缘由！哈，梅莉森特夫人！」佩里翁在他极度的苦痛中喊道，「你献我的这件馈赠，帝王受了也要怀着敬畏的感激；而我拒绝它。」他温柔地扶她起身。「如今，看在上帝份上，去吧，夫人，把那浪子留在他的豆荚中间吧。」
「你是一位非常勇敢、也非常愚蠢的绅士，」她说，「你宁可直面自己一手做下的种种，毫不闪躲。我想，对任何男人，这都必是苦差；对爱着他的女人，更是万万不能。」
佩里翁看不见她的脸，因为他俯伏在梅莉森特脚边，呜咽着，却没有眼泪，深深尝着那种他原以为唯地狱里才有的悲恸与徒然的悔恨；她默然离去之后，他仍以这同一个姿势躺着，躺了许久许久。

===Original===
And after he knew not how long a while, Perion propped his chin between his hands and, still sprawling upon the rushes, stared hard into the little, crackling fire. He was thinking of a Perion de la Forêt that once had been. In him might have been found a fit mate for Melicent had this boy not died very long ago.
It is no more cheerful than any other mortuary employment, this disinterment of the person you have been, and are not any longer; and so did Perion find his cataloguing of irrevocable old follies and evasions.
Then Perion arose and looked for pen and ink. It was the first letter he ever wrote to Melicent, and, as you will presently learn, she never saw it.
In such terms Perion wrote:

===Chinese===
又过了不知多久，佩里翁双手支着下巴，仍旧摊手摊脚地趴在灯芯草铺上，死死盯着那一小簇噼啪作响的火。他想着那个曾经存在过的佩里翁·德·拉福雷。在那个人身上，本可以寻得配得上梅莉森特的良偶——倘若这少年不是很久以前便死了的话。
这桩发掘——把那个你曾经是、而如今不再是的人从土里刨出来——同一切殡葬的营生一样，没有半点叫人开怀的地方；佩里翁清点那些无可挽回的旧日愚行与推诿时，发觉正是如此。
于是佩里翁起身，寻来笔墨。这是他写给梅莉森特的第一封信；而如你稍后便会知道的，她从未见过它。
佩里翁是这样写的：

===Original===
> “**Madame**﻿—It may please you to remember that when Dame Mélusine and I were interrogated, I freely confessed to the murder of King Helmas and the theft of my dead master’s jewels. In that I lied. For it was my manifest duty to save the woman whom, as I thought, I loved, and it was apparent that the guilty person was either she or I.
> “She is now at Brunbelois, where, as I have heard, the splendour of her estate is tolerably notorious. I have not ever heard she gave a thought to me, her cat’s-paw. Madame, when I think of you and then of that sleek, smiling woman, I am appalled by my own folly. I am aghast by my long blindness as I write the words which no one will believe. To what avail do I deny a crime which every circumstance imputed to me and my own confession has publicly acknowledged?
> “But you, I think, will believe me. Look you, madame, I have nothing to gain of you. I shall not ever see you any more. I go into a perilous and an eternal banishment; and in the immediate neighbourhood of death a man finds little sustenance for romance. Take the worst of me: a gentleman I was born, and as a wastrel I have lived, and always very foolishly; but without dishonour. I have never to my knowledge﻿—and God judge me as I speak the truth!﻿—wronged any man or woman save myself. My dear, believe me! believe me, in spite of reason! and understand that my adoration and misery and unworthiness when I think of you are such as I cannot measure, and afford me no judicious moment wherein to fashion lies. For I shall not see you any more.
> “I thank you, madame, for your all-unmerited kindnesses, and, oh, I pray you to believe!”

===Chinese===
> 「**夫人**——或许你还乐意记起，当梅吕西娜夫人与我一同受审时，我坦然招认了谋杀赫尔马斯王（King Helmas）、盗取亡主珠宝之罪。在那件事上，我撒了谎。因为救下那个我当时自以为深爱着的女子，是我明白不过的本分，而有罪之人显而易见：不是她，便是我。
> 「她如今在布兰布卢瓦；据我所闻，她在那边家业的豪奢是颇有名声的。我却从未听说，她这个把我当猫爪子使的人，为我费过一分心思。夫人，每当我想起你，再想起那个皮光水滑、满面堆笑的女人，我便为自己的愚蠢骇然。我写下这些无人肯信的字句，为自己经年的盲目战栗。一桩罪行，样样情状都指向我，我自己的供状又已当众坐实——我再抵赖，又有何益？
> 「可是你，我想，会信我的。你瞧，夫人，我在你身上无利可图。我再不会与你相见了。我去赴一场凶险而永久的流放；而在死亡的左近，人寻不出多少滋养罗曼司的口粮。把我往最坏处看吧：我生为缙绅，活成了浪子，而且始终愚蠢得可以；却不曾失了名节。据我所知——愿上帝照我说真话的模样审判我！——我从不曾亏负过任何男人或女人，除了我自己。我亲爱的，信我吧！撇开情理，信我吧！也要懂得：我一想到你，我的崇慕、我的悲苦、我的不配，皆非我所能测度，不容我有半晌清醒的工夫去炮制谎言。因为我再不会与你相见了。
> 「我感谢你，夫人，谢你那些我全然不配承当的恩慈；哦，我求你相信！」

## XXVI How Men Ordered Matters / 二十六 男人们如何安排诸事

===Original===

Orestes came into the garden with Ahasuerus and nine other attendants. The master of Nacumera did not speak a syllable while his retainers seized Callistion, gagged her, and tied her hands with cords. They silently removed her. One among them bore on his shoulders the slim corpse of Diophantus, which was interred the same afternoon (with every appropriate ceremony) in company with that of his father. Orestes had the nicest sense of etiquette.

This series of swift deeds was performed with such a glib precipitancy that if was as though the action had been rehearsed a score of times. The garden was all drowsy peace now that Orestes spread his palms in a gesture of deprecation. A little distance from him, Ahasuerus with his forefinger drew upon the water’s surface designs which appeared to amuse the Jew.

“She would have killed you, Melicent,” Orestes said, “though all Olympos had marshalled in interdiction. That would have been irreligious. Moreover, by Hercules! I have not time to choose sides between snarling women. He who hunts with cats will catch mice. I aim more highly. And besides, by an incredible forced march, this Comte de la Forêt and all his Free Companions are battering at the gates of Nacumera﻿—”

===Chinese===

俄瑞斯忒斯带着亚哈随鲁和另外九名侍从走进园中。当他的随从们擒住卡利斯提翁、堵上她的嘴、用绳索捆住她双手时，这位纳库梅拉之主一言未发。他们悄无声息地把她带走了。其中一人肩头扛着狄奥凡托斯（Diophantus）那具瘦小的尸体，当天下午便（以一切合宜的仪节）与他父亲的遗体一同落葬。俄瑞斯忒斯对于礼数，讲究得无微不至。

这一连串迅捷的举动做得如此圆熟利落，倒仿佛整套行动已排演过二十来遍。此刻园中一片恹恹欲睡的安宁，俄瑞斯忒斯摊开双掌，作出一个告罪的手势。离他不远处，亚哈随鲁正用食指在水面勾画图形，这似乎颇能取悦这位犹太人。

「她本来是会杀了你的，梅莉森特，」俄瑞斯忒斯说，「哪怕全奥林波斯（Olympos）列阵出来禁阻。那便是亵渎神明了。况且，以赫拉克勒斯起誓！我可没闲工夫在两个龇牙咆哮的女人中间偏袒哪一边。同猫一道行猎的人，捉到的不过是老鼠。我的目标要高得多。再说，靠一场匪夷所思的强行军，这位拉福雷伯爵（Comte de la Forêt）率着他全部的自由佣兵，此刻正在猛击纳库梅拉的城门——」

===Original===

Hope blazed. “You know that were I harmed he would spare no one. Your troops are all at Calonak. Oh, God is very good!” said Melicent.

“I do not asperse the deities of any nation. It is unlucky. None the less, your desires outpace your reason. Grant that I had not more than fifty men to defend the garrison, yet Nacumera is impregnable except by starvation. We can sit snug a month. Meanwhile our main force is at Calonak, undoubtedly. Yet my infatuated father had already recalled these troops, in order that they might escort you into Messire de la Forêt’s camp. Now I shall use these knaves quite otherwise. They will arrive within two days, and to the rear of Messire de la Forêt, who is encamped before an impregnable fortress. To the front unscalable walls, and behind him, at a moderate computation, three swords to his one. All this in a valley from which Daedalos might possibly escape, but certainly no other man. I count this Perion of the Forest as already dead.”

It was a lumbering Orestes who proclaimed each step in his enchained deductions by the descent of a blunt forefinger upon the palm of his left hand. Demetrios had left a son but not an heir.

===Chinese===

希望熊熊燃起。「你知道，我若受到伤害，他谁也不会饶过。你的兵马全都在卡洛纳克。哦，上帝实在仁慈！」梅莉森特说。

「我不诽谤任何国度的神祇。那是不祥的。话虽如此，你的欲望跑到了你的理智前头。就算我守城的人手不过五十，纳库梅拉除困饿之外也无从攻破。我们大可安坐一个月。与此同时，我们的主力无疑远在卡洛纳克。然而我那位情迷心窍的父亲早已召回这些兵马，原是要他们护送你前往德·拉福雷爵爷的营中。如今我却要把这帮恶徒用在全然不同的去处。他们两日之内便到，直趋德·拉福雷爵爷的后背——他正扎营在一座无从攻破的要塞之前。面前是不可攀越的城墙，背后——保守估算——是三把对他一把的刀剑。而这一切，都摆在一道山谷里，代达罗斯（Daedalos）或许还逃得出去，旁人却是断然不能。我把这位林中佩里翁已经算作死人了。」

就是这样一副笨重迟缓的身量，俄瑞斯忒斯以一根钝圆的食指逐环叩落左掌，宣告出他那环环相扣的推论的每一步。德米特里奥斯留下了一个儿子，却没有留下一位继承人。

===Original===

Yet the chain held. Melicent tested every link and found each obdurate. She foresaw it all. Perion would be surrounded and overpowered. “And these troops come from Calonak because of me!”

“Things fall about with an odd patness, as you say. It should teach you not to talk about divinities lightly. Also, by this Jew’s advice, I mean to further the gods’ indisputable work. You will appear upon the walls of Nacumera at dawn tomorrow, in such a garb as you wore in your native country when the Comte de la Forêt first saw you. Ahasuerus estimates this Perion will not readily leave pursuit of you in that event, whatever his lieutenants urge, for you are very beautiful.”

Melicent cried aloud, “A bitter curse this beauty has been to me, and to all men who have desired it.”

“But I do not desire it,” said Orestes. “Else I would not have sold it to Ahasuerus. I desire only the governorship of some province on the frontier where I may fight daily with stalwart adversaries, and ride past the homes of conquered persons who hate me. Ahasuerus here assures me that the Emperor will not deny me such employment when I bring him the head of Messire de la Forêt. The raids of Messire de la Forêt have irreligiously annoyed our Emperor for a long while.”

===Chinese===

然而这条锁链牢不可断。梅莉森特检视了每一环，发觉环环坚固不移。这一切她全都预见了。佩里翁将被团团围住，被人多势众压倒。「而这些兵马从卡洛纳克赶来，全是因为我！」

「世事偏偏落得这样凑巧，正如你方才所言。这该教训你不要轻慢地谈论神明。再者，依着这犹太人的主意，我打算为诸神那桩不容置辩的功业再加一臂之力。明日拂晓，你将登上纳库梅拉的城头，穿着当初在故国、拉福雷伯爵初见你时的那身衣裳。亚哈随鲁估计，到那时无论他的副将们如何力劝，这位佩里翁都不会轻易撒开对你的追逐，因为你生得这样美。」

梅莉森特高声喊道：「这美貌于我是一道刻毒的诅咒，于一切贪求过它的男人，也是一道刻毒的诅咒。」

「可我并不贪求它，」俄瑞斯忒斯说，「否则我也不会把它卖给亚哈随鲁了。我贪求的只是边境上某一行省的总督之位，在那儿我可以日日同强壮的敌手交锋，可以骑马经过那些恨我的被征服者的家门。这里的亚哈随鲁向我担保，等我提着德·拉福雷爵爷的首级去见他，皇帝是不会拒绝赏我这样一份差事的。德·拉福雷爵爷的袭扰，长久以来一直大逆不道地搅扰着我们的皇帝。」

===Original===

She muttered, “Thou that once wore a woman’s body﻿—!”

“﻿—And I take Ahasuerus to be shrewd in all respects save one. For he desires trivialities. A wise man knows that woman are the sauce and not the meat of life; Ahasuerus, therefore, is not wise. And in consequence I do not lack a handsome bribe for this Bathyllos whom our good Emperor﻿—misguided man!﻿—is weak enough to love; my mother goes in chains; and I shall get my province.”

Here Orestes laughed. And then the master of Nacumera left Dame Melicent alone with Ahasuerus.

===Chinese===

她喃喃道：「你这曾穿过一具女人身体的——！」

「——而依我看，亚哈随鲁事事精明，只除了一桩。因为他贪求的是琐屑之物。明智的人懂得，女人是人生的佐酱，而非人生的肉食；因此亚哈随鲁算不得明智。而其结果是，我不愁拿不出一份可观的贿赂，送给那位巴蒂洛斯（Bathyllos）——我们的好皇帝，这个执迷不悟的人！——竟软弱得会疼爱他；我的母亲披枷戴锁；而我，将得到我的行省。」

说到这里，俄瑞斯忒斯放声大笑。随后，这位纳库梅拉之主便撇下梅莉森特夫人，任她独自与亚哈随鲁相对。

## XXII How Misery Held Nacumera / 二十二 苦难如何据有纳库梅拉

===Original===
Then one day Demetrios came to Melicent, and he was in a surly rage.
“Rogues all!” he grumbled. “Oh, I am wasted in this paltry age. Where are the giants and tyrants, and stalwart single-hearted champions of yesterday? Why, they are dead, and have become rotten bones. I will fight no longer. I will read legends instead, for life nowadays is no longer worthy of love or hatred.”
Melicent questioned him, and he told how his spies reported that the Cardinal de Montors could now not ever head an expedition against Demetrios’ territories. The Pope had died suddenly in the course of the preceding October, and it was necessary to name his successor. The College of Cardinals had reached no decision after three days’ balloting. Then, as is notorious, Dame Mélusine, as always hand in glove with Ayrart de Montors, held conference with the bishop who inspected the cardinals’ dinner before it was carried into the apartments where these prelates were imprisoned together until, in edifying seclusion from all worldly influences, they should have prayerfully selected the next Pope.

===Chinese===
一日，德米特里奥斯来到梅莉森特跟前，满怀一腔乖戾的狂怒。
「全是些无赖！」他咕哝道。「哦，我在这个鄙陋的时代里真是白白虚掷了。往日的巨人与暴君，还有那些刚勇磊落、心志专一的豪杰，如今都在哪里？唉，他们都死了，都化作朽骨了。我再也不厮杀了。我要改读传奇，因为如今的人生，已不配得到爱，也不配得到恨。」
梅莉森特问他缘故，他便讲起细作如何回报：德·蒙托尔枢机主教如今再也无法统率大军、征讨德米特里奥斯的领地了。教皇已在刚过去的十月里猝然驾崩，须得另立继任之人。枢机主教团投票三日，迄无定论。于是——此事尽人皆知——梅吕西娜夫人素来与艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔串通一气，这回便去同那位主教密商：枢机们的膳食，在送进幽禁众位教长的厅堂之前，例须经由此人先行查验。众位教长便这样被一同锁在厅堂之内，隔绝尘世的一切影响，在有益灵修的清静里祈祷拣选下一任教皇。

===Original===
The Cardinal of Genoa received on the fourth day a chicken stuffed with a deed to the palaces of Monticello and Soriano; the Cardinal of Parma a similarly dressed fowl which made him master of the bishop’s residence at Porto with its furniture and wine-cellar; while the Cardinals Orsino, Savelli, St. Angelo and Colonna were served with food of the same ingratiating sort. Such nourishment cured them of indecision, and Ayrart de Montors had presently ascended the papal throne under the title of Adrian VII, servant to the servants of God. His days of military captaincy were over. Demetrios deplored the loss of a formidable adversary, and jeered at the fact that the vicarship of heaven had been settled by six hens. But he particularly fretted over other news his spies had brought, which was the information that Perion had wedded Dame Mélusine, and had begotten two lusty children﻿—Bertram and a daughter called Blaniferte﻿—and now enjoyed the opulence and sovereignty of Brunbelois.
Demetrios told this unwillingly. He turned away his eyes in speaking, and doggedly affected to rearrange a cushion, so that he might not see the face of Melicent. She noted his action and was grateful.
Demetrios said, bitterly, “It is an old and tawdry history. He has forgotten you, Melicent, as a wise man will always put aside the dreams of his youth. To Cynara the Fates accord but a few years; a wanton Lyce laughs, cheats her adorers, and outlives the crow. There is an unintended moral here﻿—” Demetrios said, “Yet you do not forget.”
“I know nothing as to this Perion you tell me of. I only know the Perion I loved has not forgotten,” answered Melicent.
And Demetrios, evincing a twinge like that of gout, demanded her reasons. It was a May morning, very hot and still, and Demetrios sat with his Christian wife in the Court of Stars.

===Chinese===
第四日，热那亚（Genoa）的枢机主教收到一只鸡，腹中填着一纸地契，将蒙蒂塞洛（Monticello）与索里亚诺（Soriano）两处宫邸尽归其名下；帕尔马（Parma）的枢机主教所得的是一只同样填制的禽鸟，使他成了波尔托（Porto）主教府连同府中家具与酒窖的主人；至于奥尔西尼（Orsino）、萨维利（Savelli）、圣安杰洛（St. Angelo）与科隆纳（Colonna）诸位枢机，也被奉上了同样讨人欢心的吃食。这样的滋养治好了他们的优柔寡断，于是艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔旋即登上教皇御座，尊号阿德里安七世（Adrian VII），为上帝众仆之仆。他统领军旅的岁月就此告终。德米特里奥斯为痛失一位可畏的敌手而哀叹，又讥嘲天国的代理之位竟由六只母鸡议定。但尤其令他焦躁的，是细作们带来的另一桩消息：佩里翁已娶了梅吕西娜夫人为妻，生下两个健壮的孩子——贝特朗（Bertram），还有一个名叫布拉尼费尔特（Blaniferte）的女儿——如今安享着布兰布卢瓦的富庶与统治之权。
这番话，德米特里奥斯说得极不情愿。说话时他把眼光挪向别处，执拗地装作整理一张软垫，为的是不看梅莉森特的脸。她留意到他的举动，心怀感激。
德米特里奥斯语带辛酸地说：「这是一段陈旧而俗滥的旧事。他把你忘了，梅莉森特，正如智者总会撇开青春年代的旧梦。命运女神只肯给库娜拉（Cynara）寥寥数年；淫荡的吕刻（Lyce）笑着、诓着她的崇拜者，活得比乌鸦还久。这里面藏着一个并非有意的教训——」德米特里奥斯说，「然而你却不曾忘记。」
「你说的这个佩里翁，我一无所知。我只知道，我爱过的那个佩里翁不曾忘记。」梅莉森特答道。
德米特里奥斯像痛风发作似的一阵抽痛，追问她缘由何在。那是五月的一个早晨，天气燠热而沉静，德米特里奥斯与他那位基督徒妻子同坐在星辰宫中。

===Original===
Said Melicent, “It is not unlikely that the Perion men know today has forgotten me and the service which I joyed to render Perion. Let him who would understand the mystery of the Crucifixion first become a lover! I pray for old sake’s sake that Perion and his lady may taste of every prosperity. Indeed, I do not envy her. Rather I pity her, because last night I wandered through a certain forest hand-in-hand with a young Perion, whose excellencies she will never know as I know them in our own woods.”
Said Demetrios, “Do you console yourself with dreams?” The swart man grinned.
Melicent said:
“Now it is always twilight in these woods, and the light there is neither green nor gold, but both colours intermingled. It is like a friendly cloak for all who have been unhappy, even very long ago. Iseult is there, and Thisbe, too, and many others, and they are not severed from their lovers now﻿ ﻿… Sometimes Dame Venus passes, riding upon a panther, and low-hanging leaves clutch at her tender flesh. Then Perion and I peep from a coppice, and are very glad and a little frightened in the heart of our own woods.”
Said Demetrios, “Do you console yourself with madness?” He showed no sign of mirth.
Melicent said:

===Chinese===
梅莉森特道：「世人今日所知的那个佩里翁，多半已把我忘了，也把我曾欣然献与佩里翁的效劳忘了。谁想领会十字架受难的奥秘，先让他去做一个恋人吧！我念旧日情分，祈愿佩里翁与他的夫人尝尽种种顺遂。真的，我并不嫉妒她。我反倒可怜她，因为昨夜我同一个年轻的佩里翁携手漫游了一座森林，他的种种美好，她永远无从知晓；唯有我，在我们自己的林中，知道得清清楚楚。」
德米特里奥斯道：「你竟拿梦来自宽自解么？」这黝黑的汉子咧嘴一笑。
梅莉森特说：
「那片林中永远是薄暮时分，那里的光既非绿，亦非金，而是两色交融。它像一件友善的斗篷，披在一切曾经不幸的人身上，哪怕他们的不幸已是久远久远。伊瑟（Iseult）在那里，提斯柏（Thisbe）也在那里，还有许多别的女子，她们如今都不曾与爱人分离……有时维纳斯夫人（Dame Venus）骑着一头豹打这里经过，低垂的树叶勾扯着她柔嫩的肌肤。佩里翁和我便从一处矮树丛里窥望，在我们自己的林子深处，满心欢喜，又微微有些害怕。」
德米特里奥斯道：「你竟拿癫狂来自宽自解么？」他脸上不见一丝欢容。
梅莉森特说：

===Original===
“Ah, no, the Perion whom Mélusine possesses is but a man﻿—a very happy man, I pray of God and all His saints. I am the luckier, who may not ever lose the Perion that today is mine alone. And though I may not ever touch this younger Perion’s hands﻿—and their palms were as hard as leather in that dear time now overpast﻿—or see again his honest and courageous face, the most beautiful among all the faces of men and women I have ever seen, I do not grieve immeasurably, for nightly we walk hand-in-hand in our own woods.”
Demetrios said, “Ay; and then night passes, and dawn comes to light my face, which is the most hideous to you among all the faces of men and women!”
But Melicent said only:
“Seignior, although the severing daylight endures for a long while, I must be brave and worthy of Perion’s love﻿—nay, rather, of the love he gave me once. I may not grieve so long as no one else dares enter into our own woods.”
“Now go,” cried the proconsul, when she had done, and he had noted her soft, deep, devoted gaze at one who was not there; “now go before I slay you!” And this new Demetrios whom she then saw was featured like a devil in sore torment.

===Chinese===
「啊，不是的。梅吕西娜占有的那个佩里翁不过是个凡人——我祈求上帝与他的一切圣徒，愿他做个非常幸福的凡人。我才是更有福的一个，因为今日唯我独有的这个佩里翁，我永远也不会失去。虽然我再不能触碰这年轻佩里翁的双手——在那段业已逝去的亲爱时光里，那双手的掌心硬得如同皮革——再不能见到他那诚实而勇敢的脸，那是我所见过的世人男女的脸上最美的一张，我却并不悲伤得没有边际，因为每夜我们都携手同行，在我们自己的林子里。」
德米特里奥斯说：「是啊；随后黑夜过去，曙光降临，来照亮我的这张脸——在世人男女的一切脸孔之中，你眼里最可憎的便是它！」
梅莉森特却只说：
「大人，尽管这拆散人的白昼要持续许久，我也必须勇敢，必须无愧于佩里翁的爱——不，说得更确切些，无愧于他从前给我的那份爱。只要再没有旁人胆敢踏进我们自己的林子，我便不容自己悲伤。」
等她说完，总督也看清了她那柔和、深沉而一往情深的凝望，正落在一个并不在场的人身上；他喊道：「去吧，趁我还没杀你，快去！」她此刻所见的这个判若两人的德米特里奥斯，眉目狰狞，有如身受酷刑、苦苦煎熬的魔鬼。

===Original===
Wonderingly Melicent obeyed him.
Thought Melicent, who was too proud to show her anguish: “I could have borne aught else, but this I am too cowardly to bear without complaint. I am a very contemptible person. I ought to love this Mélusine, who no doubt loves her husband quite as much as I love him﻿—how could a woman do less?﻿—and yet I cannot love her. I can only weep that I, robbed of all joy, and with no children to bewail me, must travel very tediously toward death, a friendless person cursed by fate, while this Mélusine laughs with her children. She has two children, as Demetrios reports. I think the boy must be the more like Perion. I think she must be very happy when she lifts that boy into her lap.”
Thus Melicent; and her full-blooded husband was not much more lighthearted. He went away from Nacumera shortly, in a shaking rage which robbed him of his hands’ control, intent to kill and pillage, and, in fine, to make all other persons share his misery.

===Chinese===
梅莉森特满怀诧异地依从了他。
梅莉森特心中自忖——她太高傲，不肯显露自己的伤痛：「别的什么我都受得住，唯独这一桩，我太怯懦，受不住而不发一句怨言。我是个十分可鄙的人。我本该爱这位梅吕西娜，她无疑像我一样深爱着她的丈夫——一个女人又怎能爱得更少？——可我偏偏不能爱她。我只能哭泣：我被夺去了一切欢愉，又没有儿女来为我举哀，只落得做个被命运诅咒、无亲无友的人，万分乏味地一路跋涉，走向死亡，而这位梅吕西娜却同她的孩子们笑语欢声。照德米特里奥斯说来，她有两个孩子。我想，那男孩必定更像佩里翁。我想，她把那男孩抱上膝头的时候，一定幸福得很。」
梅莉森特如此自忖；她那位血气旺盛的丈夫，心绪也不见得轻快多少。他不久便离开了纳库梅拉，带着一身簌簌发抖、令双手失控的狂怒，一心只要杀戮与劫掠，总而言之，要让其余所有的人来分担他的苦难。

## XXIV How Orestes Ruled / 二十四 俄瑞斯忒斯如何统治
===Original===

Melicent returned into the Court of Stars; and as she entered, Orestes lifted one of the red cushions from Demetrios’ face. The eyes of Ahasuerus, who stood by negligently, were as expressionless as the eyes of a snake.

“The great proconsul laid an inconvenient mandate upon me,” said Orestes. “The great proconsul has been removed from us in order that his splendour may enhance the glories of Elysium.”

She saw that the young man had smothered his own father in the flesh as Demetrios lay helpless; and knew thereby that Orestes was indeed the son of Demetrios.

“Go,” this Orestes said thereafter; “go, and remember I am master here.”

===Chinese===

梅莉森特回到星辰宫；她进门时，俄瑞斯忒斯正从德米特里奥斯脸上掀起一只红色软垫。在一旁闲立的亚哈随鲁，那双眼睛恰似蛇眼，全无表情。

「那位大总督向我降过一道令人为难的训令，」俄瑞斯忒斯说道。「大总督如今已从我们中间移去，好教他的光辉为至福乐土（Elysium）的荣耀增色。」

她看明白了：趁着德米特里奥斯无力挣扎，这年轻人已亲手闷死了自己血肉所出的父亲；她由此知道，俄瑞斯忒斯确乎是德米特里奥斯的儿子。

「去吧，」随后这位俄瑞斯忒斯说道；「去吧，记住，我是这里的主人。」

===Original===

Said Melicent, “And by which door?” A little hope there was as yet.

But he, as half in shame, had pointed to the entrance of the Women’s Garden. “I have no enmity against you, outlander. Yet my mother desires to talk with you. Also there is some bargaining to be completed with Ahasuerus here.”

Then Melicent knew what had prompted the proconsul’s murder. It seemed unfair Callistion should hate her with such bitterness; yet Melicent remembered certain thoughts concerning Dame Mélusine, and did not wonder at Callistion’s mania half so much as did Callistion’s son.

“I must endure discomfort and, it may be, torture for a little longer,” said Melicent, and laughed wholeheartedly. “Oh, but today I find a cure for every ill,” said Melicent; and thereupon she left Orestes as a princess should.

===Chinese===

梅莉森特道：「那么，走哪一扇门呢？」此际她心里还存着一点希望。

而他却像是半含羞惭，指向了女眷园的入口。「我与你并无仇怨，外乡人。只是我母亲想同你谈谈。再者，与这里的亚哈随鲁，还有一桩买卖有待了结。」

于是梅莉森特明白了，是什么促成了这场对总督的谋害。卡利斯提翁恨她竟恨得这般刻毒，似乎有失公道；然而梅莉森特记起自己关于梅吕西娜夫人曾有过的某些念头，故而对于卡利斯提翁的疯魔，她的诧异还不及卡利斯提翁的儿子的一半。

「我还得再忍受一阵困苦，或许还有酷刑，」梅莉森特说，随即由衷地笑了。「哦，可就在今天，我找到了医治百病的灵药，」梅莉森特说；说罢，她便以一位公主应有的仪态，辞别了俄瑞斯忒斯。

===Original===

But first she knelt by that which yesterday had been her master.

“I have no word of praise or blame to give you in farewell. You were not admirable, Demetrios. But you depart upon a fearful journey, and in my heart there is just memory of the long years wherein according to your fashion you were kind to me. A bargain is a bargain. I sold with open eyes that which you purchased. I may not reproach you.”

Then Melicent lifted the dead face between her hands, as mothers caress their boys in questioning them.

“I would I had done this when you were living,” said Melicent, “because I understand now that you loved me in your fashion. And I pray that you may know I am the happiest woman in the world, because I think this knowledge would now gladden you. I go to slavery, Demetrios, where I was queen, I go to hardship, and it may be that I go to death.
