But I have learned this assuredly﻿—that love endures, that the strong knot which unites my heart and Perion’s heart can never be untied. Oh, living is a higher thing than you or I had dreamed! And I have in my heart just pity, poor Demetrios, for you who never found the love of which I must endeavour to be worthy. A curse was I to you unwillingly, as you﻿—I now believe﻿—have been to me against your will. So at the last I turn anew to bargaining, and cry﻿—in your deaf ears﻿—*Pardon for pardon, O Demetrios*!”

===Chinese===

但她先跪了下来，跪在昨日尚且是她主上的那具身躯旁。

「临别之际，我没有称许或责难的话要给你。你并不值得敬仰，德米特里奥斯。但你即将踏上一段可怖的旅程，而我心中，对这许多漫长的岁月存着一份公道的追念——在这些岁月里，你依着你自己的方式善待过我。买卖就是买卖。我睁着双眼，卖出了你所买下的东西。我无从怨责你。」

随后，梅莉森特双手捧起那张死者的脸，如同母亲诘问儿子时抚爱他们那样。

「但愿你活着时我便这样做了，」梅莉森特说，「因为我如今才明白，你曾以你自己的方式爱过我。我祈望你能知道，我是世上最幸福的女人，因为我想，这个消息此刻必能使你欢喜。我去为奴了，德米特里奥斯，在我曾做王后的地方；我去受苦，或许还去赴死。但我已确确实实地懂得了——爱是恒久的；系结着我的心与佩里翁的心的那个牢固的结，永远不会解开。哦，活着，是一件比你我所曾梦想的更高远的事！而我心中怀着一份公道的怜悯，可怜的德米特里奥斯，怜悯你从未寻得那份爱——那份我必须勉力去配得上的爱。我于你，是一场非出本心的诅咒；而你——我如今相信——于我，也曾是一场违背本意的诅咒。所以到最后，我重新做起买卖，向你那双已然聋聩的耳朵喊出——*宽恕换宽恕，哦，德米特里奥斯*！」

===Original===

Then Melicent kissed pitiable lips which would not ever sneer again, and, rising, passed into the Women’s Garden, proudly and unafraid.

Ahasuerus shrugged so patiently that she was half afraid. Then, as a cloud passes, she saw that all further buffetings would of necessity be trivial.

For Perion, as she now knew, was very near to her﻿—single of purpose, clean of hands, and filled with such a love as thrilled her with delicious fears of her own poor unworthiness.

===Chinese===

随后，梅莉森特吻了吻那再也不会讥诮的两片可怜的嘴唇，起身走进女眷园去，傲然而无所畏惧。

亚哈随鲁耸了耸肩，耸得那样隐忍，倒叫她几乎害怕起来。随后，仿佛一片云掠过，她看清了：往后的一切摧折，都必然是微不足道的了。

因为佩里翁——她如今知道了——已离她极近：他志念专一，双手洁净，心中怀着那样一种爱，令她因自觉可怜的卑微难配，而甜蜜地震颤不已。

## XIV How Perion Braved Theodoret / 十四 佩里翁如何勇抗泰奥多雷特

===Original===

The city of Megaris (then Theodoret’s capital) was ablaze with bonfires on the night that the Comte de la Forêt entered it at the head of his forces. Demetrios, meanly clothed, his hands tied behind him, trudged sullenly beside his conqueror’s horse. Yet of the two the gloomier face showed below the count’s coronet, for Perion did not relish the impendent interview with King Theodoret. They came thus amid much shouting to the Hôtel d’Ebelin, their assigned quarters, and slept there.
Next morning, about the hour of prime, two men-at-arms accompanied a fettered Demetrios into the presence of King Theodoret. Perion of the Forest preceded them. He pardonably swaggered, in spite of his underlying uneasiness, for this last feat, as he could not ignore, was a performance which Christendom united to applaud.
They came thus into a spacious chamber, very inadequately lighted. The walls were unhewn stone. There was but one window, of uncoloured glass; and it was guarded by iron bars. The floor was bare of rushes. On one side was a bed with tattered hangings of green, which were adorned with rampant lions worked in silver thread much tarnished; to the right hand stood a prie-dieu. Between these isolated articles of furniture, and behind an unpainted table sat, in a high-backed chair, a wizen and shabbily-clad old man. This was Theodoret, most pious and penurious of monarchs. In attendance upon him were Fra Battista, prior of the Grey Monks, and Melicent’s near kinsman, once the Bishop, now the Cardinal, de Montors, who, as was widely known, was the actual monarch of this realm. The latter was smartly habited as a cavalier and showed in nothing like a churchman.
The infirm King arose and came to meet the champion who had performed what many generals of Christendom had vainly striven to achieve. He embraced the conqueror of Demetrios as one does an equal.

===Chinese===

梅加里斯城——当时泰奥多雷特的都城——在拉福雷伯爵率军入城的那一夜，篝火熊熊，满城通明。德米特里奥斯衣衫寒素，双手反缚，闷闷地跋涉在他征服者的马旁。然而两人之中，更阴沉的一张脸却在伯爵冠之下，因为对于即将与泰奥多雷特王进行的那场陛见，佩里翁殊无喜色。就这样，他们在鼎沸的欢呼声中来到埃贝兰府（Hôtel d’Ebelin）——那是指拨给他们驻宿的地方——便在那里睡下了。
次日清晨，约莫晨祷时分，两名武装军士押着锁链加身的德米特里奥斯，来到泰奥多雷特王驾前；林中佩里翁走在他俩前头。他不免有几分昂然自得——尽管心底隐隐不安——因为这桩新立的功业，正如他无法无视的，是整个基督世界齐声喝彩的一场壮举。
他们就这样走进一间宽敞的厅堂，光线极为不足。四壁是未加凿治的毛石。窗户只有一扇，玻璃不曾着色，还装着铁条护栏。地面上没有铺灯心草。一侧是一张床，绿色的帐幔已然褴褛，上面用银线绣出的立狮，光泽早已黯败；右手边立着一张祈祷跪凳。在这两件彼此孤立的家具之间、一张未上漆的桌子后面，一张高背椅上坐着一个形容枯槁、衣衫寒碜的老者。这便是泰奥多雷特，列王之中最虔诚也最悭吝的一位。侍立在他身边的是灰袍修士会（Grey Monks）会长弗拉·巴蒂斯塔（Fra Battista），还有梅莉森特的近亲——旧日的主教、如今的枢机主教德·蒙托尔——众所周知，他才是这个王国真正的君主。后一位衣装鲜亮，一身骑士打扮，浑身上下没有一处像个教士。
那孱弱多病的君王站起身来，亲自迎接这位豪杰——基督世界多少将帅曾徒然力争的功业，被他一手成就。他拥抱这位德米特里奥斯的征服者，如同拥抱一位平辈。

===Original===

Said Theodoret:
“Hail, my fair friend! you who have lopped the right arm of heathenry! Today, I know, the saints hold festival in heaven. I cannot recompense you, since God alone is omnipotent. Yet ask now what you will, short of my crown, and it is yours.” The old man kissed the chief of all his treasures, a bit of the True Cross, which hung upon his breast supported by a chain of gold.
“The King has spoken,” Perion returned. “I ask the life of Demetrios.”
Theodoret recoiled, like a small flame which is fluttered by its kindler’s breath. He cackled thinly, saying:
“A jest or so is privileged in this high hour. Yet we ought not to make a jest of matters which concern the Church. Am I not right, Ayrart? Oh, no, this merciless Demetrios is assuredly that very Antichrist whose coming was foretold. I must relinquish him to Mother Church, in order that he may be equitably tried, and be baptised﻿—since even he may have a soul﻿—and afterward be burned in the marketplace.”

===Chinese===

泰奥多雷特说道：
「万福，我俊美的朋友！你这斩下了异教右臂的人！我知道，今日众圣徒正在天上庆节。我无从酬报你，因为唯独上帝是全能的。然而现在，除了我的王冠，你要什么便开口，一切都归你。」老人吻了吻他万般珍藏的头一件——真十字架上的一片圣木——它由一条金链托着，悬在他胸前。
「王言已出，」佩里翁答道，「我只求德米特里奥斯的性命。」
泰奥多雷特往后一缩，像一朵被点灯人的气息吹得摇曳的小火苗。他嗬嗬地发出一阵尖细的笑，说道：
「在这崇高的时辰，赏一两句戏言原是恩许的。然而关系到教会的事，我们不该拿来戏言。艾亚尔，我说得不对么？哦，不，这个毫无怜悯的德米特里奥斯，千真万确就是预言要来的那位敌基督。我必须把他移交给慈母教会，好让他受一场公允的审判，领一次洗礼——因为就连他也可能有个灵魂——随后到市集上被烧死。」

===Original===

“The King has spoken,” Perion replied. “I too have spoken.”
There was a pause of horror upon the part of King Theodoret. He was at first in a mere whirl. Theodoret said:
“You ask, in earnest, for the life of this Demetrios, this arch-foe of our Redeemer, this spawn of Satan, who has sacked more of my towns than I have fingers on this wasted hand! Now, now that God has singularly favoured me﻿—!” Theodoret snarled and gibbered like a frenzied ape, and had no longer the ability to articulate.
“Beau sire, I fought the man because he infamously held Dame Melicent, whom I serve in this world without any reservation, and trust to serve in Paradise. His person, and this alone, will ransom Melicent.”

===Chinese===

「王言已出，」佩里翁回道，「我言也已出。」
泰奥多雷特王那边先起了一阵骇然，一时哑然。起初他只觉一片天旋地转。泰奥多雷特说：
「你当真要这个德米特里奥斯的性命——我们救主的大仇敌、撒旦的孽种——他洗劫我的城镇，比我这只枯手上的指头还多！如今，如今上帝既这般格外眷顾了我——！」泰奥多雷特嗥叫着、呓语着，像一只发狂的猿猴，再没有能力把话说明白了。
「好爵爷，我与这人交兵，只因他卑污地羁留着梅莉森特夫人——我在今生侍奉她，毫无保留，还指望到天堂再侍奉她。能赎回梅莉森特的，是他的性命，唯有这一样。」

===Original===

“You plan to loose this fiend!” the old King cried. “To stir up all this butchery again!”
“Sire, pray recall how long I have loved Melicent. Reflect that if you slay Demetrios, Dame Melicent will be left destitute in heathenry. Remember that she will be murdered through the hatred of this man’s other wives whom her inestimable beauty has supplanted.” Thus Perion entreated.
All this while the cardinal and the proconsul had been appraising each other. It was as though they two had been the only persons in the dimly-lit apartment. They had not met before. “Here is my match,” thought each of these two; “here, if the world affords it, is my peer in cunning and bravery.”
And each lusted for a contest, and with something of mutual comprehension.
In consequence they stinted pity for Theodoret, who unfeignedly believed that whether he kept or broke his recent oath damnation was inevitable. “You have been ill-advised﻿—” he stammered. “I do not dare release Demetrios﻿—My soul would answer that enormity﻿—But it was sworn upon the Cross﻿—Oh, ruin either way! Come now, my gallant captain,” the King barked. “I have gold, lands, and jewels﻿—”

===Chinese===

「你要放走这个凶魔！」老王喊道。「要把这满场的屠戮重新搅起来！」
「陛下，请回想我爱上梅莉森特已有多少年头。请细想：您若杀了德米特里奥斯，梅莉森特夫人便要孤零无依地沦落在异教之中。请记取：她终将遭此人的其他妻妾毒手——她那无可估量的美貌已将她们取而代之。」佩里翁如此恳求着。
这一边唇舌往来的当口，枢机主教与总督一直在彼此估量。仿佛在这间光线昏暗的屋子里，在场的只有他们两个。他们从前不曾会过面。「这是我的对手，」两人各自想道；「这人——倘若世上寻得出——便是我在狡黠与勇武上的匹敌。」
而两人都渴慕着一场较量，且彼此怀着几分心照不宣。
于是他们对泰奥多雷特的怜悯便都吝惜起来——这位君王可是真心相信：新近许下的那道誓言，无论守与不守，沉沦都无可幸免。「你这是失策了——」他讷讷地说道。「我不敢放了德米特里奥斯——我的灵魂要为那桩滔天罪愆担罪——可誓是在十字架前立的——哦，横竖都是毁亡！来吧，我豪勇的统领，」国王厉声喝道，「我有黄金、土地、珠宝——」

===Original===

“Beau sire, I have loved this my dearest lady since the time when both of us were little more than children, and each day of the year my love for her has been doubled. What would it avail me to live in however lofty estate when I cannot daily see the treasure of my life?”
Now the Cardinal de Montors interrupted, and his voice was to the ear as silk is to the fingers.
“Beau sire,” said Ayrart de Montors, “I speak in all appropriate respect. But you have sworn an oath which no man living may presume to violate.”
“Oh, true, Ayrart!” the fluttered King assented. “This blusterer holds me as in a vise.” He turned to Perion again, fierce, tense and fragile, like an angered cat. “Choose now! I will make you the wealthiest person in my realm﻿—My son, I warn you that since Adam’s time women have been the devil’s peculiar bait. See now, I am not angry. Heh, I remember, too, how beautiful she was. I was once tempted much as you are tempted. So I pardon you. I will give you my daughter Ermengarde in marriage, I will make you my heir, I will give you half my kingdom﻿—” His voice rose, quavering; and it died now, for he foreread the damnation of Theodoret’s soul while he fawned before this impassive Perion.
“Since Love has taken up his abode within my heart,” said Perion, “there has not ever been a vacancy therein for any other thought. How may I help it if Love recompenses my hospitality by afflicting me with a desire which can neither subdue the world nor be subdued by it?”

===Chinese===

「好爵爷，我爱这位我最亲爱的夫人，从我们两人还近乎孩童的年纪就开始了；一年之中的每一天，我对她的爱都要翻上一倍。倘若不能日日见到我生命中的珍藏，任凭身处何等尊荣的高位，于我又有什么益处？」
这时蒙托尔枢机主教插了进来，他的声音入耳，犹如丝绸入手。
「好爵爷，」艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔说，「我此言怀着全然的敬意。只是您已立下誓言，普天之下没有一个活人胆敢妄自违犯。」
「哦，正是，艾亚尔！」这位心慌意乱的君王连声附和。「这个咆哮的家伙把我夹进了虎钳。」他又转向佩里翁，凶悍、紧绷而脆弱，像一只被激怒的猫。「现在选吧！我要使你成为我王国里最富有的人——我的孩子，我警告你，自亚当的时代起，女人便是魔鬼特设的钓饵。你看，我并不动怒。嘿，我也还记得她是何等美丽。我从前也受过诱惑，同你眼下受的十分相像。所以我饶恕你。我把我的女儿埃尔门加德（Ermengarde）许配给你，我立你做我的继承人，我把半个王国给你——」他的声音越拔越高，颤颤巍巍；此刻却哑了下去，因为当他在这个不动声色的佩里翁面前卑辞承欢时，他已预先读出：泰奥多雷特的灵魂必要沉沦。
「自从爱神在我心中安下居所，」佩里翁说，「那里便再没有空位留给任何别的念头。爱神报答我的款待，反倒降我以这样一种欲望——它既不能降服世界，也不能被世界降服——教我有什么法子呢？」

===Original===

Theodoret continued like the rustle of dead leaves:
“﻿—Else I must keep my oath. In that event you may depart with this unbeliever. I will accord you twenty-four hours wherein to accomplish this. But, oh, if I lay hands upon either of you within the twenty-fifth hour I will not kill my prisoner at once. For first I must devise unheard-of torments﻿—”
The King’s face was not agreeable to look upon.
Yet Perion encountered it with an untroubled gaze until Battista spoke, saying:
“I promise worse. The Book will be cast down, the bells be tolled, and all the candles snuffed﻿—ah, very soon!” Battista licked his lips, gingerly, just as a cat does.
Then Perion was moved, since excommunication is more terrible than death to any of the Church’s loyal children, and he was now more frightened than the King. And so Perion thought of Melicent a while before he spoke.

===Chinese===

泰奥多雷特又说了下去，声音如同枯叶的窸窣：
「——不然，我就必须守住我的誓言。当真如此，你便可带着这个不信之徒离开。我准给你二十四个钟头去办妥此事。可是，哦，倘若我在第二十五个钟头之内把你们两人中无论哪一个拿到手上，我不会立刻杀死我的囚徒。因为我还得先设计出些闻所未闻的酷刑——」
国王的那张脸，实在不宜细看。
佩里翁却以毫不纷扰的目光迎着那张脸，直到巴蒂斯塔开口说道：
「我担保还有更糟的。圣书要被掀翻在地，丧钟要被敲响，所有的蜡烛要被掐灭——啊，就在不久之后！」巴蒂斯塔舔了舔嘴唇，小心翼翼地，正像一只猫那样。
这时佩里翁动摇了，因为对教会任何一个忠实的儿女来说，绝罚比死亡更可怕；此刻他比国王还要惶恐。于是佩里翁先默想了一阵梅莉森特，方才开口。

===Original===

Said Perion:
“I choose. I choose hell fire in place of riches and honour, and I demand the freedom of Demetrios.”
“Go!” the King said. “Go hence, blasphemer. Hah, you will weep for this in hell. I pray that I may hear you then, and laugh as I do now﻿—”
He went away, and was followed by Battista, who whispered of a makeshift. The cardinal remained and saw to it that the chains were taken from Demetrios.

===Chinese===

佩里翁说道：
「我选。我选地狱之火，以代富贵与荣名；我要求给予德米特里奥斯自由。」
「去！」国王说。「去吧，渎神的人。哈，你在地狱里要为这事痛哭的。我祈求到那时能听见你，还像我现在这样发笑——」
他走了，巴蒂斯塔跟在后头，一路低语着某个权宜之计。枢机主教留了下来，着人把德米特里奥斯身上的锁链卸去。

===Original===

“In consequence of Messire de la Forêt’s﻿—as I must term it﻿—most unchristian decision,” said the cardinal, “it is not impossible, Messire the Proconsul, that I may head the next assault upon your territory﻿—”
Demetrios laughed. He said:
“I dare to promise your Eminence that reception you would most enjoy.”
“I had hoped for as much,” the cardinal returned; and he too laughed. To do him justice, he did not know of Battista’s makeshift.

===Chinese===

「由于德·拉福雷爵爷——恕我只能这样称呼——这一极不基督徒的决定，」枢机主教说，「总督爵爷，下一次进攻您的领地，领军的未必不是我——」
德米特里奥斯笑了。他说：
「我敢向枢机大人担保，必为您备下您最乐于消受的那等接待。」
「我正巴不得如此呢，」枢机主教回道；他也笑了。平心而论，巴蒂斯塔的那个权宜之计，他并不知道。

---

===Original===

The cardinal remained when they had gone. Seated in a king’s chair, Ayrart de Montors meditated rather wistfully upon that old time when he, also, had loved Melicent wholeheartedly. It seemed a great while ago, made him aware of his maturity.
He had put love out of his life, in common with all other weaknesses which might conceivably hinder the advancement of Ayrart de Montors. In consequence, he had climbed far. He was not dissatisfied. It was a man’s business to make his way in the world, and he had done this.
“My cousin is a brave girl, though,” he said aloud, “I must certainly do what I can to effect her rescue as soon as it is convenient to send another expedition against Demetrios.”
Then the cardinal set about concoction of a moving sonnet in praise of Monna Vittoria de’ Pazzi. Desperation loaned him extraordinary eloquence (as he complacently reflected) in addressing this obdurate woman, who had held out against his lovemaking for six weeks now.

===Chinese===

他们走后，枢机主教还留在那里。艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔坐在一张国王的座椅里，微带怅惘地追想那段旧日时光——那时节，他也曾一心一意爱着梅莉森特。那似乎已是许久许久以前的事了，令他觉出了自己的老成。
他已把爱情逐出了自己的生活，连同一切或许会妨碍艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔高升的弱点，一概逐了出去。其结果，他爬得很高。他并无不满。在世上挣得前程，本是男儿的事业，而他已经成了。
「不过，我这位表妹是个勇敢的姑娘，」他出声说道，「一旦方便再派一支远征军去讨伐德米特里奥斯，我务必要尽我所能，促成对她的营救。」
随后，枢机主教着手炮制一首动人的十四行诗，赞颂莫娜·维托里亚·德·帕齐（Monna Vittoria de’ Pazzi）。绝望借给了他非凡的辩才（他颇为自得地这样想着），去攻克这位顽固的女人——对他的求爱，她负隅顽抗至今，已有六个星期了。

## XV How Perion Fought / 十五 佩里翁如何奋战

===Original===

Demetrios and Perion, by the quick turn of fortune previously recorded, were allied against all Christendom. They got arms at the Hôtel d’Ebelin, and they rode out of the city of Megaris, where the bonfires lighted overnight in Perion’s honour were still smouldering, amid loud execrations. Fra Battista had not delayed to spread the news of King Theodoret’s dilemma. The burghers yelled menaces; but, knowing that an endeavour to constrain the passage of these champions would prove unwholesome for at least a dozen of the arrestors, they cannily confined their malice to a vocal demonstration.

Demetrios rode unhelmeted, intending that these snarling little people of Megaris should plainly see the man whom they most feared and hated.

It was Perion who spoke first. They had passed the city walls, and had mounted the hill which leads toward the Forest of Sannazaro. Their road lay through a rocky pass above which the leaves of spring were like sparse traceries on a blue cupola, for April had not come as yet.

“I meant,” said Perion, “to hold you as the ransom of Dame Melicent. I fear that is impossible. I, who am a landless man, have neither servitors nor any castle wherein to retain you as a prisoner. I earnestly desire to kill you, forthwith, in single combat; but when your son Orestes knows that you are dead he will, so you report, kill Melicent. And yet it may be you are lying.”

===Chinese===

德米特里奥斯与佩里翁，因前文所记的命运骤转，结成了同盟，与整个基督教世界为敌。他们在埃贝兰府（Hôtel d’Ebelin）取了兵甲，便驰出梅加里斯城；城中昨夜为礼敬佩里翁而燃起的篝火犹自闷燃，咒骂之声四起。弗拉·巴蒂斯塔（Fra Battista）毫不耽搁，早把泰奥多雷特王进退维谷的消息散布开来。市民们高声叫嚷着威吓；但他们心知，若想强行拦阻这两位豪杰的去路，至少十几个动手之人准讨不了好，于是乖觉地只把怨毒发为一场口舌喧嚣。

德米特里奥斯骑行不戴头盔，有意让梅加里斯这些嗥嗥狂吠的小民，把这位他们最畏惧、最憎恨之人看得清清楚楚。

先开口的是佩里翁。此刻他们已过城墙，登上了通往萨纳扎罗森林的山丘。道路穿过一道岩石嶙峋的隘口，头顶春叶疏疏落落，宛如蓝色穹顶上稀疏的镂花格，因为四月尚未来临。

「我原打算，」佩里翁说，「把你留作赎金，好换回梅莉森特夫人。如今我怕这是办不到了。我是个没有领地的人，既无仆从，又无一堡一寨可以囚你为质。我衷心渴望即刻与你单打独斗，取你性命；可是照你的说法，你儿子俄瑞斯忒斯一旦得知你死，便会杀了梅莉森特。然而，你也可能在撒谎。」

===Original===

Perion was of a tall imperious person, and accustomed to command. He had black hair, grey eyes which challenged you, and a thin pleasant face which was not pleasant now.

“You know that I am not a coward﻿—.” Demetrios began.

“Indeed,” said Perion, “I believe you to be the hardiest warrior in the world.”

“Therefore I may without dishonour repeat to you that my death involves the death of Melicent. Orestes hates her for his mother’s sake. I think, now we have fought so often, that each of us knows I do not fear death. I grant I had Flamberge to wield, a magic weapon﻿—” Demetrios shook himself, like a dog coming from the water, for to consider an extraneous invincibility was nauseous. “However! I who am Demetrios protest I will not fight with you, that I will accept any insult rather than risk my life in any quarrel extant, because I know the moment that Orestes has made certain I am no longer to be feared he will take vengeance on Dame Melicent.”

===Chinese===

佩里翁身量颀长、气概威严，惯于发号施令。他生着黑发，一双灰眼睛咄咄逼人，一张清瘦的宜人面孔——此刻却无半点宜人可言。

「你知道我不是懦夫——」德米特里奥斯开口道。

「的确，」佩里翁说，「我相信你是天下最刚猛的战士。」

「因此我不妨全无损于名誉地向你再说一遍：我一死，梅莉森特便也难活。俄瑞斯忒斯为他母亲的缘故恨她。我想，我们既已交手过这么多次，彼此都知道我并不怕死。我承认，我昔日挥使的弗朗贝尔热乃是一件魔法兵器——」德米特里奥斯猛然抖了抖身子，像一条从水里出来的狗，因为去想一件身外的无敌之物，令他作呕。「可是！我，德米特里奥斯，在此申言：我不与你交手；我宁受任何侮辱，也决不肯为眼前任何一场争端拿性命冒险，因为我知道，俄瑞斯忒斯一确知我再不足惧，便会向梅莉森特夫人复仇。」

===Original===

“Prove this!” said Perion, and with deliberation he struck Demetrios. Full in the face he struck the swart proconsul, and in the ensuing silence you could hear a feeble breeze that strayed about the treetops, but you could hear nothing else. And Perion, strong man, the willing scourge of heathendom, had half a mind to weep.

Demetrios had not moved a finger. It was appalling. The proconsul’s countenance had throughout the hue of wood-ashes, but his fixed eyes were like blown embers.

“I believe that it is proved,” said Demetrios, “since both of us are still alive.” He whispered this.

“In fact the thing is settled,” Perion agreed. “I know that nothing save your love for Melicent could possibly induce you to decline a proffered battle. When Demetrios enacts the poltroon I am the most hasty of all men living to assert that the excellency of his reason is indisputable. Let us get on! I have only 500 sequins, but this will be enough to buy your passage back to Quesiton. And inasmuch as we are near the coast﻿—”

===Chinese===

「那就证明这一点啊！」佩里翁说罢，从容不迫地动手打了德米特里奥斯。一拳正中面门，打在这位黝黑的总督脸上；随之而起的寂静中，你听得见一缕微弱的清风在树梢间游荡，此外便什么也听不见了。而佩里翁这个刚强的男子、自愿充任异教世界之鞭的人，竟有一半的心思想要恸哭。

德米特里奥斯连一根手指也不曾动弹。这真骇人。这位总督的脸色自始至终都是柴灰的颜色，唯有那双凝定不动的眼睛，像吹旺了的炭烬。

「我相信，这已经证明了，」德米特里奥斯说，「既然我们两个都还活着。」这话他是低声说的。

「其实，事情就这么定了，」佩里翁深以为然。「我知道，除了你对梅莉森特的爱，再没有什么能叫你拒却一场送上来的决斗。每逢德米特里奥斯扮起懦夫，普天之下数我最心急，急着承认他那道理的高明无可置辩。我们上路吧！我只有五百塞奎因，但足够买你回凯西通的船资。况且我们离海岸已不远——」

===Original===

“I think some others mean to have a spoon in that broth,” Demetrios returned. “For look, *messire*!” Perion saw that far beneath them a company of retainers in white and purple were spurring up the hill. “It is Duke Sigurd’s livery,” said Perion.

Demetrios forthwith interpreted and was amused by their common ruin. He said, grinning:

“Pious Theodoret has sworn a truce of twenty-four hours, and in consequence might not send any of his own lackeys after us. But there was nothing to prevent the dropping of a hint into the ear of his brother in-law, because you servitors of Christ excel in these distinctions.”

“This is hardly an opportunity for theological debate,” Perion considered. “And for the rest, time presses. It is your instant business to escape.” He gave his tiny bag of gold to his chief enemy. “Make for Narenta. It is a free city and unfriendly to Theodoret. If I survive I will come presently and fight with you for Melicent.”

===Chinese===

「我看还有旁人想在这锅汤里也插上一柄勺子，」德米特里奥斯回道。「你瞧啊，爵爷！」佩里翁望见远远的下方，一队身着白紫两色号衣的家臣正策马奔上山来。「那是西格德公爵（Duke Sigurd）的号衣。」佩里翁说。

德米特里奥斯当即道破了其中缘故，并为他们共同的覆灭大感有趣。他咧嘴笑道：

「虔敬的泰奥多雷特已立誓休战二十四小时，因此不得派自家的任何仆从来追我们。可要把一句风声漏进他内兄的耳朵里，却没有什么拦得住——因为你们这些基督的仆役，最擅长的正是这类精细的分辨。」

「眼下实在不是作神学辩论的机会，」佩里翁思忖道。「再者，时辰紧迫。你的当务之急是逃命。」他把那只小小的金袋交到他头号仇敌手里。「往纳伦塔去。那是一座自由城，又与泰奥多雷特不睦。我若不死，稍顷便来寻你，为梅莉森特与你再决一战。」

===Original===

“I shall do nothing of the sort,” Demetrios equably returned. “Am I the person to permit the man whom I most hate﻿—you who have struck me and yet live!﻿—to fight alone against some twenty adversaries! Oh, no, I shall remain, since after all, there are only twenty.”

“I was mistaken in you,” Perion replied, “for I had thought you loved Dame Melicent as I do. I find too late that you would estimate your private honour as set against her welfare.”

The two men looked upon each other. Long and long they looked, and the heart of each was elated. “I comprehend,” Demetrios said. He clapped spurs to his horse and fled as a coward would have fled. This was one occasion in his life when he overcame his pride, and should in consequence be noted.

The heart of Perion was glad.

“Oh, but at times,” said Perion, “I wish that I might honourably love this infamous and lustful pagan.”

===Chinese===

「这种事我才不干，」德米特里奥斯心平气和地回道。「我是那种人么——竟容我最恨之人——你这个打了我居然还活着的家伙！——独自一人迎战大约二十个敌手！哦，不，我要留下，毕竟拢共也不过二十个人。」

「我看错你了，」佩里翁答道，「因为我原以为你同我一样爱着梅莉森特夫人。如今发现得太迟：你竟会把一己的名誉，摆到她福祉的对立面去掂量。」

两人彼此对望。望了许久许久，两颗心俱自昂扬。「我明白了。」德米特里奥斯说。他一磕马刺，像个懦夫会逃的那样逃走了。这是他生平一次制服了自己骄傲的场合，因此理应记上一笔。

佩里翁的心是欢畅的。

「啊，可有些时候，」佩里翁说，「我真希望自己能够名正言顺地爱这个声名狼藉、贪淫好色的异教徒。」

===Original===

Afterward Perion wheeled and met Duke Sigurd’s men. Then like a reaper cutting a field of wheat Sire Perion showed the sun his sword and went about his work, not without harvesting.

In that narrow way nothing could be heard but the striking of blows on armour and the clash of swords which bit at one another. The Comte de la Forêt, for once, allowed himself the privilege of fighting in anger. He went without a word toward this hopeless encounter, as a drunkard to his bottle. First Perion killed Ruggiero of the Lamberti and after that Perion raged as a wolf harrying sheep. Six other stalwart men he cut down, like a dumb maniac among tapestries. His horse was slain and lay blocking the road, making a barrier behind which Perion fought. Then Perion encountered Giacomo di Forio, and while the two contended Gulio the Red very warily cast his sword like a spear so that it penetrated Perion’s left shoulder and drew much blood. This hampered the lone champion. Marzio threw a stone which struck on Perion’s crest and broke the fastenings of Perion’s helmet. Instantly Giacomo gave him three wounds, and Perion stumbled, the sunlight glossing his hair. He fell and they took him. They robbed the corpses of their surcoats, which they tore in strips. They made ropes of this bloodied finery, and with these ropes they bound Perion of the Forest, whom twenty men had conquered at last.

He laughed feebly, like a person bedrugged; but in the midst of this superfluous defiance Perion swooned because of many injuries. He knew that with fair luck Demetrios had a sufficient start. The heart of Perion exulted, thinking that Melicent was saved.

It was the happier for him he was not ever destined to comprehend the standards of Demetrios.

===Chinese===

随后佩里翁拨转马头，迎击西格德公爵的人马。于是佩里翁爵爷便像刈麦人收割一片麦田，向太阳亮出他的剑，着手办起他的活计——而且并非颗粒无收。

在那条狭窄的隘道上，除了兵刃击打甲胄的声响、彼此噬咬的剑锋相错的铿锵，什么也听不见。拉福雷伯爵破天荒头一遭，容许自己享有怀着怒气作战的特权。他一言不发地走向这场无望的遭遇，如同酒徒走向他的酒瓶。佩里翁先杀了兰贝蒂家的鲁杰罗（Ruggiero of the Lamberti），随后便狂怒如一头蹂躏羊群的狼。他又砍翻六条健壮的汉子，像哑口的狂人毁坏满堂绣帷。他的战马被杀，横尸堵住去路，成了一道屏障，佩里翁便在屏障之后接战。接着佩里翁遇上贾科莫·迪·福里奥（Giacomo di Forio），两人缠斗之际，红衣古利奥（Gulio the Red）极审慎地将剑当长枪掷出，穿透了佩里翁的左肩，血流如注。这便妨碍了这位孤胆的豪杰。马尔齐奥（Marzio）掷出一块石头，正打在佩里翁的盔顶上，砸断了头盔的系带。贾科莫立时又给他添了三处创伤，佩里翁一个踉跄，阳光给他发上抹了一层光。他倒下了，他们便擒住了他。他们剥下尸首上的罩袍，撕成一条条，用这血污的华服搓成绳索，便用这些绳索捆缚住林中佩里翁——二十个人到底把他征服了。

他微弱地笑了，像服了迷药的人；然而就在这番多余的倔强之中，佩里翁因遍体创伤昏厥过去。他知道，只要运气不太坏，德米特里奥斯已争取到足够的先程。佩里翁的心在欢腾：梅莉森特得救了。

他命中注定永远无从领会德米特里奥斯的准则，这于他反倒更为幸福。

## XX How Perion Got Aid / 二十 佩里翁如何得到援手

===Original===

Then Perion turned from the Needle of Assignano, and went westward into the Forest of Columbiers. He had no plan. He wandered in the high woods that had never yet been felled or ordered, as a beast does in watchful care of hunters.

He came presently to a glade which the sunlight flooded without obstruction. There was in this place a fountain, which oozed from under an iron-coloured boulder incrusted with grey lichens and green moss. Upon the rock a woman sat, her chin propped by one hand, and she appeared to consider remote and pleasant happenings. She was clothed throughout in white, with metal bands about her neck and arms; and her loosened hair, which was coloured like straw, and was as pale as the hair of children, glittered about her, and shone frostily where it lay outspread upon the rock behind her.

She turned toward Perion without any haste or surprise, and Perion saw that this woman was Dame Mélusine, whom he had loved to his own hurt (as you have heard) when Perion served King Helmas. She did not speak for a long while, but she lazily considered Perion’s honest face in a sort of whimsical regret for the adoration she no longer found there.

===Chinese===

于是佩里翁离开安西尼亚诺之针，向西走进了科隆比耶森林。他心里并无定算。他在那片从未经砍伐、从未经整饬的高林间游荡，像一头时时提防着猎人的兽。

不多时，他来到一片空地，日光毫无遮拦地泻满其间。此地有一眼泉水，自一块铁色巨岩底下渗出，岩上结满灰色的地衣与绿色的苔藓。一个女子坐在岩上，一手支颐，神情仿佛在思量遥远而可心的事情。她通身衣白，颈间与臂上箍着金属的环带；她披散的长发色如麦秸，又淡浅得像孩童的头发，在她周身熠熠闪烁，铺展在身后岩上的地方，泛出霜也似的寒光。

她朝佩里翁转过身来，不慌不忙，也毫无讶色。佩里翁认出，这女子正是梅吕西娜夫人——当年佩里翁在赫尔马斯王麾下效力时，曾因爱她而自受其伤（如君所闻）。她良久不言，只懒懒地端详着佩里翁那张诚朴的脸，怀着一种异样的怅惜，怅惜那上面再也寻不见的崇慕。

===Original===

“Then it was really you,” he said, in wonder, “whom I saw talking with Demetrios when I awakened today.”

“You may be sure,” she answered, “that my talking was in no way injurious to you. Ah, no, had I been elsewhere, Perion, I think you would by this have been in Paradise.” Then Mélusine fell again to meditation. “And so you do not any longer either love or hate me, Perion?” Here was an odd echo of the complaint Demetrios had made.

“That I once loved you is a truth which neither of us, I think, may ever quite forget,” said Perion, very quiet. “I alone know how utterly I loved you﻿—no, it was not I who loved you, but a boy that is dead now. King’s daughter, all of stone, O cruel woman and hateful, O sleek, smiling traitress! today no man remembers how utterly I loved you, for the years are as a mist between the heart of the dead boy and me, so that I may no longer see the boy’s heart clearly. Yes, I have forgotten much.﻿ ﻿… Yet even today there is that in me which is faithful to you, and I cannot give you the hatred which your treachery has earned.”

===Chinese===

「那么，我今日醒来时看见与德米特里奥斯说话的，当真是你了。」他惊异地说。

「你尽可放心，」她答道，「我那一番言语，绝无半点害你之处。啊，不，佩里翁，倘若我当时身在别处，我想你此刻早已进了乐园。」随后梅吕西娜复又坠入沉思。「这么说，你如今既不再爱我，也不再恨我了，佩里翁？」这话古怪地应和着德米特里奥斯先前那番怨言。

「我曾爱过你，这件事，我想你我二人都永远无法全然忘却。」佩里翁说道，声气极静。「唯有我知道，当年我爱得何等彻底——不，爱你的并不是我，而是一个如今已死的少年。国王的女儿，一身心肠都是石头做的，哦，残忍而可憎的女人，哦，光艳巧笑的女叛徒！今日再没有人记得我爱得何等彻底，因为岁月像一片薄雾，隔在死去的少年的心与我之间，叫我再不能把那少年的心看个分明。是的，我已忘却了许多。……然而即使在今日，我身上仍有什么东西忠贞于你，你那背信弃义应得的憎恨，我给不出来。」

===Original===

Mélusine spoke shrewdly. She had a sweet, shrill voice.

“But I loved you, Perion﻿—oh, yes, in part I loved you, just as one cannot help but love a large and faithful mastiff. But you were tedious, you annoyed me by your egotism. Yes, my friend, you think too much of what you owe to Perion’s honour; you are perpetually squaring accounts with heaven, and you are too intent on keeping the balance in your favour to make a satisfactory lover.” You saw that Mélusine was smiling in the shadow of her pale hair. “And yet you are very droll when you are unhappy,” she said, as of two minds.

He replied:

===Chinese===

梅吕西娜言辞犀利。她有一副甜美而尖细的嗓子。

「可是我爱过你，佩里翁——哦，是的，我也算爱过你，就像人免不了要爱一条高大而忠心的獒犬。可你实在乏味，你的自大让我着恼。是的，我的朋友，你把你欠佩里翁名誉的债看得太重；你无时无刻不在与上天结账，又太操心让账面总是偏向自己，做不成一个叫人满意的情人。」看得出，梅吕西娜正在那头淡色头发的荫影里微笑。「不过，你不快活的时候倒真是滑稽。」她说，似有两难之意。

他答道：

===Original===

“I am, as heaven made me, a being of mingled nature. So I remember without distaste old happenings which now seem scarcely credible. I cannot quite believe that it was you and I who were so happy when youth was common to us﻿ ﻿… O Mélusine, I have almost forgotten that if the world were searched between the sunrise and the sunsetting the Mélusine I loved would not be found. I only know that a woman has usurped the voice of Mélusine, and that this woman’s eyes also are blue, and that this woman smiles as Mélusine was used to smile when I was young. I walk with ghosts, king’s daughter, and I am none the happier.”

“Ay, Periori,” she wisely answered, “for the spring is at hand, intent upon an ageless magic. I am no less comely than I was, and my heart, I think, is tenderer. You are yet young, and you are very beautiful, my brave mastiff﻿ ﻿… And neither of us is moved at all! For us the spring is only a dotard sorcerer who has forgotten the spells of yesterday. I think that it is pitiable, although I would not have it otherwise.” She waited, fairy-like and wanton, seeming to premeditate a delicate mischief.

He declared, sighing, “No, I would not have it otherwise.”

===Chinese===

「我正如上天造我的模样，是个天性驳杂的人。所以追忆起旧日种种，我并不觉得厌恶，虽然那些事如今看来几乎难以置信。我几乎不能相信，青春为我们两人所共有的年月里，那样幸福地活着的，真是你和我。……哦，梅吕西娜，我几乎已经忘记：纵使把世界从日出搜到日落，我所爱的那个梅吕西娜也再寻不见了。我只知道，一个女子僭占了梅吕西娜的声音，这女子的眼睛也是蓝的，这女子笑起来的模样，也像我年轻时梅吕西娜惯常的笑。我与幽灵同行，国王的女儿，却并不因此快活分毫。」

「是了，佩里翁，」她睿智地答道，「因为春天已近在手边，正一门心思要行那不老的魔法。我不比从前减色，而我这颗心，我想，比从前更软了。你还年轻，你又生得这样俊美，我勇敢的獒犬。……可我们两个，竟谁也不动一丝心！对我们来说，春天不过是个年迈昏聩的巫师，念忘了昨日的咒语。我想这着实可悲，虽然我也不愿事情另有模样。」她等候着，仙灵似的冶艳轻狂，仿佛在预谋一桩精巧的恶作剧。

他叹了一声，宣言道：「不，我也不愿事情另有模样。」

===Original===

Then presently Mélusine arose. She said:

“You are a hunted man, unarmed﻿—oh, yes, I know. Demetrios talked freely, because the son of Miramon Lluagor has good and ancient reasons to trust me. Besides, it was not for nothing that Pressina was my mother, and I know many things, pilfering light from the past to shed it upon the future. Come now with me to Brunbelois. I am too deeply in your debt, my Perion. For the sake of that boy who is dead﻿—as you tell me﻿—you may honourably accept of me a horse, arms, and a purse, because I loved that boy after my fashion.”

“I take your bounty gladly,” he replied; and he added conscientiously: “I consider that I am not at liberty to refuse of anybody any honest means of serving my lady Melicent.”

Mélusine parted her lips as if about to speak, and then seemed to think better of it. It is probable she was already informed concerning Melicent; she certainly asked no questions. Mélusine only shrugged, and laughed afterward, and the man and the woman turned toward Brunbelois. At times a shaft of sunlight would fall on her pale hair and convert it into silver, as these two went through the high woods that had never yet been felled or ordered.

===Chinese===

随后，梅吕西娜站起身来。她说：

「你如今是个遭人追捕的人，手无寸铁——哦，是的，我知道。德米特里奥斯说话向来无遮无拦，因为米拉蒙·卢阿戈尔之子自有充分而古老的缘由信任我。再者，我的母亲既是普雷西娜（Pressina），这也不是全然无谓的事，我知道许多事情，惯会从往昔窃来光，洒向未来。现在，随我到布兰布卢瓦去。我欠你的债太深了，我的佩里翁。看在那个已死的少年的份上——照你的说法——你尽可以光明正大地从我手里接过一匹马、一副兵甲和一只钱袋，因为我曾按我自己的方式爱过那个少年。」

「你的慨赠，我欣然领受。」他答道；随即又本着良心补了一句：「依我想，凡有助于事奉我女主人梅莉森特的正当手段，无论出自何人，我都无权推辞。」

梅吕西娜启了启唇，似欲有言，随即又像是想了想，觉得还是不说为妙。多半她早已听闻梅莉森特其人；她确实一句也没有问。梅吕西娜只是耸了耸肩，随后笑出声来，这一男一女便转身朝布兰布卢瓦去了。两人穿行在那片从未经砍伐、从未经整饬的高林间，不时有一道日光落在她淡色的头发上，把它化作灿灿的白银。

## XXVIII How Perion Saw Melicent / 二十八 佩里翁如何望见梅莉森特

===Original===

The manner of the torment of Melicent was this: A little before dawn she was conducted by Ahasuerus and Orestes to the outermost turrets of Nacumera, which were now beginning to take form and colour. Very suddenly a flash of light had flooded the valley, the big crimson sun was instantaneously apparent as though he had leaped over the bleeding night-mists. Darkness and all night’s adherents were annihilated. Pelicans and geese and curlews were in uproar, as at a concerted signal. A buzzard yelped thrice like a dog, and rose in a long spiral from the cliff to Melicent’s right hand. He hung motionless, a speck in the clear zenith, uncannily anticipative. Warmth flooded the valley.
Now Melicent could see the long and narrow plain beneath her. It was overgrown with a tall coarse grass which, rippling in the dawn-wind, resembled moving waters from this distance, save where clumps of palm trees showed like islands. Farther off, the tents of the Free Companions were as the white, sharp teeth of a lion. Also she could see﻿—and did not recognise﻿—the helmet-covered head of Perion catch and reflect the sunrays dazzlingly, where he knelt in the shimmering grass just out of bowshot.
Now Perion could see a woman standing, in the newborn sunlight, under many gaily coloured banners. The maiden was attired in a robe of white silk, and about her wrists were heavy bands of silver. Her hair blazed in the light, bright as the sunflower glows; her skin was whiter than milk; the down of a fledgling bird was not more grateful to the touch than were her hands. There was never anywhere a person more delightful to gaze upon, and whosoever beheld her forthwith desired to render love and service to Dame Melicent. This much could Perion know, whose fond eyes did not really see the woman upon the battlements but, instead, young Melicent as young Perion had first beheld her walking by the sea at Bellegarde.
Thus Perion, who knelt in adoration of that listless girl, all white and silver, and gold, too, where her blown hair showed like a halo. Desirable and lovelier than words may express seemed Melicent to Perion as she stood thus in lonely exaltation, and behind her, glorious banners fluttered, and the blue sky took on a deeper colour. What Perion saw was like a church window when the sun shines through it. Ahasuerus perfectly understood the baiting of a trap.

===Chinese===

折磨梅莉森特的手段是这样的：拂晓前不久，亚哈随鲁与俄瑞斯忒斯把她带到了纳库梅拉最外缘的角楼上；角楼此刻正开始在晨光里显出形影与颜色。一道闪光蓦地涌满了山谷，硕大赤红的太阳霎时显现，仿佛一跃跨过了血色未干的夜霭。黑暗与黑夜的一切党羽就此荡灭。鹈鹕、雁与鹬鸟喧腾骚动，如同得了共同约定的信号。一只鵟鸟像狗似的吠了三声，从梅莉森特右手边的峭壁上划出一道长长的螺线腾空而起。它一动不动地悬着，成了澄澈天顶上的一个斑点，诡异地预示着什么。暖意涌遍了山谷。
此刻梅莉森特能望见脚下那条狭长的平原。平原上遍生高高的粗硬野草，在晨风中翻起涟漪，从这么远望下去宛如一片流动的水面，唯有一簇簇棕榈树像岛屿似的凸现其间。更远处，自由佣兵的营帐宛如狮子那一口白森森的利齿。她还望见——却并未认出——佩里翁那覆着头盔的头颅耀眼地接住并映回日光；他正跪在微微闪烁的草丛里，恰在箭程之外。
此刻佩里翁也能望见一个女子，在初生的日光里，伫立于许多色彩绚丽的旌旗之下。那少女身披一袭白丝长袍，双腕上箍着沉甸甸的银镯。她的头发在光中灼灼生辉，明亮如向日葵的光焰；她的肌肤比牛乳还白；雏鸟的绒毛触在手上，也不及她的双手那样柔婉可亲。普天之下再没有比她更悦目的人，谁见了她，都会即刻甘愿向梅莉森特夫人献上爱慕与效忠。佩里翁所能知晓的便是这些：他那一双痴情的眼睛，其实并没有看见城垛上的这个妇人，看见的乃是年轻的梅莉森特——正如年轻的佩里翁初次望见她时那样，她正在贝勒加德的海边散步。
佩里翁就这样跪着崇慕那个慵倦的少女，她通身是白、是银，也是金——她飘拂的头发映着光，宛如一轮光环。梅莉森特这般伫立在孤高的昂扬之中，身后华美的旌旗猎猎飘动，蓝天也染上一层更深的颜色；此刻在佩里翁眼里，她是何等可欲，何等可爱，绝非言语所能形容。佩里翁望见的这一幕，就像阳光照透教堂的彩窗。而亚哈随鲁对如何给陷阱布饵，实在是了然于胸。

===Original===

Perion came into the open plain before the castle and called on her dear name three times. Then Perion, naked to his enemies, and at the disposal of the first pagan archer that chose to shoot him down, sang cheerily the waking-song which Melicent had heard a mimic Amphitryon make in Dame Alcmena’s honour, very long ago, when people laughed and Melicent was young and ignorant of misery.
Sang Perion, “*Rei glorios, verais lums e clardatz﻿—*” or, in other wording:
“Thou King of glory, veritable light, all-powerful deity! be pleased to succour faithfully my fair, sweet friend. The night that severed us has been long and bitter, the darkness has been shaken by bleak winds, but now the dawn is near at hand.
“My fair sweet friend, be of good heart! We have been tormented long enough by evil dreams. Be of good heart, for the dawn is approaching! The east is astir. I have seen the orient star which heralds day. I discern it clearly, for now the dawn is near at hand.”

===Chinese===

佩里翁走进城堡前的开阔平原，把她亲爱的名字呼唤了三遍。随后，佩里翁——周身袒露于敌人眼前，任凭第一个乐意将他射杀的异教弓手处置——欢畅地唱起了那支唤醒之歌。那是很久以前，梅莉森特曾听一位扮演安菲特律翁（Amphitryon）的伶人，为向阿尔克墨涅夫人（Dame Alcmena）致敬而唱过的歌；那时人们还会欢笑，梅莉森特还年轻，还不知道苦难为何物。
佩里翁唱道：「*Rei glorios, verais lums e clardatz*——」换一种言辞来说，便是：
「荣耀之王，真实之光，全能的神明啊！求你垂怜，忠实地护佑我美好而甜蜜的爱人。使我们分离的这一夜漫长而苦涩，黑暗被凛冽的风撼动，而今黎明已近在眼前。
「我美好而甜蜜的爱人，请振作！恶梦折磨我们，已经够久了。请振作，因为黎明正在来临！东方已经骚动。我望见了那颗报晓的晨星。我看得清清楚楚，因为此刻黎明已近在眼前。」

===Original===

The song was no great matter; but the splendid futility of its performance amid such touch-and-go surroundings Melicent considered to be august. And consciousness of his words’ poverty, as Perion thus lightly played with death in order to accord due honour to the lady he served, was to Dame Melicent in her high martyrdom as is the twist of a dagger in an already fatal wound; and made her love augment.
Sang Perion:
“My fair sweet friend, it is I, your servitor, who cry to you, *Be of good heart*! Regard the sky and the stars now growing dim, and you will see that I have been an untiring sentinel. It will presently fare the worse for those who do not recognise that the dawn is near at hand.
“My fair sweet friend, since you were taken from me I have not ever been of a divided mind. I have kept faith, I have not failed you. Hourly I have entreated God and the Son of Mary to have compassion upon our evil dreams. And now the dawn is near at hand.”

===Chinese===

这支歌本身算不得什么；可是在这般一触即发的险境里唱它，那份堂皇的徒劳，梅莉森特觉得庄严无比。而佩里翁为了给他所事奉的夫人应得的敬意，就这样轻掷生死，那份词句寒伧的自觉，落在身处崇高殉难中的梅莉森特夫人心上，恰似往一道本已致命的伤口里再拧一匕首；这使她的爱愈发深重。
佩里翁又唱道：
「我美好而甜蜜的爱人，是我，你的仆役，在向你呼喊：*请振作*！看那天空和正渐渐黯淡的群星，你便知道我一直是个不知疲倦的守夜人。那些认不出黎明已近在眼前的人，转眼便要遭殃了。
「我美好而甜蜜的爱人，自从你被人从我身边夺去，我的心从未有过半分旁骛。我守住了信义，我从未辜负你。我时时刻刻恳求上帝和马利亚之子，怜悯我们的恶梦。而今黎明已近在眼前。」

===Original===

“My poor, bruised, puzzled boy,” thought Melicent, as she had done so long ago, “how came you to be blundering about this miry world of ours? And how may I be worthy?”
Orestes spoke. His voice disturbed the woman’s rapture thinly, like the speech of a ghost, and she remembered now that a bustling world was her antagonist.
“Assuredly,” Orestes said, “this man is insane. I will forthwith command my archers to despatch him in the middle of his caterwauling. For at this distance they cannot miss him.”
But Ahasuerus said:

===Chinese===

「我可怜的、遍体鳞伤、懵懂茫然的孩子啊，」梅莉森特暗自想着，一如很久以前想过的那样，「你是怎么跌跌撞撞闯进我们这个泥泞的人世来的？而我，又怎样才能配得上你？」
俄瑞斯忒斯开口了。他的声音轻而薄地搅扰了那女子的心醉神迷，像鬼魂的话语一般；她这才想起，与她对敌的，原是这熙熙攘攘的人世。
「可以断言，」俄瑞斯忒斯说，「这人疯了。我这就吩咐弓箭手，趁他嗥唱到一半时把他结果了。在这么近的距离，他们绝不会射不中。」
但亚哈随鲁说道：

===Original===

“No, seignior, not by my advice. If you slay this Perion of the Forest, his retainers will speedily abandon a desperate siege and retreat to the coast. But they will never retreat so long as the man lives and sways them, and we hold Melicent, for, as you plainly see, this abominable reprobate is quite besotted with love of her. His death would win you praise; but the destruction of his armament will purchase you your province. Now in two days at most our troops will come, and then we will slay all the Free Companions.”
“That is true,” said Orestes, “and it is remarkable how you think of these things so quickly.”
So Orestes was ruled by Ahasuerus, and Perion, through no merit of his own, departed unharmed.
Then Melicent was conducted to her own apartments; and eunuchs guarded her, while the battle was, and men she had not ever seen died by the score because her beauty was so great.

===Chinese===

「不，大人，依我的忠告不可。你若杀了这位林中佩里翁，他的部众很快便会放弃这场殊死的围攻，退往海岸。可只要此人生存一日、还能统御他们一日，而我们又握着梅莉森特，他们就决不会退走；因为，你看得明明白白，这个可憎的浪荡子已被对她的爱迷住了心窍。他的死会为你赢得赞誉；可唯有歼灭他麾下的兵马，才会为你买下你的行省。至多两日，我们的兵马便到，那时我们再将自由佣兵斩尽杀绝。」
「这话不错，」俄瑞斯忒斯说，「你竟能这样快想到这些事，实在令人称奇。」
就这样，俄瑞斯忒斯被亚哈随鲁左右了，而佩里翁——并非凭自己的任何功绩——安然无损地离去了。
随后梅莉森特被送回自己的居所；战事进行期间，由宦官看守着她；她从未见过的男人们成十成二十地死去，只因她的美貌太过惊人。

## VII How Perion Was Freed / 七 佩里翁如何获释

===Original===

Demetrios lay on a divan within the Court of Stars, through which you passed from the fortress into the Women’s Garden and the luxurious prison where he kept his wives. This court was circular in form and was paved with red and yellow slabs, laid alternately, like a chessboard. In the centre was a fountain, which cast up a tall thin jet of water. A gallery extended around the place, supported by columns that had been painted scarlet and were gilded with fantastic designs. The walls were of the colour of claret and were adorned with golden cinquefoils regularly placed. From a distance they resembled stars, and so gave the enclosure its name.

Demetrios lay upon a long divan which was covered with crimson, and which encircled the court entirely, save for the apertures of the two entrances. Demetrios was of burly person, which he by ordinary, as today, adorned resplendently; of a stature little above the common size, and disproportionately broad as to his chest and shoulders. It was rumoured that he could bore an apple through with his forefinger and had once killed a refractory horse with a blow of his naked fist; nor looking on the man, did you presume to question the report. His eyes were large and insolent, coloured like onyxes; for the rest, he had a handsome surly face which was disfigured by pimples.

He did not speak at all while Jocelin explained that his errand was to ransom Perion. Then, “At what price?” Demetrios said, without any sign of interest; and Jocelin, with many encomiums, displayed his emeralds.

“Ay, they are well enough,” Demetrios agreed. “But then I have a superfluity of jewels.”

===Chinese===

德米特里奥斯躺在星辰宫中的一张长榻上。从堡垒通往女眷园——他安置妻妾的那座奢华牢狱——正须穿过这座宫院。宫院呈圆形，地面铺着红黄两色的石板，交错相间，宛如棋盘。正中是一眼喷泉，喷起一道高而细的水柱。一道回廊环绕整座院落，由一根根漆成朱红、又以鎏金绘上奇诡纹样的柱子承托。四壁皆是酒红色，缀着排列齐整的五瓣金花；远远望去宛如繁星，这座院落便由此得名。

德米特里奥斯躺在一张蒙着绯红织物的长榻上，长榻环绕整座宫院，只在两处入口留下缺口。他身躯魁伟，平素一如今日，总把自己装点得光彩夺目；个子只比常人略高，胸膛与肩膀却宽阔得不成比例。有传言说，他能用食指将一只苹果洞穿，还曾赤手空拳一拳打死过一匹桀骜不驯的马；而只要看上这人一眼，你便不敢贸然质疑这传闻。他双眼大而倨傲，色如缟玛瑙；此外，他生了一张英俊而乖戾的脸，只是被几颗脓疱破了相。

乔斯林（Jocelin）陈明来意，说是为佩里翁赎身，他自始至终一言不发。随后——「出什么价？」德米特里奥斯说，全无一丝兴致的样子；乔斯林便极口称誉，亮出了他的祖母绿。

「哎，这些也算不错，」德米特里奥斯应道，「只是我的珠宝早已多得过剩。」

===Original===

He raised himself a little among the cushions, and in this moving the figured golden stuff in which he was clothed heaved and glittered like the scales of a splendid monster. He leisurely unfastened the great chrysoberyl, big as a hen’s egg, which adorned his fillet.

“Look you, this is of a far more beautiful green than any of your trinkets, I think it is as valuable also, because of its huge size. Moreover, it turns red by lamplight﻿—red as blood. That is an admirable colour. And yet I do not value it. I think I do not value anything. So I will make you a gift of this big coloured pebble, if you desire it, because your ignorance amuses me. Most people know Demetrios is not a merchant. He does not buy and sell. That which he has he keeps, and that which he desires he takes.”

The boy was all despair. He did not speak. He was very handsome as he stood in that still place where everything excepting him was red and gold.

“You do not value my poor chrysoberyl? You value your friend more? It is a page out of Theocritos﻿—‘when there were golden men of old, when friends gave love for love.’ And yet I could have sworn﻿—Come now, a wager,” purred Demetrios. “Show your contempt of this bauble to be as great as mine by throwing this shiny pebble, say, into the gallery, for the next passerby to pick up, and I will credit your sincerity. Do that and I will even name my price for Perion.”

===Chinese===

他在软垫间微微支起身来；这一动，他身上那袭织金花衣便随之起伏流光，宛如一头华彩怪兽的鳞甲。他从容解下缀在束发带上的那颗硕大金绿宝石——宝石大如鸡蛋。

「你瞧，这一颗的绿，比你那些小玩意儿随便哪一颗都要美得多；我想它也一样值钱，因为它这般巨大。再者，它在灯下会变红——红得像血。那是一种令人赞叹的颜色。然而我并不珍视它。我想我对什么都不珍视。所以这块彩色的大卵石，你若想要，我便送你，因为你的无知让我觉得有趣。大多数人都知道，德米特里奥斯不是买卖人。他不做买进卖出的营生。已有的，他留住；想要的，他夺取。」

那少年满心绝望。他没有作声。他站在那片静谧之地，除他而外，周遭尽是红与金，这情景衬得他异常俊美。

「你不珍视我这颗可怜的金绿宝石？你更珍视你的朋友？这是忒奥克里托斯（Theocritos）书页里的一句——『当远古还有黄金之人的时候，朋友以爱还爱。』然而我原可以起誓——来吧，来打个赌，」德米特里奥斯柔声说道。「把你对这件小玩物的轻蔑表现得同我不相上下：把这颗亮晶晶的卵石扔出去——譬如扔进回廊，让下一个过路人捡去——我便信你的诚意。做到这一步，我甚至肯为佩里翁开出价来。」

===Original===

The boy obeyed him without hesitation. Turning, he saw the horrid change in the intent eyes of Demetrios, and quailed before it. But instantly that flare of passion flickered out.

Demetrios gently said:

“A bargain is a bargain. My wives are beautiful, but their caresses annoy me as much as formerly they pleased me. I have long thought it would perhaps amuse me if I possessed a Christian wife who had eyes like violets and hair like gold, and a plump white body. A man tires very soon of ebony and amber.﻿ ﻿… Procure me such a wife and I will willingly release this Perion and all his fellows who are yet alive.”

“But, seignior,”﻿—and the boy was shaken now﻿—“you demand of me an impossibility!”

===Chinese===

那少年毫不犹豫地依言而行。他转过身来，正看见德米特里奥斯那双凝注的眼睛里起了骇人的变化，不由得望而生畏。但那阵情欲的火光旋即摇曳而灭。

德米特里奥斯温和地说：

「买卖便是买卖。我的妻妾都生得美，可她们的爱抚如今令我厌烦，一如往日令我欢愉。我久已在想：倘若我拥有一个基督徒妻子，生着紫罗兰般的眼睛、黄金般的头发，还有一副丰腴白皙的身体，那或许会让我觉得有趣。乌木与琥珀，一个男人很快就腻了。……替我觅来这样一位妻子，我便甘愿放走这个佩里翁，连同他那班尚在人世的同伴。」

「可是，大人，」——那少年此刻已经方寸大乱——「您这是向我索求一件不可能的事！」

===Original===

“I am so hardy as to think not. And my reason is that a man throws from the elbow only, but a woman with her whole arm.”

There fell a silence now.

“Why, look you, I deal fairly, though. Were such a woman here﻿—Demetrios of Anatolia’s guest﻿—I verily believe I would not hinder her departure, as I might easily do. For there is not a person within many miles of this place who considers it wholesome to withstand me. Yet were this woman purchasable, I would purchase. And﻿—if she refused﻿—I would not hinder her departure; but very certainly I would put Perion to the Torment of the Waterdrops. It is so droll to see a man go mad before your eyes, I think that I would laugh and quite forget the woman.”

She said, “O God, I cry to You for justice!”

===Chinese===

「我却斗胆不这么想。我的理由是：男人投掷，只凭手肘；女人投掷，却用整条手臂。」

这时，一阵沉默落了下来。

「不过，你瞧，我行事到底公道。倘若这样一个女子在此地——做安纳托利亚的德米特里奥斯的宾客——我确信我不会阻拦她离去，尽管我轻而易举便能阻拦。因为这地方方圆许多里之内，没有一个人认为与我作对还能保得安康。然而这女子若肯出售，我便买下。而——倘若她拒绝——我仍旧不阻拦她离去；只是我必定要把佩里翁送上水滴刑（Torment of the Waterdrops）。眼看一个人在你眼前发疯，实在滑稽得很，我想我会放声大笑，把那女人忘个干净。」

她说道：「哦，上帝，我向祢呼求公义！」

===Original===

He answered:

“My good girl, in Nacumera the wishes of Demetrios are justice. But we waste time. You desire to purchase one of my belongings? So be it. I will hear your offer.”

Just once her hands had gripped each other. Her arms fell now as if they had been drained of life. She spoke in a dull voice.

“Seignior, I offer Melicent who was a princess. I cry a price, seignior, for red lips and bright eyes and a fair woman’s tender body without any blemish. I cry a price for youth and happiness and honour. These you may have for playthings, seignior, with everything which I possess, except my heart, for that is dead.”

===Chinese===

他答道：

「我的好姑娘，在纳库梅拉，德米特里奥斯的心愿便是公义。不过，我们这是在虚耗光阴。你想买下我的一件所有物？也好。我便听听你的出价。」

方才她的双手只狠狠绞紧过一回。此刻她双臂垂落，仿佛里头的生机已被抽干。她声音木然地开了口。

「大人，我献上梅莉森特，她从前是一位公主。我为一双红唇、一双明眸、一具无瑕可指的姣好女子的温柔身体喊出价来。我为青春、幸福与名节喊出价来。这些您尽可拿去作玩物，大人，连同我拥有的一切——只除却我的心，因为那颗心已经死了。」

===Original===

Demetrios asked, “Is this true speech?”

She answered:

“It is as sure as Love and Death. I know that nothing is more sure than these, and I praise God for my sure knowledge.”

He chuckled, saying, “Platitudes break no bones.”

===Chinese===

德米特里奥斯问：「此话当真？」

她答道：

「它与爱和死一样确凿。我知道世间再没有比这两样更确凿的事物，我为这份确凿的认知赞美上帝。」

他轻声笑了，说道：「陈词滥调，折不断骨头。」

===Original===

So on the next day the chains were filed from Perion de la Forêt and all his fellows, save the nine unfortunates whom Demetrios had appointed to fight with lions a month before this, when he had entertained the Sultan of Bacharia. These men were bathed and perfumed and richly clad.

A galley of the proconsul’s fleet conveyed them toward Christendom and set the twoscore slaves of yesterday ashore not far from Megaris. The captain of the galley on departure left with Perion a blue napkin, wherein were wrapped large emeralds and a bit of parchment.

Upon this parchment was written:

> “Not these, but the body of Melicent, who was once a princess, purchased your bodies. Yet these will buy you ships and men and swords with which to storm my house where Melicent now is. Come if you will and fight with Demetrios of Anatolia for that brave girl who loved a porter as all loyal men should love their Maker and customarily do not. I think it would amuse us.”

===Chinese===

于是第二天，佩里翁·德·拉福雷与他全部同伴身上的锁链都锉断了，只有那九个不幸之人除外——一个月前，德米特里奥斯款待巴卡里亚的苏丹（Sultan of Bacharia）时，指定了他们去同狮子搏斗。这些人沐了浴，熏了香，穿上了华贵的衣裳。

总督舰队的一艘桨帆船载着他们驶向基督教世界，在离梅加里斯不远之处，把这昨日犹为奴隶的四十人送上了岸。桨帆船的船长临行前留给佩里翁一条蓝色餐巾，里头裹着几颗硕大的祖母绿和一小片羊皮纸。

羊皮纸上写着：

> 「买下你们肉身的，并非这些宝石，而是梅莉森特的肉身——她从前是一位公主。然而这些宝石能替你买来船舰、人马与刀剑，好去攻陷我的宅邸，梅莉森特如今便在那里。你若愿意，便来与安纳托利亚的德米特里奥斯为那个勇敢的姑娘一战——她爱一个挑夫，爱得如同一切忠贞之士爱他们的造物主一般，而忠贞之士通常并不那样去爱。我想，那会让我们都觉得有趣。」

===Original===

Then Perion stood by the languid sea which severed him from Melicent and cried:

“O God, that hast permitted this hard bargain, trade now with me! now barter with me, O Father of us all! That which a man has I will give.”

Thus he waited in the clear sunlight, with no more wavering in his face than you may find in the next statue’s face. Both hands strained toward the blue sky, as though he made a vow. If so, he did not break it.

And now no more of Perion.

===Chinese===

于是佩里翁伫立在那道把他与梅莉森特隔开的慵懒大海边，呼喊道：

「哦，上帝，祢既容许了这桩苛刻的买卖，如今便与我交易吧！与我易货吧，哦，我们众人之父！一个男人所有的一切，我都愿献出。」

他就这样在明澈的日光里等候着，脸上不见一丝动摇，一如你在近旁那尊雕像的脸上所能寻见的。他双手向蓝天竭力张举，仿佛在立一个誓。若果真如此，这誓言他从未背弃。

至于佩里翁，此处便不再多叙。

---

===Original===

At the same hour young Melicent, wrapped all about with a flame-coloured veil and crowned with marjoram, was led by a spruce boy toward a threshold, over which Demetrios lifted her, while many people sang in a strange tongue. And then she paid her ransom.

“Hymen, O Hymen!” they sang. “Do thou of many names and many temples, golden Aphrodite, be propitious to this bridal! Now let him first compute the glittering stars of midnight and the grasshoppers of a summer day who would count the joys this bridal shall bring about! Hymen, O Hymen, rejoice thou in this bridal!”

===Chinese===

同一时刻，年轻的梅莉森特周身裹着一条火焰色的面纱，头上戴着墨角兰花冠，由一个衣冠楚楚的少年引到一道门槛前；德米特里奥斯将她抱过门槛，许多人用一种陌生的语言唱起歌来。随后，她付清了自己的赎金。

「许门（Hymen），哦许门！」他们唱道。「有诸多名号、诸多神庙的祢，金色的阿芙罗狄忒，求祢眷顾这桩婚事！谁若要数算这桩婚事将催生的欢愉，就让他先去数算午夜闪烁的繁星、夏日一昼的蚱蜢吧！许门，哦许门，请祢在这桩婚事中欢欣！」

## I How Perion Was Unmasked / 一 佩里翁如何被揭穿

===Original===

Perion afterward remembered the two weeks spent at Bellegarde as in recovery from illness a person might remember some long fever dream which was all of an intolerable elvish brightness and of incessant laughter everywhere. They made a deal of him in Count Emmerick’s pleasant home: day by day the outlaw was thrust into relations of mirth with noblemen, proud ladies, and even with a king; and was all the while half lightheaded through his singular knowledge as to how precariously the self-styled Vicomte de Puysange now balanced himself, as it were, upon a gilded stepping-stone from infamy to oblivion.

Now that King Theodoret had withdrawn his sinister presence, young Perion spent some seven hours of every day alone, to all intent, with Dame Melicent. There might be merry people within a stone’s throw, about this recreation or another, but these two seemed to watch aloofly, as royal persons do the antics of their hired comedians, without any condescension into open interest. They were together; and the jostle of earthly happenings might hope, at most, to afford them matter for incurious comment.

They sat, as Perion thought, for the last time together, part of an audience before which the Confraternity of St. Médard was enacting a masque of *The Birth of Hercules*. The Bishop of Montors had returned to Bellegarde that evening with his brother, Count Gui, and the pleasure-loving prelate had brought these mirth-makers in his train. Clad in scarlet, he rode before them playing upon a lute﻿—unclerical conduct which shocked his preciser brother and surprised nobody.

In such circumstances Perion began to speak with an odd purpose, because his reason was bedrugged by the beauty and purity of Melicent, and perhaps a little by the slow and clutching music to whose progress the chorus of Theban virgins was dancing. When he had made an end of harsh whispering, Melicent sat for a while in scrupulous appraisement of the rushes. The music was so sweet it seemed to Perion he must go mad unless she spoke within the moment.

===Chinese===

事后，佩里翁回忆在贝勒加德度过的那两个星期，就像大病初愈的人忆起一场漫长的热病之梦：那梦里满是一种妖精似的、令人消受不起的璀璨光亮，四下里响着无休无止的笑声。在埃默里克伯爵那座其乐融融的宅邸里，众人都百般抬举他：日复一日，这个亡命之徒被推入与贵族老爷们、高傲的贵妇们、甚至与一位国王欢笑周旋的往来之中；而他自始至终都有几分神思恍惚，只因他独自知晓，那位自封的皮桑日子爵眼下正何等险仄地维持着平衡——仿佛踩在一块镀金的踏脚石上，从恶名跨向湮没无闻。

如今泰奥多雷特王已携着他那阴鸷的身影退场，年轻的佩里翁每天总有约莫七个钟头，实际上可说是与梅莉森特夫人单独相守。一投石之遥的近处或许正有寻欢作乐的人们，为着这样那样的消遣热闹着，但这两人只是超然旁观，恰如王室贵人观看自家雇来的伶人插科打诨，绝不肯纡尊降贵，露出分明的兴致。他们俩厮守在一处；尘世的种种纷扰，至多也只能指望为他们添些无心细究的谈资罢了。

在佩里翁想来，这是他们最后一次同坐一处了：此刻圣梅达尔兄弟会正在众宾客面前搬演假面剧《赫拉克勒斯诞生记》，他们两人也在观众之列。当晚，蒙托尔主教偕他的兄弟居伊伯爵（Count Gui）回到了贝勒加德，这位贪恋行乐的高级教士还把那班逗笑的角色带在了随行的队伍里。他一身猩红衣裳，骑马行在众人之前，手里弹着一把鲁特琴——这般有失教士体统的行径，骇坏了他那位端方持重的兄弟，却没有让任何人觉得意外。

就在这样的情境里，佩里翁怀着一种古怪的用意开了口，因为他的理智已被梅莉森特的美貌与纯洁所麻醉，或许也被那缓慢而攫人的乐声麻醉了几分——底比斯（Thebes）少女们的歌队正应和着乐声的进展翩翩起舞。他这一番低语，说得刺耳刺心；说完之后，梅莉森特有一会儿只是端坐着，细细估量地上铺陈的灯芯草。那音乐甜美极了，佩里翁觉得，她若不当场开口，自己必得发疯不可。

===Original===

Then Melicent said:

“You tell me you are not the Vicomte de Puysange. You tell me you are, instead, the late King Helmas’ servitor, suspected of his murder. You are the fellow that stole the royal jewels﻿—the outlaw for whom half Christendom is searching﻿—”

Thus Melicent began to speak at last; and still he could not intercept those huge and tender eyes whose purple made the thought of heaven comprehensible.

The man replied:

“I am that widely hounded Perion of the Forest. The true *vicomte* is the wounded rascal over whose delirium we marvelled only last Tuesday. Yes, at the door of your home I attacked him, fought him﻿—hah, but fairly, madame!﻿—and stole his brilliant garments and with them his papers. Then in my desperate necessity I dared to masquerade. For I know enough about dancing to estimate that to dance upon air must necessarily prove to everybody a disgusting performance, but preeminently unpleasing to the main actor. Two weeks of safety till the *Tranchemer* sailed I therefore valued at a perhaps preposterous rate. Tonight, as I have said, the ship lies at anchor off Manneville.”

===Chinese===

于是梅莉森特说道：

「你对我说，你不是皮桑日子爵。你对我说，你其实是已故赫尔马斯王的仆从，有弑主之嫌。你就是盗窃王室珠宝的那个人——半个基督教世界都在搜捕的亡命之徒——」

梅莉森特终于这样开了口；而他依旧无从截住那双大而温柔的眼睛，正是那眼中的紫色，令天堂这一意念变得可以理喻。

那男子答道：

「我正是那个遭到四方追缉的林中佩里翁。真正的子爵，就是上星期二我们还对着他的呓语啧啧称奇的那个受伤的无赖。不错，在你们家门前，我袭击了他，与他交了手——哈，可是堂堂正正交的手，夫人！——偷走了他那身鲜亮的衣裳，连同衣裳里的文书。随后，在走投无路的急难之中，我斗胆冒名顶替。因为说起跳舞，我总算也懂得一点，足以断定：在半空中跳舞，于众人必定是一场不堪入目的表演，而于主跳的那一位，尤其不愉快。因此，直到特朗什梅尔号启航为止那两个星期的安稳，我便标了一副或许荒唐透顶的价钱。今夜，如我方才所言，那艘船正下锚泊在曼讷维尔（Manneville）外海。」

===Original===

Melicent said an odd thing, asking, “Oh, can it be you are a less despicable person than you are striving to appear!”

“Rather, I am a more unmitigated fool than even I suspected, since when affairs were in a promising train I have elected to blurt out, of all things, the naked and distasteful truth. Proclaim it now; and see the late Vicomte de Puysange lugged out of this hall and after appropriate torture hanged within the month.” And with that Perion laughed.

Thereafter he was silent. As the masque went, Amphitryon had newly returned from warfare, and was singing under Alcmena’s window in the terms of an aubade, a waking-song. “*Rei glorios, verais lums e clardatz﻿—*” Amphitryon had begun. Dame Melicent heard him through.

And after many ages, as it seemed to Perion, the soft and brilliant and exquisite mouth was pricked to motion.

===Chinese===

梅莉森特说出了一句古怪的话，问道：「哦，难道你竟是一个不似你竭力装出的那般可鄙的人么！」

「毋宁说，我是一个连我自己都疑想不到的、更不可救药的傻瓜，因为正当诸事大有指望之际，我却偏偏拣中了这件事：把那赤裸裸、倒人胃口的真相脱口抖搂了出来。这就去宣告吧；然后看这位前皮桑日子爵被人拖出这座大厅，先受足相应的酷刑，不出这个月便被绞死。」说罢，佩里翁笑了起来。

此后他便沉默了。假面剧演到此时，安菲特律翁（Amphitryon）刚刚从戎马中归来，正用一支晨曲（aubade）——一支唤醒之歌——的辞句，在阿尔克墨涅（Alcmena）的窗下歌唱。「*Rei glorios, verais lums e clardatz*——」安菲特律翁已经这样起了头。梅莉森特夫人从头到尾听他唱完。

而在佩里翁的感觉里，仿佛隔了漫漫数世，那张柔软、明艳而精妙的嘴才仿佛被轻轻刺了一下，动了起来。

===Original===

“You have affronted, by an incredible imposture and beyond the reach of mercy, every listener in this hall. You have injured me most deeply of all persons here. Yet it is to me alone that you confess.”

Perion leaned forward. You are to understand that, through the incurrent necessities of every circumstance, each of them spoke in whispers, even now. It was curious to note the candid mirth on either side. Mercury was making his adieux to Alcmena’s waiting-woman in the middle of a jig.

“But you,” sneered Perion, “are merciful in all things. Rogue that I am, I dare to build on this notorious fact. I am snared in a hard golden trap, I cannot get a guide to Manneville, I cannot even procure a horse from Count Emmerick’s stables without arousing fatal suspicions; and I must be at Manneville by dawn or else be hanged. Therefore I dare stake all upon one throw; and you must either save or hang me with unwashed hands. As surely as God reigns, my future rests with you. And as I am perfectly aware, you could not live comfortably with a gnat’s death upon your conscience. Eh, am I not a seasoned rascal?”

“Do not remind me now that you are vile,” said Melicent. “Ah, no, not now!”

“Lackey, impostor, and thief!” he sternly answered. “There you have the catalogue of all my rightful titles. And besides, it pleases me, for a reason I cannot entirely fathom, to be unpardonably candid and to fling my destiny into your lap. Tonight, as I have said, the *Tranchemer* lies off Manneville; keep counsel, get me a horse if you will, and tomorrow I am embarked for desperate service under the harried Kaiser of the Greeks, and for throat-cuttings from which I am not likely ever to return. Speak, and I hang before the month is up.”

===Chinese===

「你以一场匪夷所思的冒名顶替，冒犯了这座大厅里的每一位听众，冒犯到连宽恕都无从施及的地步。在场众人里，你伤我伤得最深。可你偏偏只向我一人招认。」

佩里翁俯身向前。你们须得明白，因着眼前情势的种种必然，即便到了此刻，两人交谈仍全是耳语。两侧各自脸上那份坦率的欢愉，看来煞是新奇。台上的墨丘利（Mercury）正跳着一支吉格舞，跳到中段，向阿尔克墨涅的贴身侍女道别。

「可是你，」佩里翁讥诮道，「却在一切事上都心怀慈悲。我这样的恶棍，就敢在这条尽人皆知的美德上头下注。我如今陷在一只坚硬的黄金陷阱里脱身不得：我寻不到向导引我去曼讷维尔，甚至连从埃默里克伯爵的马厩里弄一匹马，都免不了惹来杀身之祸的猜疑；而天亮之前我必须赶到曼讷维尔，否则便只有绞索一条路。所以我敢孤注一掷，把全部身家押上；而你不免要做抉择：不是救我，便是带着一双未曾洗净的手把我送上绞架。上帝君临在上，千真万确，我的前程全系于你一身。何况我看得再清楚不过：你良心上但凡压着一只蚊蚋的死，也休想过安稳日子。嗯，我岂非一个老练的恶棍么？」

「现在别再向我提起你的卑劣，」梅莉森特说，「啊，别说了，此刻别说了！」

「跟班、骗子、窃贼！」他声色俱厉地答道。「这便是我一切名正言顺头衔的清册。再者，不知怎的——那缘故我自己也参详不透——我竟乐于这样不可宽恕地坦白，乐于把我的命运掷进你的怀中。今夜，如我方才所说，特朗什梅尔号正泊在曼讷维尔外海；你若肯守口如瓶，肯替我备一匹马，明日我便登船启碇，去投效那位备受侵凌的希腊皇帝，服一场殊死的劳役，去赴那些割喉沥血的厮杀，多半有去无还。你若声张，不出这个月我便吊死在绞架上。」

===Original===

Dame Melicent looked at him now, and within the moment Perion was repaid, and bountifully, for every folly and misdeed of his entire life.

“What harm have I ever done you, Messire de la Forêt, that you should shame me in this fashion? Until tonight I was not unhappy in the belief I was loved by you. I may say that now without paltering, since you are not the man I thought some day to love. You are but the rind of him. And you would force me to cheat justice, to become a hunted thief’s accomplice, or else to murder you!”

“It comes to that, madame.”

“Then I must help you preserve your life by any sorry stratagems you may devise. I shall not hinder you. I will procure you a guide to Manneville. I will even forgive you all save one offence, since doubtless heaven made you the foul thing you are.” The girl was in a hot and splendid rage. “For you love me. Women know. You love me. You!”

“Undoubtedly, madame.”

===Chinese===

这时梅莉森特夫人望着他；顷刻之间，佩里翁一生全部的痴愚与罪愆都得了报偿，而且报偿得丰厚无比。

「德·拉福雷爵爷，我究竟何曾伤害过你，你要这般羞辱我？直到今夜之前，我还自以为被你所爱，且并不以此为苦。这话如今我可以毫无虚饰地出口了，因为你并不是我素日指望有朝一日去爱的那个人。你不过是那个人的一层皮壳。而你竟要逼我欺瞒公义，逼我去做一个遭人追缉的窃贼的同谋，再不然，就得谋害你！」

「事情落到最后，正是如此，夫人。」

「那么，无论你筹划出何等蹩脚的计策来保全性命，我也只得帮你。我不会拦你。我会替你寻一个引你去曼讷维尔的向导。我甚至愿意饶恕你的一切，只除了一桩罪——因为毋庸置疑，是上天把你造就成了这等污秽之物。」这姑娘正当着一腔炽烈而堂皇的怒火。「因为你爱着我。这种事，女人知道。你爱着我。你！」

「毫无疑问，夫人。」

===Original===

“Look into my face! and say what horrid writ of infamy you fancied was apparent there, that my nails may destroy it.”

“I am all base,” he answered, “and yet not so profoundly base as you suppose. Nay, believe me, I had never hoped to win even such scornful kindness as you might accord your lapdog. I have but dared to peep at heaven while I might, and only as lost Dives peeped. Ignoble as I am, I never dreamed to squire an angel down toward the mire and filth which is henceforward my inevitable kennel.”

“The masque is done,” said Melicent, “and yet you talk, and talk, and talk, and mimic truth so cunningly﻿—Well, I will send some trusty person to you. And now, for God’s sake!﻿—nay, for the fiend’s love who is your patron!﻿—let me not ever see you again, Messire de la Forêt.”

===Chinese===

「看着我的脸！你说说，你臆想那上面明写着怎样一纸狰狞的耻辱文书，好让我的指甲把它毁掉。」

「我卑劣至极，」他答道，「却还不像你料想的那样卑劣到骨头里。不，请相信我，我从不曾指望赢得什么，就连你可能赏给膝头爱犬的那种带着轻蔑的温存，我也不曾指望。我不过是趁还能够的时候，斗胆窥了一眼天堂，而且窥探的模样，同那个沉沦的迪维斯（Dives）一般无二。我纵然卑劣，却从不曾梦想着护送一位天使，下到那泥泞污秽里去——从今往后，那便是我注定的狗窝。」

「假面戏已经收场了，」梅莉森特说，「你却还在说呀，说呀，说个不住，还把真话模仿得这般乖巧——好吧，我会差一个可靠的人去你那里。如今，看在上帝份上！——不，看在庇佑你的那位魔王的爱宠份上！——让我永远不要再见到你，德·拉福雷爵爷。」

## IV How the Bishop Aided Perion / 四 主教如何援助佩里翁

===Original===

Then at three o’clock, as Perion supposed, someone tapped upon the door. Perion went out into the corridor, which was now unlighted, so that he had to hold to the cloak of Ayrart de Montors as the young prelate guided Perion through the complexities of unfamiliar halls and stairways into an inhospitable night. There were ready two horses, and presently the men were mounted and away.
Once only Perion shifted in the saddle to glance back at Bellegarde, black and formless against an empty sky; and he dared not look again, for the thought of her that lay awake in the Marshal’s Tower, so near at hand as yet, was like a dagger. With set teeth he followed in the wake of his taciturn companion. The bishop never spoke save to growl out some direction.
Thus they came to Manneville and, skirting the town, came to Fomor Beach, a narrow sandy coast. It was dark in this place and very still save for the encroachment of the tide. Yonder were four little lights, lazily heaving with the water’s motion, to show them where the *Tranchemer* lay at anchor. It did not seem to Perion that anything mattered.

===Chinese===

随后，据佩里翁推想已是三点钟光景，有人叩响了房门。佩里翁走出门外，进入走廊。走廊此刻已无灯火，这位年轻的高级教士引着佩里翁穿过陌生厅堂与楼梯的重重纡曲，踏入一片荒寒无情的夜色，佩里翁只得一路攥着艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔的斗篷。两匹马早已备好，不多时，两人便上马疾驰而去。
佩里翁只在马鞍上回过一次头，望了一眼贝勒加德——它衬着空荡荡的天空，黑沉沉地不成形状；他再不敢回望，因为一想到她还醒着躺在元帅塔（Marshal's Tower）里，此刻犹在近旁，那念头便如一柄匕首。他咬紧牙关，跟在沉默寡言的同伴身后。主教除了偶尔吼出一两句指路的言语，再不开口。
他们就这样来到曼讷维尔，绕镇而过，到了福莫尔海滩——一带狭窄的沙岸。此地一片昏黑，万籁俱寂，唯有潮水在悄然进逼。远处有四点细小的灯火，随着水波懒懒地摇荡起落，指明「特朗什梅尔号」下锚之处。在佩里翁看来，一切都已无所谓了。

===Original===

“It will be nearing dawn by this,” he said.
“Ay,” Ayrart de Montors said, very briefly; and his tone evinced his willingness to dispense with further conversation. Perion of the Forest was an unclean thing which the bishop must touch in his necessity, but could touch with loathing only, as a thirsty man takes a fly out of his drink. Perion conceded it, because nothing would ever matter any more; and so, the horses tethered, they sat upon the sand in utter silence for the space of a half hour.
A bird cried somewhere, just once, and with a start Perion knew the night was not quite so murky as it had been, for he could now see a broken line of white, where the tide crept up and shattered and ebbed. Then in a while a light sank tipsily to the water’s level and presently was bobbing in the darkness, apart from those other lights, and it was growing in size and brilliancy.
Said Perion, “They have sent out the boat.”
“Ay,” the bishop answered, as before.
A sort of madness came upon Perion, and it seemed that he must weep, because everything fell out so very ill in this world.

===Chinese===

「这时该近天亮了。」他说。
「是啊。」艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔应得极简短；那语气显出，他巴不得免掉一切多余的交谈。林中佩里翁是一件不洁之物，主教于万不得已时不得不去触碰，却只能怀着厌恶去触碰，就像口渴的人从自己的饮料里拣出一只苍蝇。这一点佩里翁认了，因为再没有什么事是要紧的了；于是，拴好两匹马，两人便坐在沙地上，足足半个钟头一言不发。
一只鸟在什么地方叫了一声，只叫了一声；佩里翁蓦然察觉，夜色已不似先前那般浓黑，因为他此刻看得见一道断续的白线——潮水悄悄爬上来，迸碎，又退了下去。又过片刻，一点灯火醉汉也似的沉落到水面，随即在黑暗中颠簸浮动，同那几点灯火分了开来，而且渐渐变大、渐渐明亮。
佩里翁说：「他们把小艇放出来了。」
「是啊。」主教如先前一样应道。
一种近乎疯狂的悲绪攫住了佩里翁，他觉得自己非恸哭一场不可，只因这世上万事竟都落得如此糟糕。

===Original===

“Messire de Montors, you have aided me. I would be grateful if you permitted it.”
De Montors spoke at last, saying crisply:
“Gratitude, I take it, forms no part of the bargain. I am the kinsman of Dame Melicent. It makes for my interest and for the honour of our house that the man whose rooms she visits at night be got out of Poictesme﻿—”
Said Perion, “You speak in this fashion of the most lovely lady God has made﻿—of her whom the world adores!”

===Chinese===

「德·蒙托尔爵爷，您援助了我。若蒙您见许，我是感激的。」
德·蒙托尔终于开了口，字字干脆：
「依我看来，感激并不在这桩买卖之内。我是梅莉森特夫人的亲族。她夜里前去造访其房间的那个男子，非得送出波伊捷斯不可——这才于我的利益相宜，也于我们门第的荣誉相宜——」
佩里翁说：「您竟这样谈论上帝造出的最可爱的夫人——谈论这位为全世界所爱慕的女子！」

===Original===

“Adores!” the bishop answered, with a laugh; “and what poor gull am I to adore an attested wanton?” Then, with a sneer, he spoke of Melicent, and in such terms as are not bettered by repetition.
Perion said:
“I am the most unhappy man alive, as surely as you are the most ungenerous. For, look you, in my presence you have spoken infamy of Dame Melicent, though knowing I am in your debt so deeply that I have not the right to resent anything you may elect to say. You have just given me my life; and armoured by the fire-new obligation, you blaspheme an angel, you condescend to buffet a fettered man﻿—”
But with that his sluggish wits had spied an honest way out of the imbroglio.

===Chinese===

「爱慕！」主教笑着答道，「那我又是哪一门可怜的呆鸟，会去爱慕一个查有实据的荡妇？」随后他又冷笑着议论起梅莉森特来，用的那些字眼，转述一遍也增不了半分光彩。
佩里翁说道：
「我是世上最不幸的男子，正如您无疑是最刻薄的一位。因为，您瞧，您当着我的面污蔑了梅莉森特夫人，尽管您明知我欠您的情分深到这般田地，深到您无论拣什么话来说，我都无权怨恨。您方才给了我这条性命；仗着这份簇新的恩惠作铠甲，您亵渎一位天使，您屈尊去殴打一个戴着镣铐的人——」
可话到此处，他那素来迟钝的心智已窥见一条光明正大的出路，足以脱出这团纠葛。

===Original===

Perion said then, “Draw, *messire*! for, as God lives, I may yet repurchase, at this eleventh hour, the privilege of destroying you.”
“Heyday! but here is an odd evincement of gratitude!” de Montors retorted; “and though I am not particularly squeamish, let me tell you, my fine fellow, I do not ordinarily fight with lackeys.”
“Nor are you fit to do so, *messire*. Believe me, there is not a lackey in this realm﻿—no, not a cutpurse, nor any pander﻿—who would not in meeting you upon equal footing degrade himself. For you have slandered that which is most perfect in the world; yet lies, Messire de Montors, have short legs; and I design within the hour to insure the calumny against an echo.”

===Chinese===

佩里翁随即说道：「拔剑吧，爵爷！因为上帝在上，说不定还来得及，让我在这最后关头，赎回将您毁掉的特权。」
「嗬！这可真是谢恩谢得出奇！」德·蒙托尔反唇相讥，「虽说我也并非多么讲究体面的人，可是告诉你，我的漂亮朋友，我平素不同男仆交手。」
「您也不配同他们交手，爵爷。请相信我，这王国之内，随便哪一个男仆——不，随便哪一个扒手，随便哪一个皮条客——与您平等照面，无不会自贬身份。因为您诽谤了世上至为完美的造物；然而，德·蒙托尔爵爷，谎言向来腿短；我打算在一小时之内，为这番诬蔑保个险，叫它再无回声。」

===Original===

“Rogue, I have given you your very life within the hour﻿—”
“The fact is undeniable. Thus I must fling the bounty back to you, so that we sorry scoundrels may meet as equals.” Perion wheeled toward the boat, which was now within the reach of wading. “Who is among you? Gaucelm, Roger, Jean Britauz﻿—” He found the man he sought. “Ahasuerus, the captain that was to have accompanied the Free Companions oversea is of another mind. I cede my leadership to Landry de Bonnay. You will have the kindness to inform him of the unlooked-for change, and to tender your new captain every appropriate regret and the dying felicitations of Perion de la Forêt.”
He bowed toward the landward twilight, where the sand hillocks were taking form.
“Messire de Montors, we may now resume our vigil. When yonder vessel sails there will be no conceivable happening that can keep breath within my body two weeks longer. I shall be quit of every debt to you. You will then fight with a man already dead if you so elect; but otherwise﻿—if you attempt to flee this place, if you decline to cross swords with a lackey, with a convicted thief, with a suspected murderer, I swear upon my mother’s honour! I will demolish you without compunction, as I would any other vermin.”

===Chinese===

「无赖，就在这一个钟头里，我方把你的性命白白给了你——」
「事实无可否认。所以我必须把这份恩赏掷还给您，好叫我们这两个可怜的恶棍以平等之身相会。」佩里翁转身朝向小艇，那艇此刻已到涉水可及之处。「你们中间都有谁？戈塞尔姆（Gaucelm）？罗歇（Roger）？让·布里托（Jean Britauz）——」他找到了他要找的人。「亚哈随鲁，原定随自由佣兵一同渡海的船长改了主意。我把首领之位让与朗德里·德·博内（Landry de Bonnay）。劳驾你把这意外的变故知会于他，并向你们的新首领致以一切合宜的歉意，以及佩里翁·德·拉福雷临终的贺辞。」
他朝陆地那面的熹微处躬身一礼，那里的沙丘正渐渐显出形影。
「德·蒙托尔爵爷，如今我们可以重续守候了。待那边那艘船扬帆而去，这世上便再想不出任何事情，能让我这具躯壳里的呼吸再延续两个星期。到那时，我欠您的每一重债便都清了。您若愿意，便同一个死人交手好了；但若不然——倘若您企图逃离此地，倘若您不屑与一个男仆、与一个定罪的窃贼、与一个涉嫌凶杀之人交剑——我凭我母亲的名誉起誓！我会毫不留情地把您了结，如同了结任何一只虫豸。」

===Original===

“Oh, brave, brave!” sneered the bishop, “to fling away your life, and perhaps mine too, for an idle word﻿—” But at that he fetched a sob. “How foolish of you! and how like you!” he said, and Perion wondered at this prelate’s voice.
“Hey, gentlemen!” cried Ayrart de Montors, “a moment if you please!” He splashed knee-deep into the icy water, wading to the boat, where he snatched the lantern from the Jew’s hands and fetched this light ashore. He held it aloft, so that Perion might see his face, and Perion perceived that, by some wonder-working, the person in man’s attire who held this light aloft was Melicent. It was odd that Perion always remembered afterward most clearly of all the loosened wisp of hair the wind tossed about her forehead.
“Look well upon me, Perion,” said Melicent. “Look well, ruined gentleman! look well, poor hunted vagabond! and note how proud I am. Oh, in all things I am very proud! A little I exult in my high station and in my wealth, and, yes, even in my beauty, for I know that I am beautiful, but it is the chief of all my honours that you love me﻿—and so foolishly!”

===Chinese===

「哦，好胆气，好胆气！」主教讥诮道，「为一句闲话，抛掉你的性命，说不定还搭上我的——」可是话到此处，他喉中迸出一声呜咽。「你多么傻啊！又多么像你！」他说。佩里翁听着这位高级教士的嗓音，好生诧异。
「嘿，诸位！」艾亚尔·德·蒙托尔喊道，「劳驾，且请稍待片刻！」他蹚进没膝的冰冷海水，向小艇走去，从那犹太人手中夺过提灯，把这盏灯带上了岸。他将灯高高举起，好教佩里翁看清他的脸；佩里翁这才认出，凭着某种奇迹，这个一身男装、高擎灯火的人，竟是梅莉森特。说来奇怪，日后佩里翁记得最清楚的，偏偏是那被风吹拂在她额前的、松散的一缕发丝。
「好好看看我，佩里翁，」梅莉森特说，「好好看吧，败落的绅士！好好看吧，可怜的遭追捕的流浪汉！且看我何等骄傲。哦，在一切事上，我都是极其骄傲的！我为我的高位、为我的财富略略自得，是的，甚至为我的美貌自得，因为我知道自己美丽；然而我一切荣誉之首，是你爱着我——爱得这样痴！」

===Original===

“You do not understand﻿—!” cried Perion.
“Rather I understand at last that you are in sober verity a lackey, an impostor, and a thief, even as you said. Ay, a lackey to your honour! an imposter that would endeavour﻿—and, oh, so very vainly!﻿—to impersonate another’s baseness! and a thief that has stolen another person’s punishment! I ask no questions; loving means trusting; but I would like to kill that other person very, very slowly. I ask no questions, but I dare to trust the man I know of, even in defiance of that man’s own voice. I dare protest the man no thief, but in all things a madly honourable gentleman. My poor bruised, puzzled boy,” said Melicent, with an odd mirthful tenderness, “how came you to be blundering about this miry world of ours! Only be very good for my sake and forget the bitterness; what does it matter when there is happiness, too?”
He answered nothing, but it was not because of misery.
“Come, come, will you not even help me into the boat?” said Melicent. 
 She, too, was glad.

===Chinese===

「你并不明白——！」佩里翁喊道。
「不如说，我终于明白了：你千真万确是个男仆、冒名顶替的骗子、窃贼，正如你自己所言。是啊，一个侍奉自己名节的男仆！一个极力想——哦，多么徒劳地想！——冒充他人卑贱身份的骗子！一个偷走别人罪罚的窃贼！我不发一问；爱便意味着信；只是我倒真想慢慢地、慢慢地弄死那另外一个人。我不发一问，但我敢信我所识得的这个男子，哪怕违逆他亲口的言辞。我敢断言这人绝非窃贼，而是事事疯狂地恪守荣誉的绅士。我可怜的、受了创伤又满心迷惘的孩子，」梅莉森特说，带着一种奇异的、含笑的温柔，「你怎么会在我们这个泥泞的世上跌跌撞撞地乱闯！只求你看在我的份上安分些，把那些苦楚都忘了吧；既然也有幸福在，别的又何足道呢？」
他没有作答，却并非因为悲伤。
「来吧，来吧，你难道连扶我上艇也不肯么？」梅莉森特说。
她也是欢喜的。

## II How the Vicomte Was Very Gay / 二 子爵如何尽欢

===Original===

There was dancing afterward and a sumptuous supper. The Vicomte de Puysange was generally accounted that evening the most excellent of company. He mingled affably with the revellers and found a prosperous answer for every jest they broke upon the projected marriage of Dame Melicent and King Theodoret; and meanwhile hugged the reflection that half the realm was hunting Perion de la Forêt in the more customary haunts of rascality. The springs of Perion’s turbulent mirth were that tomorrow every person in the room would discover how impudently every person had been tricked, and that Melicent deliberated even now, and could not but admire, the hunted outlaw’s insolence, however much she loathed its perpetrator; and over this thought in particular Perion laughed like a madman.

“You are very gay tonight, Messire de Puysange,” said the Bishop of Montors.

This remarkable young man, it is necessary to repeat, had reached Bellegarde that evening, coming from Brunbelois. It was he (as you have heard) who had arranged the match with Theodoret. The bishop himself loved his cousin Melicent; but, now that he was in holy orders and possession of her had become impossible, he had cannily resolved to utilise her beauty, as he did everything else, toward his own preferment.

===Chinese===

其后是舞蹈，又有一席盛馔。当晚，皮桑日子爵被众人公认为最出色的席间良伴。他和颜悦色地周旋于寻欢的宾客之间，凡有人拿梅莉森特夫人与泰奥多雷特王的婚事掷来戏言，他总能回敬一句圆满讨喜的答话；与此同时，他心里却暗自窃喜，只想着：半个王国的兵马，正在匪类惯常出没的巢穴里搜捕佩里翁·德·拉福雷。佩里翁那阵狂乱欢愉，源头有二：其一，明日满厅之人个个都将发现自己受了何等无礼的愚弄；其二，梅莉森特此刻正自思量那被追缉的法外之徒何其骄狂——她纵然憎恶这骄狂的始作俑者，却不能不暗暗叹赏。尤其是想到这后一层，佩里翁笑得像个疯子。

「皮桑日爵爷，您今晚真是欢快得很呐。」蒙托尔主教说道。

这位不同凡响的年轻人——有必要再提一句——是当晚才从布兰布卢瓦赶到贝勒加德的。正是他（如诸位所闻）促成了与泰奥多雷特的这门亲事。主教本人爱着自己的表妹梅莉森特；但他既已身领圣秩，占有她已断无可能，便精明地拿定了主意，要把她的美貌——一如其他一切——都用于谋求自己的晋身。

===Original===

“Oh, sir,” replied Perion, “you who are so fine a poet must surely know that *gay* rhymes with *today* as patly as *sorrow* goes with *tomorrow*.”

“Yet your gay laughter, Messire de Puysange, is after all but breath: and *breath* also”﻿—the bishop’s sharp eyes fixed Perion’s﻿—“has a hackneyed rhyme.”

“Indeed, it is the grim rhyme that rounds off and silences all our rhyming,” Perion assented. “I must laugh, then, without rhyme or reason.”

===Chinese===

「哦，先生，」佩里翁答道，「您这般出色的诗人，想必深知 *gay*（欢快）与 *today*（今日）押起韵来何等熨帖，恰似 *sorrow*（哀愁）一路相随 *tomorrow*（明日）。」

「然而您这欢快的笑声，皮桑日爵爷，归根结底不过是一口气息：而 *breath*（气息）」——主教那双锐利的眼睛盯住了佩里翁——「也有一个用滥了的韵脚。」

「的确，正是那个冷峻的韵脚，替我们所有的押韵收束作结，令其一齐喑默，」佩里翁深以为然，「那么我发笑，只好既无韵、又无由了。」

===Original===

Still the young prelate talked rather oddly. “But,” said he, “you have an excellent reason, now that you sup so near to heaven.” And his glance at Melicent did not lack pith.

“No, no, I have quite another reason,” Perion answered; “it is that tomorrow I breakfast in hell.”

“Well, they tell me the landlord of that place is used to cater to each according to his merits,” the bishop, shrugging, returned.

===Chinese===

这位年轻的高级教士谈吐依旧颇为古怪。「不过，」他说，「您如今用晚餐之处离天国这样近，自有绝好的理由。」而他投向梅莉森特的那一眼，也不乏锋锐的意味。

「不，不，我的缘由全然是另一个，」佩里翁答道，「那便是：明日清晨，我要在地狱用早餐。」

「唔，听说那地方的老掌柜，向来是按各人的应得来款待客人的。」主教耸了耸肩，回敬道。

---

===Original===

And Perion thought how true this was when, at the evening’s end, he was alone in his own room. His life was tolerably secure. He trusted Ahasuerus the Jew to see to it that, about dawn, one of the ship’s boats would touch at Fomor Beach near Manneville, according to their old agreement. Aboard the *Tranchemer* the Free Companions awaited their captain; and the savage land they were bound for was a thought beyond the reach of a kingdom’s lamentable curiosity concerning the whereabouts of King Helmas’ treasure. The worthless life of Perion was safe.

For worthless, and far less than worthless, life seemed to Perion as he thought of Melicent and waited for her messenger. He thought of her beauty and purity and illimitable loving-kindness toward every person in the world save only Perion of the Forest. He thought of how clean she was in every thought and deed; of that, above all, he thought, and he knew that he would never see her any more.

“Oh, but past any doubting,” said Perion, “the devil caters to each according to his merits.”

===Chinese===

到晚宴散尽、佩里翁独自回到自己房中之时，他才觉得此言何其真切。他的性命已算得上安稳。他信得过犹太人亚哈随鲁自会料理妥帖：依照旧日的约定，拂晓前后，船上会放下一条小艇，靠上曼讷维尔（Manneville）附近的福莫尔海滩。自由佣兵们正在特朗什梅尔号上等候他们的首领；而他们此去的那片蛮荒之土，比一个王国那份可悲的好奇心——惦着赫尔马斯王的珍宝如今下落何方——所能企及之处，还要更远一步。佩里翁这条一钱不值的性命，已然无虞。

因为当佩里翁思念着梅莉森特、等候她差来的信使时，生命在他看来不惟一钱不值，更远贱于一钱不值。他想着她的美丽与纯洁，想着她对世上每一个人所怀的那份无边慈爱——独独除却林中佩里翁。他想着她的每一个念头、每一桩行事何等洁净无瑕；他想的尤其是这一层，而他知道，自己从此再也见不到她了。

「哦，这是千真万确、无可置疑的，」佩里翁说，「魔鬼款待各人，也是按各人的应得。」

## XVIII How They Cried Quits / 十八 他们如何喊成两讫

===Original===
Demetrios went into Perion’s cell and filed away the chains of Perion of the Forest. Demetrios thrust the gaoler’s corpse under the bed, and washed away all stains before the door of the cell, so that no awkward traces might remain. Demetrios locked the door of an unoccupied apartment and grinned as Old Legion must have done when Judas fell.
More thanks to Bracciolini’s precautions, these two got safely from the confines of San’ Alessandro, and afterward from the city of Megaris. They trudged on a familiar road. Perion would have spoken, but Demetrios growled, “Not now, *messire*.” They came by night to that pass in Sannazaro which Perion had held against a score of men-at-arms.
Demetrios turned. Moonlight illuminated the warriors’ faces and showed the face of Demetrios as sly and leering. It was less the countenance of a proud lord than a carved head on some old waterspout.

===Chinese===
德米特里奥斯走进佩里翁的囚室，锉断了林中佩里翁身上的锁链。他把狱吏的尸首塞到床底，又洗净了囚室门前的一切污迹，免得留下任何棘手的痕迹。随后德米特里奥斯锁上一间空屋的门，咧嘴狞笑——当年犹大坠地身亡时，老魔「群」（Old Legion）露出的想必也正是这样一副笑容。
多亏布拉乔利尼的种种防范，这两人才安然脱出圣亚历山德罗堡的范围，随后又出了梅加里斯城。他们在一条熟识的路上徒步跋涉。佩里翁本待开口，德米特里奥斯却低吼道：「现在不行，*爵爷*。」入夜时分，两人到了萨纳扎罗的那处隘口——佩里翁曾在此独拒二十名武装兵士。
德米特里奥斯转过身来。月光照亮了这两名战士的脸，也照出德米特里奥斯那张阴狡淫邪的面孔。那已算不得一位骄傲领主的尊容，倒像是从某根古老泄水嘴上雕出来的兽首。

===Original===
“Messire de la Forêt,” Demetrios said, “now we cry quits. Here our ways part till one of us has killed the other, as one of us must surely do.”
You saw that Perion was tremulous with fury. “You knave,” he said, “because of your pride you have imperilled your accursed life﻿—your life on which the life of Melicent depends! You must need delay and rescue me, while your spawn inflicted hideous infamies on Melicent! Oh, I had never hated you until tonight!”
Demetrios was pleased.

===Chinese===
「德·拉福雷爵爷，」德米特里奥斯说，「如今你我喊成两讫。在此地你我的路就此分开，直到我们中的一个杀掉另一个——而这，你我之中必有一人要做。」
你看得出，佩里翁怒得浑身发抖。「你这恶棍，」他说，「就为你那点骄傲，你竟拿自己那条该诅咒的性命去冒险——而梅莉森特的性命，正系在你这条命上！你偏要耽搁下来救我，任凭你的孽种对梅莉森特施加骇人的秽行！哦，直到今夜之前，我从没恨过你！」
德米特里奥斯颇为得意。

===Original===
“Behold the increment,” he said, “of the turned cheek and of the contriving of good for him that had despitefully used me! Be satisfied, O young and zealous servitor of Love and Christ. I am alone, unarmed and penniless, among a people whom I have never been at pains even to despise. Presently I shall be taken by this vermin, and afterward I shall be burned alive. Theodoret is quite resolved to make of me a candle which will light his way to heaven.”
“That is true,” said Perion; “and I cannot permit that you be killed by anyone save me, as soon as I can afford to kill you.”
The two men talked together, leagued against entire Christendom. Demetrios had thirty sequins and Perion no money at all. Then Perion showed the ring which Melicent had given him, as a love-token, long ago, when she was young and ignorant of misery. He valued it as he did nothing else.

===Chinese===
「瞧啊，」他说，「这便是把脸转过去由人打、替凌辱过我的人筹谋善事，所生出的利息！你该知足了，哦，爱与基督年轻而热忱的仆役。如今我孑然一身，手无寸铁，囊空如洗，落在一群人中间——对这些人，我素来连费神蔑视都不曾有过。不消多久，我就会落入这帮虫豸手中，随后被活活烧死。泰奥多雷特已拿定主意，要把我做成一支蜡烛，好照亮他升天的路。」
「这话不假，」佩里翁说，「而且我不能容许你死在旁人手里，只许死在我手里——只等我杀得起你的时候。」
这两人促膝共语，结成了与整个基督教世界为敌的同盟。德米特里奥斯有三十枚塞奎因，佩里翁却一文不名。于是佩里翁取出一枚戒指——那是很久以前梅莉森特赠他的定情信物，那时她还年轻，未识愁苦。他珍视此物，胜过世间一切。

===Original===
Perion said:
“Oh, very dear to me is this dear ring which once touched a finger of that dear young Melicent whom you know nothing of! Its gold is my lost youth, the gems of it are the tears she has shed because of me. Kiss it, Messire Demetrios, as I do now for the last time. It is a favour you have earned.”
Then these two went as mendicants﻿—for no one marks a beggar upon the highway﻿—into Narenta, and they sold this ring, in order that Demetrios might be conveyed oversea, and that the life of Melicent might be preserved. They found another vessel which was about to venture into heathendom. Their gold was given to the captain; and, in exchange, the bargain ran, his ship would touch at Assignano, a little after the ensuing dawn, and take Demetrios aboard.
Thus the two lovers of Melicent foreplanned the future, and did not admit into their accounting vagarious Dame Chance.

===Chinese===
佩里翁说：
「哦，这枚可爱的戒指于我是何等珍贵——它曾触过那位可爱的、年轻的梅莉森特的一根手指，而她是什么样的人，你全然不知！它的黄金，是我逝去的青春；它的宝石，是她为我流过的眼泪。吻它吧，德米特里奥斯爵爷，就像我现在这样，最后一次吻它。这份恩赏，是你挣来的。」
随后这两人便扮作乞丐——因为大路上没有人会留意一个乞丐——进了纳伦塔，把这枚戒指卖掉，好让德米特里奥斯得以渡海而去，也让梅莉森特的性命得以保全。他们寻到另一条船，正要冒险驶入异教之域。他们的金子都交给了船长；作为交换，买卖谈定：船将于下一个拂晓过后不久停靠安西尼亚诺（Assignano），把德米特里奥斯接上船。
梅莉森特的这两个情人，就这样预先筹划了未来，却没有把变幻无常的机运夫人（Dame Chance）记进他们的账目。

## XIII How They Praised Melicent / 十三 他们如何赞颂梅莉森特

===Original===

The conqueror and the conquered sat together upon the prow of Perion’s ship. It was a warm, clear night, so brilliant that the stars were invisible. Perion sighed. Demetrios inquired the reason. Perion said:

“It is the memory of a fair and noble lady, Messire Demetrios, that causes me to heave a sigh from my inmost heart.
